  ruling; the ledger rows
  carry "PLAN GAP" in their notes column so the decision is not lost.


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ADDED PARTIAL -- SOURCE LOCATIONS AND MUSIC FUNCTIONS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Seven of the group's nine ledger rows landed. lily-parser-scheme.cc and parse-scm.cc
did NOT -- see "STILL OWED" at the end of this section.

  lily/input.cc, lily/include/input.hh        ->  Origins/Input.cs
  lily/source-file.cc, .../source-file.hh     ->  Origins/SourceFile.cs
  lily/sources.cc, lily/include/sources.hh    ->  Origins/Sources.cs
  lily/diagnostics.cc, .../diagnostics.hh     ->  Origins/IDiagnostics.cs
  lily/music-function.cc, .../music-function.hh -> Music/MusicFunction.cs
  lily/input-scheme.cc                        ->  Bootstrap/OriginPrimitives.cs
  lily/music-function-scheme.cc               ->  Bootstrap/OriginPrimitives.cs

POSITIONS ARE CHARACTER OFFSETS, NOT BYTE POINTERS

  Upstream's Input holds two `char const *` into a UTF-8 buffer and Source_file
  works in the same currency; get_counts explicitly skips UTF-8 continuation
  bytes so that its column count comes out in CHARACTERS.

  The port holds a SourceFile plus two character offsets. Line and column come
  out identical by construction -- counting .NET chars, with a surrogate pair
  counted once, is the same measurement upstream's continuation-byte skip
  performs. The one figure that differs is the WITHIN-LINE offset, a character
  offset here and a byte offset upstream; it is used only to split a line for
  the error caret, and both split at the same place.

  SourceFile.OffsetOfLineColumn is NEW, with no upstream counterpart: upstream's
  parser carries pointers, so it never needs to go the other way. This port's
  lexer reports line and column, and that method is the bridge back.

Input IS A CLASS, NOT A VALUE TYPE

  Upstream copies Input by value everywhere. Here it is a reference type so an
  origin can be stored in a Scheme property as one object, which is what
  MusicObject.SetSpot has always wanted. Spot() therefore copies EXPLICITLY
  where upstream's copy is implicit in return-by-value; SetSpot copies fields
  rather than rebinding, so an origin already handed out does not change under
  its holder.

Diagnostics IS AN INTERFACE

  Upstream reaches it by multiple inheritance (class Music : public
  Smob<Music>, public Diagnostics). C# has none, so IDiagnostics is an interface
  with default implementations -- same routing, same fallbacks, no base-class
  slot spent. Nothing implements it yet; Grob, MusicObject and MusicIterator
  adopt it when their groups touch them.

THE MUSIC-FUNCTION CALL PROTOCOL IS NOT SCHEME'S, AND IS TRANSLATED LITERALLY

  When an optional argument's predicate REJECTS the next actual argument, that
  argument is NOT consumed: the default is substituted, and every FOLLOWING
  optional is unconditionally defaulted too. \default triggers the same skip
  explicitly, and *unspecified* is its Scheme spelling -- which IS consumed.

  Trailing optionals need no *unspecified* stand-in, because the end of the
  argument list is recognisable on its own. That is what makes (key) in Scheme
  mean what \key \default means in LilyPond.

  The loop shape, the do/while over consecutive optionals and the two separate
  trailing passes are load bearing. MusicFunctionTests pins each case; a tidier
  rewrite rebinds arguments silently rather than failing.

  Signature values are CLONED on the way in (a default is shared by every call
  that uses it, and music is mutable), and the RESULT is not (it is freshly
  made and has no other owner). That asymmetry is upstream's.

MusicFunctionSupport IS NEW-IN-FAMILY

  Upstream reaches %location, argument-error and music-function-call-error
  through lily/lily-imports.cc's Scm_module/Variable pairs -- Guile module
  handles this port does not have (lily-imports.cc is a no-port ledger row).
  The replacements look their targets up BY NAME at the moment of use, because
  ly-syntax-constructors.scm is an on-demand module: caching a variable at
  install time captures whatever was bound before it loaded, which is nothing,
  and the failure looks like a music function that silently reports no errors.

FLOWER GAINED Warn.DeprecationWarning

  flower/warn.cc's deprecation_warning was never ported, and it DE-DUPLICATES:
  each distinct message is reported once. ly:deprecation-warning was bound to
  the generic sink, so the port repeated a warning that upstream prints once.
  Now ported, with the set cleared by Warn.ClearMessages -- upstream never
  clears it (a run is one document), but a test run is many documents in one
  process, and a warning suppressed by an EARLIER test is a test that has
  silently stopped checking anything.

STILL OWED BY THE SOURCE-LOCATIONS GROUP

  lily-parser-scheme.cc   16 bindings (the ly:parser-* family, ly:parse-file,
                          ly:parse-string-expression, ly:parse-init,
                          ly:output-file-name-for-input-file-name). These need
                          the Track P parser session wired to the engine.
  parse-scm.cc            the embedded-Scheme reader remainder.
  ly:source-files, ly:note-extra-source-file
                          sources.cc's two bindings. The TYPE is ported, but
                          both need the lexer's list of opened files, which
                          arrives with lily-parser-scheme.cc. Recorded here
                          rather than left silent -- the ledger's `ported` row
                          deliberately does not assert binding completeness,
                          and the standing bindings rule still owes these.

  Consequence: the group's exit criterion (all 62 ly/ init files load to completion,
  pitchnames real, music-function calls run from .ly text) is NOT yet met.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
OUTPUT DEFINITIONS HOLD A REAL MODULE (corrected)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Output_def::scope_ is a Guile module upstream, and this file used to record
  the port's dictionary as an invisible difference: "nothing in the engine
  treats an output definition's scope as a module -- it is only ever read and
  written by name."

  The ly/ init layer disproved that twice over.

    * parser.yy's output_def_head does `parser->lexer_->add_scope (p->scope_)`.
      With a dictionary there was nothing to push, so the port kept output-def
      scopes in a list of its own -- while output_def's closing brace still
      called remove_scope on the MODULE stack. One push on one list, one pop
      from another: a session emptied its scope stack on the first \paper or
      \layout it read, and the next SetIdentifier indexed past the end.

    * Every assignment inside the block therefore landed OUTSIDE the
      definition, silently.

    * declarations-init.ly calls (set-paper-dimension-variables (current-module))
      from inside a \layout block, which can only mean the layout's own module.

  OutputDef.Scope is now a SchemeModule built by Bootstrap/LilyModules.cs
  (ly_make_module's recipe), LookupVariable goes through scm_module_variable
  semantics -- the module AND its imports, then the parent definition -- and
  the copy constructor makes a FRESH module and copies into it, as upstream's
  does, so a cloned \paper cannot write through to its original.

  The old dictionary view is still available as OutputDef.Variables(), which
  reads the module's own bound bindings. It is a snapshot, not a live view.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ly:set-origin! AND THE %location FLUID
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Every syntax constructor in scm/ly-syntax-constructors.scm ends in
  (ly:set-origin! ...). While that entry point was a stub, each constructor
  handed its RESULT back as an inert UnportedValue placeholder and the next
  rule action failed casting it -- one unported binding standing between the
  port and the whole ly/ layer. It is bound now in MusicPrimitives.cs, with
  ly_set_origin_x's three-way argument reading: absent means (*location*), a
  MUSIC origin means that music's own origin property, and #f or '() means do
  nothing at all.

  MusicFunctionSupport.CurrentLocation reads %location from (lily), not from
  the root module. scm/lily.scm defines it there and does NOT export it, so a
  root-module lookup answered nothing and every origin came out #f.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MUSIC FUNCTIONS ARE APPLICABLE
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  music-function.hh declares LY_DECLARE_SMOB_PROC (&Music_function::call, 0, 0, 1),
  so a Music_function sits in operator position like any procedure. That is not
  decoration: property-set, property-unset and their neighbours are built by
  define-syntax-function, so they ARE music functions, and every
  MAKE_SYNTAX (property_set, ...) applies one directly. The first \set in the
  init layer failed with "wrong type to apply" until MusicFunction implemented
  CodeBrix.LilyScheme's IApplicable, which is that library's equivalent of
  Guile's scm_set_smob_apply.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
context-mod-scheme.cc -- PULLED FORWARD OUT OF THE CONTEXTS GROUP
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  All four bindings (ly:get-context-mods, ly:add-context-mod,
  ly:make-context-mod, ly:context-mod-apply!) are in
  Bootstrap/TranslationPrimitives.cs. They are the contexts group's by the
  plan, and the init-layer demand loop pulled them forward because the ly/
  init layer could not finish
  without them.

  This is standing rule 3's textbook case. Context_mod itself had been ported
  and its type predicate registered, so every C# caller worked; what nobody had
  was ly:make-context-mod. context-mod-from-music -- the Scheme half of \with
  and of every context-definition body -- therefore built its result out of an
  unported placeholder, and the parser reported "not a context mod", naming the
  symptom rather than the cause. Eight lines of engraver-init.ly failed that
  way: every \omit, \stemUp, \cadenzaOn, \englishChords and \grobdescriptions
  written inside a \context block.

  ly:output-def-scope was pulled forward the same way and for the same kind of
  reason -- scm/paper.scm's set-default-paper-size and set-global-staff-size
  both need it at declarations-init.ly:156-157. The rest of
  output-def-scheme.cc remains the output pipeline's; the ledger row says so.

===============================================================================
CONTEXTS GO REAL
===============================================================================

  The whole group landed: context-handle.cc, context-scheme.cc,
  deprecated-property.cc, engraver-scheme.cc, global-context-scheme.cc,
  scheme-engraver.cc, translator-ctors.cc, translator-group-ctors.cc,
  translator-dispatch-list.cc and translator-scheme.cc.

  THE STAND-IN IS RETIRED. Context.ContextFactory is gone. Every context is now
  built from a Context_def read out of the Output_def the score is laid out
  under, which is where ly/engraver-init.ly put it, and LilyPortEngraver builds
  Global -> Score -> Staff -> Voice from those definitions with their real
  \consists lists.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE DEFECT THE STAND-IN WAS HIDING: TRANSLATOR LISTENERS WERE ON THE WRONG
DISPATCHER
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Translator::ListenTo registered on Context.EventSource. Upstream's
  Translator_group::connect_to_context takes c->events_below () and registers
  every translator's listeners THERE. The difference is invisible while every
  engraver lives in the context its events are addressed at -- which is exactly
  what the hand-built stand-in tree produced -- and it silently deafens every
  ancestor-context engraver the moment a real Staff sits above a real Voice.

  Fixing it alone was not enough, and the second half is the more interesting
  one. The port had never done upstream's

      events_below ()->register_as_listener (event_source_)

  and had instead compensated by making Context::SendStreamEvent broadcast on
  BOTH dispatchers. That compensation cannot work, because the iterators do not
  go through SendStreamEvent: Music_iterator::report_event broadcasts on the
  event source directly, upstream and here alike. So with listeners moved to
  EventsBelow, note events stopped being heard at all -- the note head vanished
  while the clef and the staff symbol stayed, because those two engravers do
  their work without listening.

  Both halves are now upstream's: the chain is registered in the Context
  constructor, and SendStreamEvent broadcasts once, on the event source.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (contexts)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Context (Symbol, string) -- a name-only constructor building a SYNTHETIC
  Context_def carrying nothing but the name. Upstream has no such constructor
  because upstream always has an output definition to read a real one out of.
  It exists for fixtures that exercise the dispatcher, the property lookup or
  one engraver without standing up the whole init layer, and such a context has
  an empty acceptance set -- so it is a bottom context until the fixture says
  otherwise through Context.Acceptance.

  translator-dispatch-list.cc -- the interface-keyed acknowledger lookup lives
  in SchemeEngraver.Acknowledge rather than in a per-group memoised
  Engraver_dispatch_list. Upstream's list is a CACHE keyed on the grob's name,
  and the port has nothing to key it on: the C# engravers filter inside their
  own AcknowledgeGrob overrides rather than registering per-interface
  acknowledgers. The set of procedures that run, and the order they run in, is
  the same.

  PerformerGroupPlaceholder stands in for Performer_group and Score_performer
  until the MIDI group landed. It is a real, empty Translator_group rather
  than a null return,
  and the difference matters: a null group makes \midi fail at context creation
  with a programming error, whereas an empty one builds the same context tree
  and simply produces no performance. That keeps ly/performer-init.ly loadable.

  Context_handle carries no destructor assertion. Upstream asserts the handle
  was cleared, because garbage collection makes RAII unreliable and it wants a
  leak detected early; C# has no deterministic destructor to assert in and a
  finalizer would fire on the collector's thread. The owner's obligation is
  unchanged.

  GlobalContext registers all five of Context's listeners (CreateContext,
  RemoveContext, ChangeParent, SetProperty, UnsetProperty). Upstream's
  Global_context registers only CreateContext and Prepare, "the most basic
  stuff to bootstrap the context tree". The port's set is a superset, so a \set
  addressed at Global takes effect here and is ignored upstream.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
G4's MEASUREMENT IS COMPUTED
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TranslatorRegistry.RegisterBuiltIn lists the port's C#-side translators and
  runs when the interpreter's registries are built -- the equivalent point to
  upstream's ADD_TRANSLATOR static initialisers, which run before Guile starts.
  ly:register-translator writes into the SAME dictionary, so a \consists
  resolves identically whether the translator is C# or Scheme.

  EngraveResult.MissingTranslators () measures that registry against
  Scheme/translators.tsv rather than against a hand-kept list. An unknown name
  in a \consists produces a warning naming it, which is the demand loop's
  signal: silence there would turn a missing engraver into missing OUTPUT with
  nothing to explain it.

===============================================================================
OUTPUT PIPELINE, FIRST SLICE
===============================================================================

  Landed: music-output.cc, paper-system.cc (+scheme), page-marker.cc
  (+scheme), point-and-click.cc, score-scheme.cc, paper-score-scheme.cc,
  glib-regex-scheme.cc, most of book-scheme.cc and most of
  output-def-scheme.cc.

  STILL OWED BY THE OUTPUT PIPELINE, and the ledger rows say so per file:
  paper-book.cc,
  paper-outputter.cc (+scheme), function-documentation.cc, lily-version.cc,
  the general-scheme.cc remainder, ly:book-process / ly:book-process-to-systems
  (both go through Book::process into Paper_book, which is page layout), and
  the INTERNAL in-process batch runner.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (output pipeline, first slice)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Paper_score::get_paper_systems answers the SINGLE unbroken system. Upstream
  runs calc_breaking and System::break_into_pieces first, so its answer is one
  paper system per LINE. Line breaking is its own group. This is the right answer for
  music that fits on one line and the wrong one for anything longer, which is
  what the regression ratchet will report by file rather than something
  silently plausible.

  System::get_paper_system sets staff-refpoint-extent from the vertical
  alignment's spaceable staves, as upstream does. This entry used to record the
  opposite -- that the property was deliberately left unset, because computing it
  needed Page_layout_problem::is_spaceable, "a page-layout file", and because the
  only alternative to an absent property was a zero interval that would have
  claimed the staves all sit at the origin. Both halves of that had stopped being
  true. is_spaceable landed as PageLayoutSpacing.IsSpaceable, which SystemGrob
  itself already called three times in PureRefpointExtent; and the choice was never
  absent-or-zero, because an Interval that collects no point is EMPTY, which both
  engines spell (+inf . -inf) and which every reader on both sides already guards
  for. Upstream stores empty here and so does the port. Found by the trap-17a
  read/write sweep, which is also how the stale wording came to light.

  scale_output_def calls scm/paper.scm's scale-layout when it is bound and
  falls back to a plain clone when it is not. A clone is the identity at
  scale 1, which is every case the port reaches today.

  LilyPondInit parents the reconstructed PaperDefaults under $defaultlayout's
  root when the layout cannot resolve output-scale. When ly/paper-defaults-init
  .ly's half has arrived this does nothing; when it has not, every dimension
  below would silently come out zero.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ly:regex-exec RETURNS A GOOPS <regex-match>
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Not a bare match object, and this cost a whole file. scm/lily-library.scm
  defines ly:regex-match?, ly:regex-match-positions, ly:regex-match-substring,
  ly:regex-match-prefix and ly:regex-match-suffix in SCHEME, over the
  original-string and substring-positions slots of a GOOPS <regex-match>. Those
  definitions REPLACE the port's own primitives of the same names when
  lily-library.scm loads, so anything else handed back makes every one of them
  slot-ref a non-object.

  ly:regex-replace was wrong in the same area and in two ways: its replacements
  are a REST list whose elements are strings emitted as-is, non-negative
  integers naming capture groups, or PROCEDURES called on the match object. The
  port read the third argument as a single .NET replacement pattern, which made
  a procedure a type error and let `$1' in an ordinary replacement string
  expand where upstream emits it literally.

  Together these are what unblocked hyphenate-internal-words.scm, whose whole
  job is ly:regex-replace with a procedure replacement over $defaultlayout's
  context names.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (output pipeline, finish)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  THE BATCH RUNNER runs D20's short-circuit. BatchRunner (src/CodeBrix.LilyPort)
  defines default-toplevel-book-handler -- the escape hatch ly/init.ly itself
  checks for -- and takes each book's scores STRAIGHT to the SVG backend,
  score by score, instead of through ly:book-process. Page assembly is page
  layout's
  subsystem; when Paper_book lands there, the handler comes out and the real
  book path goes in. Until then:

    * The runner's prologue and epilogue are VERBATIM extracts of the vendored
      ly/init.ly (lines 27-35 and 54-91), parsed around an \include of the
      target file. \maininput itself is session-lifecycle machinery the
      port's caller owns -- the same recorded divergence as LoadInitLayer
      driving declarations-init.ly. The verbose gc-stats block is omitted:
      it prints Guile's collector statistics, which do not exist here.

    * session-replay is NOT carried. One parser session serves the whole
      batch; the prologue's re-defines reset the toplevel collection state
      per file, but an identifier a file defines stays visible to later
      files of the same batch. Upstream resets by replaying the session.
      Accepted until a regression file bites it -- which will be loud and
      per-file in the ratchet, not silent.

    * A score engraves under its own \layout (else $defaultlayout), CLONED and
      parented into the book's \paper so paper variables resolve; the scale
      stays 1 because Paper_book's scaling pass is page layout's. Toplevel
      markups and page markers are SKIPPED with a named diagnostic (text
      interface and page layout own them).

  ly:format renders ~f/~$/~Nf through .NET's "F<precision>" invariant
  formatting where upstream goes through C's %.*lf. The two agree except
  possibly in the last digit of exact-halfway ties. If the glyph-level
  comparator ever reports a last-digit coordinate delta, look here first.

  ly:stderr-redirect's FILE form redirects the port's diagnostic writer
  (Warn.Output) and Console.Error together. The FD form is dup2 on descriptor
  2, which has no in-process equivalent; it throws with its reason rather
  than pretending.

  function-documentation.cc: the TABLE mechanism is real and
  ly:get-all-function-documentation answers it; the docstring CONTENT is
  documentation-generation parity's (the port's bindings declare none yet, so
  the table is honestly
  sparse). The procedure-property half of ly_add_function_documentation is
  not carried -- nothing in the vendored layer reads the property where it
  reads the table. ly_check_name guards C++-to-Scheme name mangling at
  compile time and has no analogue in C#.

  ^^ CORRECTED by documentation-generation parity. BOTH halves of that
  paragraph are now
  overtaken, and the second one was FALSE when it was written:

    * The docstring content landed -- see the function-documentation.cc entry
      near the top of this file.

    * "Nothing in the vendored layer reads the property where it reads the
      table" is wrong. document-functions.scm makes TWO passes: one over
      ly:get-all-function-documentation, and a second over every public
      procedure in (lily) that has a procedure-documentation and is NOT in the
      table. The 63 entry points LilyPond re-exports under a Scheme name --
      (define-public assoc-get ly:assoc-get) and its like -- are reached only by
      the second pass, because the table is keyed ly:assoc-get while the module
      binds assoc-get. The property is now set, with upstream's exact composed
      string (" - LilyPond procedure: " name " " varlist "\n" doc).

    The general lesson, which is trap 8's shape: a divergence note that says
    "nothing reads this" is a claim about the WHOLE consuming layer, and it goes
    stale the moment a file nobody had run yet turns out to read it.

  ly_check_name remains without an analogue, as above.

  ly:spawn, ly:shutdown-gs and ly:gs-api are N/A per D25 (category
  ps-backend, rows in entry-point-na-candidates.tsv): they exist to drive the
  PostScript/Ghostscript pipeline D15 rules out. The bindings exist and THROW
  with their reason -- never a silent #f.

  Paper_outputter writes bytevector results to its port as Latin-1 text --
  the identity on byte values -- because the port's Scheme ports are text
  writers. Only the PS/PNG pipelines ever hand a Paper_outputter bytevectors.

  ly:make-book's rest arguments now walk upstream's add_score PREPEND loop.
  The earlier AppendRest preserved the newest-first order init.ly collects
  in, which silently REVERSED every multi-score file the moment the batch
  runner made multi-score files reachable.

  Type-check failure messages name the value's CLR type in brackets
  ("... [SourceSpan]"). Port-side diagnostic text only, never engraved
  output; it is what told a raw parser span apart from a real Input when
  both printed as file:line:col.

===============================================================================
HORIZONTAL SPACING
===============================================================================

  The whole group landed: spacing-options.cc, rod.cc, spacing-interface.cc,
  note-spacing.cc, staff-spacing.cc, spacing-basic.cc, spacing-spanner.cc,
  spacing-determine-loose-columns.cc, spacing-loose-columns.cc,
  spacing-engraver.cc, note-spacing-engraver.cc and
  separating-line-group-engraver.cc.

  Upstream splits Spacing_spanner across three translation units. The port
  keeps that split as three C# files declaring one `partial class
  SpacingSpanner`, so each file keeps its own `//was previously:` provenance
  line and the shape of the upstream source is still legible.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NAMING
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Spacing_spanner::note_spacing is SpacingSpanner.NoteSpacingSpring. A static
  method called NoteSpacing on that class would shadow the NoteSpacing type
  that sits beside it in the same namespace -- for every line of the class,
  including the ones that legitimately want the type.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE THREE THINGS HORIZONTAL SPACING PULLED FORWARD, AND WHY
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  separation-item.cc was carried as a PARTIAL port: add_item only. Its skyline
  half is not optional for horizontal spacing and was completed here --
  calc_skylines,
  boxes, set_distance, is_empty, conditional_skyline, add_conditional_item.
  Without calc_skylines, every column's horizontal-skylines property answers
  nothing, Paper_column::minimum_distance answers 0 for every pair, and the
  spacing problem is stated with no rods at all: a solution would exist, and
  it would let everything collide.

  ly:item-break-dir (item-scheme.cc, a long-tail file) came forward the moment a
  StaffSpacing grob existed. Staff_spacing::get_spacing reads the space-alist
  off a break-aligned grob, and several space-alist entries are
  break-alignment-list callbacks from scm/output-lib.scm, whose first act is to
  ask an item which side of a break it is on. The rest of item-scheme.cc is
  still the long tail's and the ledger row says so.

  robust_relative_extent (misc.cc) is carried on LooseColumns, its only
  port-side caller so far.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE DEFECT THIS FOUND, AND IT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER: A SKYLINE PAIR IS A CONS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The port had a SkylinePair class and stored it directly as the value of the
  horizontal-skylines grob property. There is no skyline-pair object in Scheme.
  scm/c++.scm defines

      (define-public (ly:skyline-pair? x)
        (and (pair? x) (ly:skyline? (car x)) (ly:skyline? (cdr x))))

  and lily-guile.cc's scm_conversions<Skyline_pair> is scm_cons of the two
  smobbed skylines. So the property is a CONS, and a CLR object in that slot
  fails ly:skyline-pair? -- which is the property's own type predicate --
  and breaks every Scheme reader that expects to car it (skyline.scm,
  paper-system.scm).

  This was invisible until horizontal spacing landed, because nothing had
  ever WRITTEN
  horizontal-skylines: the property was only ever read, and reading an unset
  property answers '() rather than a wrong type. The first write is what
  surfaced it. SkylinePair now carries ToScheme/FromScheme -- including
  upstream's guard that the first skyline must face DOWN/LEFT and the second
  UP/RIGHT, which upstream raises on rather than silently swapping -- and
  every reader goes through them.

  The same class of defect, one layer along: the spacing engraver writes
  shortest-playing-duration and shortest-starter-duration, whose type
  predicate is positive-musical-length-as-number?. A flower Rational is not a
  Scheme number, so those writes were rejected too. They go through
  SchemeConvert.FromRational now. The INEXACT case is the one that bites:
  Rational::infinity reaches Scheme as +inf.0, a real, and
  Paper_column::is_musical reads it back -- so PaperColumn's rational reader
  had to learn doubles, or a column where no note starts would have been
  silently reclassified as non-musical.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE OTHER DEFECT: A LISTENER REGISTERED IN A CONSTRUCTOR TAKES THE TREE DOWN
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Spacing_engraver has ADD_LISTENER (spacing_section). The port's first cut
  called ListenTo from the engraver's constructor. A context's below-dispatcher
  is wired up AFTER its translators are allocated, so that call failed -- and
  because it failed while the Score context was being built, there was no Score
  engraver left to report anything. The symptom was an empty drawing with no
  diagnostic at all.

  Every port engraver with listeners registers them in ConnectToContext and
  drops them in DisconnectFromContext, which is what NoteHeadsEngraver already
  did. Worth knowing because the failure is silent in the worst possible place.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES IN THIS PASS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  [RETIRED by line breaking.] PaperScore.PlaceColumnsOnOneLine was a STAND-IN
  for calc_breaking + System::break_into_pieces, and it is DELETED: the port runs
  the real Paper_score::calc_breaking -> System::break_into_pieces ->
  do_break_substitution_and_fixup_refpoints chain. The entry is kept because its
  last sentence turned out to be the thing worth knowing -- "anything longer is
  visibly wrong rather than silently plausible" was true of the stand-in and NOT
  true of what replaced it: line breaking chose the right lines for a whole
  session while the runner drew only the first of them, which is silently
  plausible in exactly the way the stand-in was not.

  System::pre_processing now reads before-line-breaking and springs-and-rods
  across every element, which is what actually invokes the spacing spanner --
  nothing calls set_springs by name. handle_prebroken_dependencies and
  fixup_refpoint, which sit between those two halves upstream, are still
  absent: both re-point grobs at prebroken pieces, which only matters once
  lines are really broken (the line-breaking group's job).

  Spacing_interface::skylines shifts by a PURE relative Y coordinate upstream.
  The pure/unpure machinery is line breaking's (unpure-pure-container.cc). The port
  uses the real coordinate: the same answer for every grob with no separate
  pure callback -- all of them, today -- but it does compute and cache a Y
  offset earlier than upstream would, which is a thing to re-check when
  vertical alignment starts moving staves.
  [DISCHARGED by the staff-line root-cause work: the shift is the pure
  coordinate now. The re-check predicted here came true at scale -- see the
  staff-line close-out entry near the end of this file.]

  Three named absences, each taking upstream's OWN fallback path rather than
  inventing one:

    * Note_spacing::stem_dir_correction is structurally complete, bar handling
      included. The branch that measures a note column's stem takes upstream's
      undeterminable-stem exit -- a bare return, leaving the space unchanged --
      and says so once, loudly.
      [DISCHARGED by the staff-line root-cause work: the stem loop is ported in
      full, knee_correction included. It had been unreachable for a different
      reason than the note assumed -- Paper_column_engraver never acknowledged
      spacing wishes onto the columns, so NO wish-based correction ever ran.]

    * Staff_spacing::optical_correction answers 0, which is upstream's answer
      whenever the note column has no stem.
      [DISCHARGED by the staff-line root-cause work: ported in full, same
      reachability story as stem_dir_correction above.]

    * Break_alignment_interface::find_nonempty_break_align_group is the
      bars/meter group's.
      Note_spacing then measures to the right side of the non-musical column,
      which is upstream's own no-staff-bar-group fallback; in
      Spacing_spanner::is_loose_column the consequence is different and worth
      knowing -- the do-not-float-a-bar-line guard cannot fire, so a bar-line
      column may be declared loose. The guard ahead of it (a column with real
      width whose two neighbours are in series) is ported and catches the
      ordinary bar line.

  Separation_item::boxes takes the unfiltered branch for conditional elements:
  the filter is Accidental_placement::get_relevant_accidentals (accidentals
  group), and
  nothing carries accidental-placement-interface yet, so the branch is
  currently unreachable rather than wrong.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE line-width COMES FROM, AND THE TWO REASONS IT WAS NOWHERE
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The spacing solver takes a line width. The port had none, and fell back to a
  guess; both halves of the reason are worth recording, because neither was
  visible until something asked for the number.

  FIRST: Output_def::normalize was never ported. output-def.cc's ledger row said
  "ported", which is true of the TYPE -- but normalize is the ONLY thing that
  ever computes line-width. scm/paper.scm's set-paper-dimensions deliberately
  REMOVES line-width from the paper module (a stale one from lilypond-book is
  worse than none), so between paper setup and normalize there is genuinely no
  line width anywhere in the system. It is ported now, asymmetric
  unstated-margin cases and consistency checks included.

  SECOND: the score's layout was parented onto the book's paper only when it
  had no parent at all. Score::book_rendering does it UNCONDITIONALLY --
  scale_output_def, then def->parent_ = layoutbook. The port's $defaultlayout
  arrives already parented to the ORIGINAL $defaultpaper, while ly:make-book
  hands the book a CLONE; so every variable the book's paper computed resolved
  to nothing from the score's side, and normalize could not have helped even
  once it existed. Both are fixed; the scaling half of scale_output_def stays
  at 1 with the rest of the D20 divergence.

  Upstream calls normalize from Book::process. The port calls it from
  BatchRunner.ScoreLayout -- same object, same point in the sequence -- until
  the real book path lands and it can move to where it belongs.

  Measured effect on the 2,146-file sweep: 121 of the 915 files that produce
  SVG went from a zero-width page to a real one, and 108 from every grob at
  x = 0 to real horizontal positions. The comparator's verdict does not move
  yet (897 pages stay at the GLYPHS-DIFFER floor) because it cannot grade
  positions while the glyph inventories differ -- that is the text-interface
  group's upgrade, and it is why the plan slots it immediately after
  horizontal spacing.


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TEXT INTERFACE, GLYPH OUTLINES AND FONT ASSETS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  WHERE THE FONTS LIVE

  lily/all-font-metrics.cc  ->  Fonts/AllFontMetrics.cs, Fonts/FontAssets.cs

  Upstream finds font files through FontConfig, seeded with LilyPond's
  installed data directory. The port has no FontConfig dependency and now
  carries every font INSIDE the assembly as an embedded resource, exactly as it
  already carried the ly/ and scm/ layers. A consuming application may put a
  directory in front of the embedded copies (FontAssets.SearchPaths); nothing
  can make the lookup fail.

  This replaced a directory probe that walked up six levels from the running
  assembly looking for assets/fonts/otf. Six levels reaches the repository root
  from a project under src/, and falls exactly ONE level short from
  tools/regression-harness/BatchDriver. The consequence was not subtle in
  effect and completely silent in appearance: the entire regression sweep ran
  with NO music font at all. 354 files died on ly:font-get-glyph against a null
  font, and the 915 that did produce SVG produced staff lines and stems with
  not one note head among them -- while the only diagnostic was one
  "cannot find font: `emmentaler-20'" warning per file. Embedding removes the
  failure mode rather than lengthening the probe.

  WHERE GLYPH OUTLINES COME FROM

  Fonts/SvgFontOutlines.cs (new-in-family), from scm/output-svg.scm

  Music glyph outlines are lifted VERBATIM out of the shipped .svg font, which
  is what upstream's own SVG backend does (music-string-to-path ->
  cache-font -> extract-glyph). They are NOT rasterised from the OTF's CFF
  charstrings, even though the port can now do that: an independently produced
  outline would be the same SHAPE and a different STRING, and the parity
  yardstick compares strings.

  The units-per-em divisor is hardcoded at 1000, carrying upstream's own
  "TODO: not urgent, but do not hard-code this value" comment. Every shipped
  music font is drawn on a 1000-unit em, so the two agree.

  THE CFF CHARSTRING INTERPRETER

  Fonts/CffFont.cs, Fonts/CharstringRun.cs (new-in-family)

  Written from the Type 2 Charstring Format specification, because a glyph's
  INK extent is recorded nowhere in an OpenType font -- it is a property of the
  outline, so the only way to learn it is to run the program. Text layout needs
  it: how tall a line of text is decides what the engraver puts above and below
  it.

  Validated against an oracle that ships in this repository. Every music font
  is vendored twice, once as an OTF holding glyph PROGRAMS and once as an SVG
  font holding the same glyphs as finished OUTLINES, both produced from one
  source by the font build. CffOutlineTests runs all 600-odd charstrings and
  compares the control hull to the SVG outline's, to within 1.5 units of a
  1000-unit em.

  DIVERGENCE: the bounding box is the CONTROL HULL, not the true ink extremum
  -- a cubic's control points are included rather than the curve's own maxima.
  The hull always CONTAINS the curve, so a text line is never reported shorter
  than it draws, which is the direction that matters when an engraver reserves
  room from it.

  TEXT FONTS

  lily/pango-font.cc  ->  Fonts/TextFontMetric.cs  (ledger: no-port, replaced)
  lily/pango-select.cc ->  Fonts/FontInterface.cs   (ledger: no-port, replaced)

  There is no Pango, no FontConfig and no FreeType. What survives verbatim is
  the CONTRACT, in two places that the rest of the system depends on exactly:

    1. The stencil expression is (utf-8-string DESCRIPTION TEXT INNER), and the
       DESCRIPTION is a Pango-style string -- "serif Bold Italic 8.25". The SVG
       backend recovers the family, weight, slant and size from it by pattern
       matching, using upstream's own regular expression. The style words and
       their order are therefore not free: a different order does not fail, it
       silently lands inside the font family name.

    2. Horizontal extent is the advance sum, from hmtx. VERTICAL extent is the
       INK extent, from the charstrings. Upstream takes exactly this split --
       logical rectangle for X, ink rectangle for Y -- in
       pango_item_string_stencil, and reading Y off the logical rectangle
       instead would make every line of text the same height.

  The INNER expression is left empty. It is the glyph-by-glyph drawing that the
  utf-8-string encapsulation replaces, and it is consumed only by backends that
  do not handle utf-8-string themselves. The port's SVG backend does handle it,
  and SVG is the parity yardstick (D15), so filling it in would be an
  unverifiable guess rather than a fidelity gain.

  Pango's scale_ member is not reproduced. Its constants cancel: an advance
  measured at PANGO_RESOLUTION dots per inch, multiplied by
  INCH_TO_BP / (PANGO_SCALE * PANGO_RESOLUTION * output_scale), leaves
  size / output_scale, and that is what TextFontMetric.Scale computes.

  D23 FALLBACK CHAIN

  Fonts/TextFace.cs

  serif      -> C059-*          -> texgyreschola-*
  sans       -> NimbusSans-*    -> texgyreheros-*
  typewriter -> NimbusMonoPS-*  -> texgyrecursor-*

  and STOPS. Upstream's 00-lilypond-fonts.conf continues into DejaVu and Noto
  CJK, which it does not ship. There is NO system-font fallback: a code point
  no face covers draws missing-glyph tofu by design, and the .notdef glyph
  keeps its own advance so the tofu takes up room instead of collapsing the
  line.

  The face file names are spelled out rather than generated from a template,
  because the three collections do not agree on how to name a face: URW writes
  "Regular" and "BoldItalic", C059 writes "Roman" and "BdIta", and TeX Gyre
  writes everything in lower case. A template silently produces a name that
  does not exist, and a missing face does not fail -- it drops out of the chain
  and leaves text measured by the FALLBACK font.

  THE BACKEND OPTION

  Bootstrap/LilyPondScheme.cs

  `backend` is set to 'svg and `music-strings-to-paths` to #t once the scm/
  layer has loaded. Upstream's option defaults to 'ps because upstream ships
  all three backends; the port has exactly one (D15). This is not cosmetic:
  ly/paper-defaults-init.ly branches on the same option to choose text font
  family names, and under 'ps it picks the FontConfig aliases "LilyPond Serif",
  "LilyPond Sans Serif" and "LilyPond Monospace" where the SVG backend wants
  the generic CSS families "serif", "sans" and "monospace". Those names are
  written verbatim into every text element.

  ENGRAVING IN OUTPUT UNITS  (pulled forward; upstream lily/paper-def.cc)

  Layout/OutputDef.cs (ScaledClone), CodeBrix.LilyPort/BatchRunner.cs

  Upstream scales BOTH the book's paper (Paper_book's constructor) and each
  score's layout (Score::book_rendering) by the paper's output-scale before a
  single grob is made, so engraving happens in output units -- one unit is one
  staff space -- and a coordinate reaching a stencil needs no further
  conversion. The port did neither, and the D20 note recorded the scale as
  staying at 1.

  Skipping it does not fail. Everything is laid out in millimetres instead and
  the whole page comes out scaled by about 1.757 with no diagnostic anywhere,
  which is why it survived the earlier groups. It is fixed here because this
  group's
  purpose is to make the comparator able to grade POSITIONS, and no position
  could ever have matched.

  output-scale and staff-height are deliberately NOT in the paper's
  dimension-variables list: the first has to survive scaling so the framework
  can convert back to millimetres for the page, and the second is read in
  POINTS to size fonts.

  ONE PROCESS PER INPUT FILE  (harness; no upstream analogue)

  CodeBrix.LilyPort/LilyPondInit.cs (RestoreDefaults)

  Upstream engraves one input file per process, so the defaults a file mutates
  die with it. The batch runner shares one session across 2,146 files, and the
  mutations are not hypothetical: a toplevel \paper block rewrites the shared
  definition IN PLACE, and #(set-global-staff-size 30) does something else
  again -- it CLONES the paper, scales the clone, and rebinds the toplevel
  $defaultpaper identifier to it, deliberately leaving the original object
  untouched ("maybe someone still refers to the old one").

  Both halves have to be undone, and the second is the one that bites:
  restoring only the object's variables fixes nothing, because the next book
  still picks up the clone by name. Measured: before this, the two
  absolute-dimensions-* files at the head of the suite rescaled every file
  swept after them -- staff line thickness read 0.0781 where the oracle says
  0.1000 -- and it looked like a port-wide unit error rather than leakage.

  LIMIT: this restores the value of every variable the definitions had after
  initialisation, which covers the numeric leaks. A variable a file INVENTS is
  left in place, because the scope has no unbind.

  SKYLINES FROM STENCILS

  lily/stencil-integral.cc  ->  Layout/StencilIntegral.cs

  All ten callbacks land. Eight are exact: the four from-extents ones use the
  grob's own bounding box, and the four from-element-stencils ones merge the
  children's skylines (Shift along the horizon axis, Raise along the other --
  upstream's comment records that getting those two the wrong way round cost
  six months).

  DIVERGENCE, and the largest one in this pass: the two from-stencil callbacks
  take the stencil's EXTENT BOX rather than walking the stencil expression.
  Upstream turns each drawing command into segments and flattens every Bezier,
  so a slur's skyline follows the curve; this port produces its rectangular
  hull. That is upstream's OWN fallback -- the branch it takes for expressions
  it cannot decompose, such as embedded PostScript.

  The consequence is bounded and one-directional: a box always CONTAINS the
  outline, so nothing is ever placed too close, and the cost is extra space
  around non-rectangular grobs rather than a collision. The expression walk
  plus Lazy_skyline_pair is the one text-interface work item left owed, and it
  wants to land with or before beams, ties and slurs, whose whole point is
  outlines that are not boxes.

  CLOSED later the same pass -- the walk landed. See THE STENCIL
  EXPRESSION WALK at the end of this file; the only case that still takes the
  box is a stencil containing TEXT, and that one is recorded there.

  PURE VARIANTS

  The pure-* skyline callbacks answer the ordinary extents. The port has no
  pure-property machinery yet -- that is unpure-pure-container.cc, line
  breaking's file -- and
  answering the ordinary extent is what a grob declaring no pure callback would
  give anyway.


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ADDED WITH THE TEXT-INTERFACE FOLLOW-UP -- THE STENCIL EXPRESSION WALK
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily/include/lazy-skyline-pair.hh  ->  Layout/LazySkylinePair.cs
  lily/stencil-integral.cc           ->  Layout/StencilIntegral.cs  (the walk)
  lily/freetype.cc Path_interpreter  ->  Fonts/CffFont.cs  (OutlineWalker)

  The item the text-interface group left owed. The two from-stencil callbacks
  now WALK the stencil
  expression instead of taking its bounding box: every draw-line, dashed-line,
  circle, ellipse, partial-ellipse, round-filled-box, polygon, path and
  named-glyph is turned into segments, curves are flattened at one segment per
  0.2 output units, and the structural heads (combine, translate, scale, rotate,
  grob-cause, color, output-attributes, with-outline) carry the transform down.

  WHAT IS FAITHFUL, DELIBERATELY, EVEN WHERE IT LOOKS WRONG

  * A curve's LAST piece is added as a plain segment, not as a contour segment,
    so it lands in both skylines while its earlier pieces land in one. That
    asymmetry is upstream's, in Path_interpreter::curve3to. Kept: the
    faithfulness rule says a plausible tidy-up is a parity bug.
  * dashed-line is measured as one solid run from the origin to (dx, dy). The
    gaps are ignored, which over-reserves; upstream does the same.
  * An unknown head contributes nothing and raises NO warning. Upstream's
    reason is in a comment: stencil-expression.cc's registry is what validates
    heads, and a complaint here would be a second, worse copy of that check.
  * A malformed path warns and keeps the commands it had already grouped,
    rather than throwing.

  DIVERGENCE 1 -- the glyph outline comes from the charstrings, not FreeType

  Upstream traces a named glyph with FT_Outline_Decompose and recovers the
  design-units-to-output-units factor by DIVIDING two boxes: FreeType's glyph
  metrics and FreeType's outline bounding box, which round differently. The
  port has one source for both -- the Type 2 charstring interpreter, which
  the text-interface group built and validated against the shipped SVG fonts
  -- so that ratio is
  the scale factor itself: the font's drawing size over its units per em. It is
  the same number SvgBackend scales the same outline by, and the traced result
  agrees with the LILC-declared extents of noteheads.s2 to within 0.002 staff
  space (StencilIntegralTests).

  A charstring leaves the contour-closing segment implicit where FreeType's
  decomposer emits it, so OutlineWalker emits it itself when a contour ends.

  Contours are traced COUNTER-CLOCKWISE, the PostScript/CFF convention. A
  TrueType face would need the other one; getting it backwards produces a
  plausible skyline that is inside out, with no error.

  DIVERGENCE 2 -- TEXT STENCILS still take the extent box

  Upstream's utf-8-string expression carries, as its fourth element, the
  glyph-string stencil Pango shaped -- and the walk recurses into it. The port
  has no Pango: TextFontMetric.TextStencil measures the run through the D23
  font chain and leaves that fourth element EMPTY, so there is nothing to
  recurse into.

  Walking such a stencil anyway would be the dangerous answer. A stencil that
  COMBINES text with drawing would yield a skyline covering the drawing alone,
  silently omitting the text -- and a skyline that omits ink is the one error
  direction that causes collisions. So SkylinesFromStencil checks for a text
  node anywhere in the expression and falls back to the whole stencil's extent
  box when it finds one. That is exactly the pre-walk behaviour, preserved for
  this one case: conservative, never a collision.

  glyph-string IS handled, as of PARITY 11 -- and the note that used to stand
  here ("NOT handled, because the port never emits it") is the exact shape of
  trap 18, a named absence that stopped being true and that nothing re-checked.
  See THE MUSIC-FONT RUN IS A glyph-string (D36) below.

  WHEN TO CLOSE IT: text does not reach a swept page yet -- in-score text needs
  Text_engraver (scripts/dynamics group) and the tagline needs page layout.
  The scripts group is the
  session where per-glyph text outlines become measurable and worth writing;
  the machinery to do it is already here (TextFontChain plus CffFont's
  AddOutlineToSkyline, which is face-agnostic).

  PURE VARIANTS are unchanged: they still answer the ordinary extents, pending
  unpure-pure-container.cc with line breaking.


===============================================================================
COLUMNS, RESTS, DOTS, COLLISIONS
===============================================================================

  The whole group landed: note-column.cc (+scheme), note-collision.cc, rest.cc,
  rest-engraver.cc, rest-collision.cc (+engraver), rhythmic-head.cc,
  rhythmic-column-engraver.cc, collision-engraver.cc, dot-column.cc (+engraver),
  dot-configuration.cc, dot-formatting-problem.cc, dots-engraver.cc,
  completion-note-heads-engraver.cc, completion-rest-engraver.cc and
  multi-measure-rest.cc (+engraver).

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE SEAM FILE, AND WHY IT EXISTED
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  An early seam file (since deleted -- see SEAM RETIREMENT below) carried
  line-for-line ports of the SMALL statics this group's files call but whose
  OWNING upstream files belong to groups running in parallel with, or after,
  this one:

    stem.cc (stems group)             head_count, duration_log, is_normal_stem,
                                      is_invisible, extremal_heads, first_head,
                                      last_head, get_reference_head, flag,
                                      is_valid_stem, thickness, support_head,
                                      internal_calc_stem_offset_from_head,
                                      head_positions, note_head_positions
    directional-element-interface.cc  get_grob_direction,
      (vertical organization)         get_strict_grob_direction,
                                      set_grob_direction
    side-position-interface.cc        add_support
      (vertical organization)
    tie.cc / tie-column.cc (ties)     set_head, head / add_tie
    script-engraver.cc (scripts)      make_script_from_event
    grob-closure.cc (line breaking)   chain_offset_callback (via the vendored
                                      grob::compose-function, exactly as
                                      upstream's Lily:: import)
    staff-symbol-referencer.cc        pure_get_position, pure_get_rounded_position,
      (row says ported; these         set_position, pure_set_position, staff_span,
      statics were never carried)     pure_position_less
    context.cc (same)                 measure_length, measure_position
    spanner.cc (same)                 add_bound_item
    include/misc.hh                   intlog2

  The class is INTERNAL, and nothing in it is new logic. When the owning group's
  port lands the integrator should re-point the callers at the real class and
  retire the copy; until then two copies of these functions must not be allowed
  to drift, which is why each carries the upstream name in its doc comment.

  The pure_* members answer the ORDINARY values -- the same standing divergence
  every pure variant takes until unpure-pure-container.cc (line breaking)
  lands.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NAMING (columns and rests)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Note_column::dot_column is NoteColumn.GetDotColumn. A static method named
  DotColumn on that class would shadow the DotColumn type beside it in the same
  namespace -- the same collision SpacingSpanner.NoteSpacingSpring records.

  Note_collision_interface is NoteCollisionInterface, unabridged, and class Rest
  keeps its upstream name: neither collides with a namespace or a root .NET
  namespace.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (columns and rests)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  * Rest_collision_engraver's busy-column set and Dot_column's stem set are
    std::unordered_set upstream -- unspecified iteration order. The port keeps
    FIRST-OCCURRENCE order, which is deterministic; for the dot column the
    skyline construction is order-independent, so the outcome is the same.
    Same device as GrobArray.remove_duplicates.

  * Every std::sort over grobs (clash groups by shift_less, colliding rests by
    rest_shift_less, dots by pure_position_less) is a stable insertion sort in
    the port: upstream's tie order is unspecified, a stable order is
    reproducible, and the comparators are strict weak orderings either way.

  * Note_column::dir casts a Slice centre to Direction. C++ keeps out-of-range
    enum values (a centre of 4 IS 4 upstream, and multiplies as 4 where the
    value is used as a factor); the port's Direction normalizes to sign on
    construction, which is what every reachable USE of the value means.
    Flower's Direction already records the normalization; noted here because
    rest-collision multiplies by a Direction that can take this path.

  * Rest_engraver drops upstream's dead `Item *dot_` member (only ever reset,
    never read -- carrying it is a CS0414 warning). Rhythmic_head's
    after_line_breaking and Dot_column's compare / side_position are DECLARED
    upstream and defined nowhere; the declarations are dead and not carried.

  * Completion engravers do not carry Stream_event::unprotect() -- GC
    protection has no analogue here. The clone/mutate structure is otherwise
    upstream's, including mutating the ORIGINAL event when the split duration
    equals the original.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PULLED-FORWARD ENTRY POINTS, AND THE Y-OFFSET ODDITY THAT FORCED ONE
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  ly:grob::x-parent-positioning and ly:grob::y-parent-positioning (grob.cc) had
  stubs only -- grob.cc's ledger row says ported, but nothing had ever
  registered them. Dot_column::add_head and Note_collision_interface::add_column
  STORE them as offset callbacks, so this group implements them in
  ColumnAndRestCallbacks.
  Note the upstream oddity, ported as-is: add_head stores the X proc under
  Y-OFFSET ("adding a dot overwrites Y-offset"), so reading a dot's Y offset
  triggers its X parent's positioning.

  ly:unpure-call and ly:pure-call (unpure-pure-container.cc, line breaking)
  are what
  the vendored grob::compose-function calls, and Rest_collision::add_column
  chains its force-shift callback through exactly that composer. With the stubs
  in place the chain would have run -- and silently produced UnportedValue
  offsets. Both are implemented faithfully (including pure-call's fall-through
  applying (data grob . rest) WITHOUT the start/end columns). Line breaking
  inherits
  them working.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT WAITS ON PARALLEL AND LATER GROUPS (columns and rests)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  * busyGrobs is written by Grob_pq_engraver (beams group). Until it lands,
    Rest_collision_engraver reads an empty queue and makes no RestCollision --
    upstream's own path for an empty queue, pinned by a test rather than
    stubbed. The rest-collision CHAIN itself is live and tested end to end.

  * The completion engravers read measureLength/measurePosition/timing exactly
    as upstream; without the bars/meter group's Timing_translator those
    answer unset and no
    split happens. The reachability test pins the split by setting the Timing
    properties by hand.

  * Multi_measure_rest_engraver finalizes on measureStartNow, which nothing
    sets until bars/meter lands: in a pre-integration sweep the spanner keeps
    measure-count
    unset (symbol_stencil answers empty), calculate_spacing_rods reports
    "Multi measure rest seems misplaced" for the missing right bound, and the
    unset MultiMeasureRestNumber text warns as a non-markup. All three resolve
    with the bars/meter group at integration; re-check the smoke files then.

  * MultiMeasureRestNumber/Text/Script read vertical organization's
    side-position and
    self-alignment callbacks, still stubs -- their X/Y-offset type-check
    warnings in the sweep are vertical organization's to silence.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEMS, FLAGS AND STEM TREMOLOS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily/stem.cc                 ->  Objects/Stem.cs  (with stem.hh's Stem_info as
  lily/include/stem.hh             StemInfo, kept beside the Stem class)
  lily/include/stem-info.hh
  lily/flag.cc                 ->  Objects/Flag.cs
  lily/stem-tremolo.cc         ->  Objects/StemTremolo.cs
  lily/stem-engraver.cc        ->  Translation/StemEngraver.cs
  Bootstrap/StemAndFlagCallbacks.cs       (new-in-family: the 33-callback installer)

  Whole notes and rests still get a stem -- an INVISIBLE one (Stem::is_invisible),
  ported faithfully because downstream spacing measures against its presence.
  The eager-flag dance is upstream's too: a Flag is made for every flagged
  duration at acknowledge time and killed again (kill_unused_flags) at the
  timestep's end if a beam claimed the stem, because whether one will is not
  knowable when the head is acknowledged.

NAMING

  Stem::flag is Stem.FlagGrob. A method called Flag on that class would shadow
  the Flag type that sits beside it in the same namespace -- the same collision
  horizontal spacing recorded for SpacingSpanner.NoteSpacingSpring. Stem_info
  is StemInfo.

THE BEAM SEAM, AND WHAT WAS PULLED FORWARD

  stem.cc's beam-aware branches are ported faithfully, not stubbed: the beams
  group
  inherits them working. The small beam.cc statics they call --
  get_beam_thickness, get_beam_translation, get_beam_count,
  get_direction_beam_count, is_cross_staff, first/last_normal_stem -- are
  carried as an INTERNAL BeamHelpers class inside Stem.cs, ported verbatim from
  beam.cc. Every one is a pure read over a live Beam grob, so none is reachable
  until a Beam_engraver exists to make one. When the beams group ports
  beam.cc these move
  to the canonical Beam class and BeamHelpers goes away; the ledger row on
  stem.cc says so, so the duplication cannot drift silently.

  Note_collision_interface::note_head_positions (note-collision.cc, the
  columns group's
  file) is carried the same way, privately in StemTremolo.cs: the whole-note
  tremolo direction decision needs it, and every read in it is a generic grob
  read over "elements" and "stem". The columns group adopts it when
  note-collision.cc lands.

DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (stems and flags)

  * PURE reads take the established fallbacks, pending line breaking's
    unpure-pure-container machinery. Stem.GetPureProperty is a minimal
    get_pure_property: an unpure-pure container's PURE half is called with
    (grob, 0, INT_MAX), a cached value answers directly, and a plain procedure
    answers '() rather than going through call_pure_function (which does not
    exist yet). pure_y_extent is the ordinary extent -- the same answer a grob
    with no pure callback gives, and the same fallback the text-interface
    skyline
    callbacks take.

    RESOLVED at PARITY 12 (2026-08-15): pure_relative_y_coordinate in the
    beamed half of internal_pure_height had ALSO been taking the real
    coordinate, on the recorded ground that it is the same answer a grob with
    no pure callback gives. The ANSWER was never what differed. Reading an
    ORDINARY Y offset forces Y-parent-positioning, which forces the
    VerticalAlignment's positioning-done, which positions every staff in the
    system -- from inside a pure callback, before line breaking. The staves
    were then placed by the before-line-breaking stand-in below and translated
    AGAIN by the real page layout, which uses translate_axis and so ADDS, so
    every cross-staff score came out with its staves exactly one
    staff-staff-spacing too far apart (21 comparator rows, the whole 12.8000
    cluster among them). The call now reads
    PureRelativeYCoordinate(common, 0, int.MaxValue), as upstream does. The
    cost of an unpure read in a pure path can be a SIDE EFFECT rather than a
    value -- trap 24 does not only mean "a different number".
    Fenced by CrossStaffSpacingEndToEndTests.

  * Stem::cache_pure_height STORES the clipped interval now, in the Item
    pure-height cache that Item::pure_y_extent reads (PARITY 25). It used to
    compute the interval faithfully and then DROP it, on a recorded reasoning
    that the Item-side cache "only matters for beams" -- it is
    Item::pure_y_extent's cache and it matters for every Item. See CACHED PURE
    HEIGHT below.

  * D31 AS AMENDED (Jeremy, 2026-08-17) -- AN UNMAPPABLE CODE POINT IN THE MUSIC
    FONT ADVANCES BY TWO SPACES AND DRAWS NOTHING.
    Objects/TextInterface.cs, MusicFontTextStencil.

    THE TWO FONT CLASSES GET DIFFERENT ANSWERS ON PURPOSE, and the reason is the
    ruling:

      TEXT FONTS -- tofu, unchanged. If a document asks for Hebrew or CJK and no
      bundled face covers it, the port DRAWS .notdef so the gap is obvious. That
      is not a fallback, it is a SIGNAL: we want the user to say "you don't have
      proper support for Hebrew", because that complaint tells us somebody cares
      whether we support it. The pinned oracle drops the run instead, and the
      resulting divergence is on g1-skip-list.tsv where D31 put it.

      THE MUSIC FONT -- faithfulness, because there is nothing to signal. Upstream
      ADVANCES and DRAWS NOTHING: Pango marks the glyph unknown,
      Pango_font::get_glyph_desc returns #f so it never reaches the glyph list,
      but the stencil's extent is `string_extent' taken over the WHOLE glyph
      string, so the box survives. The port owes the advance and nothing else.

    //was previously: the glyph was DROPPED, contributing no advance at all, so
    every glyph after it moved left by a whole character.  Emmentaler has no `i'
    glyph and no cmap entry for U+0069, which is why
    \compound-meter #'((+inf.0)) came out 1.7071 narrow.

    WHY TWO SPACES, MEASURED AND NOT CHOSEN.  Pango's unknown-glyph box is a
    per-font, per-size CONSTANT -- `i', `q' and `@' give byte-identical boxes --
    and the port cannot compute it: its width is `approximate_char_width', which
    Pango derives by laying out a SAMPLE STRING in a fallback font the port does
    not have and (having decided to move away from Pango) does not want.  So the
    port approximates it with the font's own space, U+0020, which Emmentaler has
    (advance 192/1000 em) and which draws nothing.  Measured against the pinned
    oracle at seven sizes:

        magstep    1/4     1/2      1       2       4       8      16
        box/space  2.00    2.27    2.27    2.20    2.21    2.21    2.21

    TWO is the nearest whole number at every size and is EXACT at magstep 1/4.

    /!\ IT DOES NOT CLOSE THE CORPUS ROW AND IS NOT MEANT TO, AND THE RESIDUAL IS
    ON BOTH AXES.  Measured on markup-compound-meter-denominator-style:

      X  the settled dx went from -1.7071/-1.7072 to -0.2048/-0.2049, which is
         the width the two spaces do not cover.  No mix of Emmentaler's spaces
         does better than about half a device dot, because each advance is
         rounded to one: the finest are U+2009 (66) and U+200A (22), and
         2 spaces + 1 thin overshoots to +0.068.

      Y  -0.2993/-0.2992 -> +0.0341, by RESERVING A HEIGHT for the stand-in, and
         the reason it is needed is worth knowing.  "Advances and draws nothing"
         is true of what upstream DRAWS but not of the box it KEEPS: Pango's
         unknown-glyph box has an INK RECTANGLE as well as a logical width, and
         LilyPond takes a text stencil's Y extent from the ink rect while taking X
         from the logical one (pango_item_string_stencil, trap 15a).  So a line
         containing an unmappable character is as TALL upstream as any other, and
         left inkless it measures short and everything stacked against it creeps.
         Emmentaler's space has no ink -- which is the very property that makes it
         draw nothing -- so the height cannot come from more spaces and is united
         in explicitly.

         THE HEIGHT IS THE FONT'S OWN: OS/2's TYPOGRAPHIC ascender and descender,
         read by SfntReader.ReadTypoAscenderDescender and reaching the call site
         through FontMetric.TypoAscenderDescender.  For Emmentaler that is
         (800, -200) -- exactly one em -- against the oracle's box measured at
         (-0.1909 .. +0.8141) em.
         /!\ NOT hhea's ascender/descender and NOT usWinAscent/usWinDescent: for
         Emmentaler those are 2127/-2314, FOUR AND A HALF EM, because a music
         font's glyphs reach far above and below the staff.  Taking them would be
         a plausible-looking mistake and the fence controls for it.
         /!\ AND THE FONT'S OWN METRIC BEATS THE ORACLE'S OWN NUMBER: reserving
         the measured (-0.1909 .. +0.8141) instead gives 0.0441 where OS/2 gives
         0.0341.  The residue is ONE DEVICE DOT -- a quantisation floor, not a
         sizing error -- so there is nothing to gain by chasing a more exact box,
         which is why the source is the font and not a recorded constant
         (rule 33).
         /!\ GSUB can merge or split glyphs, and a recorded POSITION would then
         name the wrong one; the stand-in positions are DISCARDED if substitution
         changes the run's length.  Emmentaler substitutes nothing involving a
         space, so this never fires in the corpus -- it is checked rather than
         assumed.

    D29 has no position tolerance, so markup-compound-meter-denominator-style is
    on g1-skip-list.tsv for both residuals.

    /!\ THE WARNING IS UNCHANGED.  MissingGlyphWarning still fires exactly once
    per unmappable code point, with upstream's wording; the diagnostics gate is at
    2146/2146 and did not move.

    /!\ THE STAND-IN PARTICIPATES IN KERNING like any other glyph, which is what
    upstream's buffer does too -- and it is why the fence compares "0i0" against
    "0  0" rather than against "00": removing the middle character changes the
    kern pairs, and the fence's first form was wrong by exactly one 0-0 kern.

    Fenced by UnmappableCodePointTests -- ELEVEN cases.  The advance half: one
    space and three spaces are both wrong, a MAPPABLE code point is untouched, the
    space glyph has no outline where `0' does.  The height half: OS/2 gives
    (800, -200) and they span exactly one em, the reserved interval IS that pair
    scaled, TWO REAL SPACES reserve nothing (which is what makes it a stand-in
    rule and not a space rule), a mappable glyph keeps its own ink, and the height
    is one em rather than the font's four-and-a-half-em glyph RANGE.  Rule 35's
    A/B run on both halves: two cases go red with the stand-in removed, three with
    the height reservation removed.
    /!\ The height fence went red once on its own arithmetic: `FontScaling /
    MusicEm' is stencil units per DESIGN unit and `FontScaling' is one EM, a
    factor of a thousand apart, and calling both `em' is how it happened.

  * CACHED PURE HEIGHT -- Item::pure_y_extent (lily/item.cc:242-251), ported at
    PARITY 25 and previously ABSENT. Upstream OVERRIDES Grob::pure_y_extent on
    Item and stores the answer in cached_pure_height_, invalidated only in the
    two constructors. The port carried both fields, cleared them in both
    constructors, and never read either: the write half of the handshake with
    no reader (trap 17a), so every pure enquiry recomputed.

    THE CACHE IS PART OF THE SPECIFICATION, NOT AN OPTIMISATION (trap 1c). The
    FIRST reader freezes the value, so a property the extent depends on that is
    rewritten LATER cannot change what an earlier reader already saw.

    WHAT IT WAS LOAD-BEARING FOR: Note_collision's `fa' merge
    (note-collision.cc:237-253) rewrites BOTH heads' stem-attachment while
    resolving positioning-done, which Separation_item::boxes forces through an
    ordinary X extent. Horizontal spacing reads the stem's PURE height in
    Note_spacing::stem_dir_correction and must go on seeing the value from
    before that rewrite. Without the cache the port's spacing FOLLOWED
    fa-merge-direction and the oracle's does not -- measured directly: the
    oracle places the bar line identically for #UP, #DOWN and the default,
    the port placed it 0.0241 apart. collision-head-solfa-fa placed every mark
    after the first bar line 0.0241 out because of it.

    Upstream's own comment on the range is reproduced rather than corrected:
    "cached_pure_height_ does not notice if start changes, implicitly assuming
    that Items' pure_heights do not depend on 'start' or 'end'."

    Fenced by ItemPureHeightCacheTests -- five cases, three of them controls:
    an item read for the first time AFTER the change follows it, a Spanner
    (which upstream does not override) follows it, and the cache is per item
    rather than shared.

  * Stem::calc_stem_info guards its knee/shorten reads with a null-beam check
    where upstream would dereference a null Beam. Unreachable in practice --
    stem-info is only read under a beam -- and the guard turns a would-be
    segfault into upstream's own 0 default.

  * Warnings attach to the event's Input origin when it has one and fall back
    to plain warnings, matching how the port's other engravers report; message
    texts are upstream's verbatim ("tremolo duration is too long", "adding
    note head to incompatible stem (type = %d/%d)", "weird stem size, check
    for narrow beams", "Whole-note tremolo may collide with simultaneous
    notes.", "stem tremolo has no note heads", "flag `%s' not found").

  * Flag::glyph_name keeps upstream's dead `adjust' branch (a local bool that
    is always true) as a plain local, so the mensural staffline logic keeps
    its upstream shape without tripping the unreachable-code warning.

  NOT PORTED: Stem::set_spacing_hints, declared in stem.hh and defined nowhere
  upstream -- a dead declaration.

  Align_interface::axis is AlignInterface.GetAxis: a static method named Axis

  Bracket_nesting_group::symbol_ is TypeSymbol, for the same reason one file
  over -- a member named Symbol shadows CodeBrix.LilyScheme.Values.Symbol.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT LIVES WHERE, AND WHY THE SPLITS EXIST (vertical organization)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Standing Wave A rules close every already-ported file, so the pieces of
  OTHER files this group needed live beside their callers here, each with its
  own `was previously:` provenance:

  * Objects/AxisGroupInterfaceVertical.cs -- the half of axis-group-interface
    .cc that Objects/AxisGroupInterface.cs (ported earlier) does not carry:
    skyline_spacing and the outside-staff placement machinery,
    combine_skylines, generic_bound_extent /
    relative_maybe_bound_group_extent, calc_common, and
    calc_maybe_pure_staff_staff_spacing. The pure-height family
    (adjacent_pure_heights, pure_height, calc_pure_relevant_grobs,
    calc_pure_y_common, calc_pure_staff_staff_spacing) is NOT ported and its
    Scheme names deliberately keep their stubs: it needs line breaking's
    pure/broken
    machinery.

  * Objects/SystemGrobVertical.cs -- system.cc's get_vertical_alignment and
    vertical_skyline_elements. The System grob definition names both in its
    object-callbacks, so they had to answer the moment a VerticalAlignment
    could exist; Objects/SystemGrob.cs stays closed.

  * Objects/GrobClosure.cs -- grob-closure.cc (axis_offset_symbol,
    axis_parent_positioning, add_offset_callback, chain_callback,
    chain_offset_callback) plus grob.cc's x/y_parent_positioning callbacks.
    The composition itself is NOT done in C#: grob::compose-function and
    grob::offset-function are looked up in the Scheme layer BY NAME at the
    moment of use, the same late-binding rule MusicFunctionSupport records.
    grob-closure.cc's ledger row stays with line breaking, which owes the rest
    of the
    unpure/pure closure story.

  * Layout/PageLayoutSpacing.cs -- page-layout-problem.cc's is_spaceable,
    read_spacing_spec, get_spacing_spec, get_fixed_spacing and
    add_stretchability, pulled forward because internal_get_minimum_
    translations reads every pair of adjacent staves through them. The solver
    is page layout's and the file's ledger row still says so. get_fixed_spacing
    skips upstream's pure-answer cache (get_cached_pure_property is line
    breaking's
    machinery); the answer is recomputed instead, never wrong.

  * Paper_column::get_interface_extent is carried in
    Objects/SelfAlignmentInterface.cs beside its only caller, ODDITY INCLUDED:
    upstream 2.27.2 measures each element relative to ITSELF, not to the
    column, and the port reproduces that.

  * Grob::get_vertical_axis_group and misc.cc's directed_round are carried in
    Objects/SidePositionInterface.cs beside their only callers.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PREMATURE ALIGNMENT TRIGGER, AND THE PURE STAND-IN THAT ABSORBS IT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The horizontal-spacing note on Spacing_interface::skylines -- "it does
  compute and cache a
  Y offset earlier than upstream would, which is a thing to re-check when
  vertical alignment starts moving staves" -- came due, and it was the
  single hardest thing in this pass.

  Upstream never reads a staff's unpure Y position before line breaking: the
  early readers go through the pure machinery, and a BARE unpure procedure
  answers #f in a pure context (call_pure_function), so an early pure read of
  a parent-positioned grob yields 0 WITHOUT running the alignment and WITHOUT
  consuming the one-shot offset. The port's spacing reads the REAL coordinate
  (the recorded horizontal-spacing stand-in), which triggers
  ly:grob::y-parent-positioning
  -> positioning-done -> the alignment, DURING System::pre_processing -- when
  the alignment has no system yet and the columns have no X positions.

  Two consequences had to be dealt with, and both cost a debugging round:

  1. Upstream's align_elements_to_ideal_distances REFUSES without a system.
     Refusing here burns every staff's one-shot offset at zero for good (a
     grob offset is computed exactly once and the property deleted), so every
     multi-staff score would keep stacking staves -- the exact defect class
     this group exists to remove.

  2. Letting the early call run the UNPURE minimum-distance stacking instead
     poisons the render: the skylines read stencils, and a STAFF SYMBOL's
     stencil depends on column X positions that are still all zero, so the
     staff lines cache at zero length and the final drawing collapses. This
     was measured, not theorized: clef-force.ly's staff lines went from
     x2=102.38 to x2=-0.05 before the fix.

  THE STAND-IN: when align_elements_to_ideal_distances finds no system, it
  takes upstream's own currency for before-line-breaking questions -- the
  PURE stacking over the full score range (start 0, end INT_MAX). Its
  skylines are flat boxes over the groups' Y extents (upstream's own pure
  shape in get_skylines), which touch no X-dependent stencil; and the full
  range brings basic-distance into the distances, exactly as upstream's pure
  estimate does, so staves land near their ideal separations rather than
