  packed. When the alignment DOES have a system (nothing triggered it early),
  it stacks at the unpure minimum distances -- still a stand-in for page
  layout's
  Page_layout_problem solution, recorded as such. Both stand-ins retire with
  line breaking (pure reads in spacing) + page layout (the real solver).

  A visible side effect, deliberate: single-staff pages now sit with the
  system's top at y=0 (the alignment translates the staff down, then
  System::post_processing re-anchors), a RIGID vertical shift of the whole
  drawing relative to earlier output. The comparator's inventory verdicts do
  not move.

  ⚠ WHAT THE STAND-IN COSTS WHEN IT FIRES, measured at PARITY 12 (2026-08-15),
  and it is not an approximation cost. The stand-in TRANSLATES the staves. So
  does page layout's find_system_offsets, later, with translate_axis -- which
  ADDS. Any early trigger therefore does not merely position the staves
  approximately; it makes the real solution land ON TOP of the stand-in's, and
  the staves come out one whole staff-staff-spacing too far apart. The
  separation was 18.0 where the oracle had 9.0, and every stage in between --
  the alignment's dy, the spring's ideal and minimum, the solved length, the
  applied translation -- reported the correct 9. Only the offset the staves
  ALREADY carried was wrong.

  The first trigger found was Stem::internal_pure_height reading an
  ordinary relative coordinate (see DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (stems and flags),
  RESOLVED at PARITY 12).
  ⚠ IT WAS NOT THE ONLY ONE, AND THIS PARAGRAPH SAID IT WAS FOR FOUR SESSIONS.
  PARITY 18 found a SECOND of the same class -- Staff_symbol_referencer::
  pure_get_position answering the ordinary position, reached from
  Dot_column::calc_positioning_done before line breaking, which is what made
  minimise_least_squares report "vertical spacing is triggered before line
  breaking" on every sweep. See PARITY GRIND -- THE PURE READS THAT WERE NOT
  PURE, at the end of this file. The prediction in the paragraph above HELD: the
  symptom was separations at exact multiples of a staff space, and the cause was
  a new early reader, not the stand-in.
  THE STAND-IN ITSELF STAYS, because the reason it exists --
  Spacing_interface::skylines reading early -- has not changed. If a
  cross-staff or multi-staff score ever again shows a separation that is an
  exact multiple of its spacing, look for a NEW early trigger first: the
  symptom is the doubling, not the stand-in.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ReadSkylinePair -- THE CONSTRUCTOR-DEFAULT STAND-IN
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Upstream's Grob constructor installs default callbacks for X-extent,
  Y-extent, vertical-skylines and horizontal-skylines (grob.cc:75-90), so a
  skyline property read ALWAYS answers a pair. The port's Grob deliberately
  does not install them yet (the first-light note), and this group is the first
  code that READS those properties broadly. AxisGroupInterfaceVertical
  .ReadSkylinePair is the stand-in at the read sites this group owns: when
  the property is unset it computes exactly what upstream's default callback
  would -- simple-skylines-from-extents, with a grob whose definition names
  no extent falling back on its STENCIL's extent, which is what the default
  X-extent/Y-extent callbacks answer. It also tolerates a bare CLR
  SkylinePair in the property, because the text-interface callbacks cache one
  (a
  defect, reported separately). When the constructor defaults land centrally,
  the fallback becomes dead code by construction.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (vertical organization)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  * PURE lookups take the unpure answers throughout (properties, coordinates,
    extents), the established text-interface/horizontal-spacing stand-in; the
    pure FLAG's
    control-flow effects (which stems are skipped, the xc=0 choice in
    aligned_side's Y branch, the spanner parent_relative choice) are kept
    exactly. Align_interface's pure get_skylines branch skips the
    begin-of-line refinement (rest_of_line_pure_height /
    begin_of_line_pure_height are line breaking's) and answers ONE flat box
    over the
    ordinary extent; Hara_kiri_group_spanner::request_suicide is "no" with
    the rest of the unported suicide machinery, in get_skylines and in
    Staff_grouper_interface::maybe_pure_within_group alike.

  * Side_position_interface::get_support_set collects into first-occurrence
    order where upstream uses std::unordered_set (unspecified order). The
    skyline arithmetic downstream is order-independent; the port's order is
    deterministic. Same choice Grob_array::remove_duplicates made.

  * skyline_spacing's priority sort is index-stabilised where upstream uses
    std::stable_sort, so tie order is upstream's insertion order.

  * aligned_side's "Improbable offset" diagnostic carries the F6 invariant
    formatting Stencil.cs already uses for the same message shape; the
    -dstrict-infinity-checking Scheme error branch is unreachable (the port
    carries no such option) and says so in a comment.

  * Vertical_align_engraver ODDITY KEPT: when alignAboveContext/
    alignBelowContext name a group that is not among the alignment's earlier
    elements, upstream pops the just-added group and never re-inserts it --
    the group silently drops out of the alignment. Ported as-is with a
    comment; the not-found warning guard makes it unreachable except for a
    context aligning against itself. The id table is a Dictionary<string,
    Grob> where upstream uses a Scheme hash table; alignAboveContext not
    found / alignBelowContext not found warnings match upstream's text and
    its else-shape (a non-string after_id prints empty).

  * Directional_element_interface's non-strict path re-reads the property
    for its return value exactly as upstream does (the value, not the
    computed dir), and is_on_axis's diagnostic fires on scm_is_true of the
    stencil property -- which counts an UNSET ('()) stencil as true, ported
    faithfully with a comment.

  * System_start_delimiter::staff_brace goes through make-left-brace-markup
    exactly as upstream's Lily::make_left_brace_markup does, so the
    brace0..braceN binary search stays in scm/define-markup-commands.scm
    where upstream keeps it. The delimiter takes a staff symbol's staff
    space directly when the element IS a staff symbol, working around the
    missing identity branch in StaffSymbolReferencer.GetStaffSymbol
    (reported as a finding).

  * ly:pure-call/ly:unpure-call reproduce upstream's asymmetry exactly: an
    unpure-pure container with the pure part omitted is "unchanging" and its
    UNPURE part is applied WITHOUT the start/end columns; a plain procedure
    in pure context gets (grob . rest), never start/end.

  * Side_position_interface.hh declares recursive_add_support and
    add_staff_support; NEITHER is defined anywhere in upstream 2.27.2. Dead
    declarations, not ported -- same disposition as Context_def::set_acceptor.

===============================================================================
BARS, METER, KEYS, MARKS
===============================================================================

  The whole group landed: bar-engraver, span-bar-engraver, span-bar-stub-engraver,
  bar-number-engraver, key-engraver, key-signature-interface,
  time-signature-engraver, timing-translator, metronome-engraver, mark-engraver,
  mark-tracking-translator, jump-engraver, caesura-engraver, breathing-sign,
  measure-grouping-spanner, measure-spanner, grid-line-interface,
  grid-line-span-engraver and grid-point-engraver.

  Timing_translator is the metric heartbeat: measurePosition, measureLength,
  currentBarNumber, internalBarNumber and measureStartNow are now REAL, the
  containing context answers to the Timing alias, and the global context is asked
  to stop at every measure boundary -- which is what finally makes a bar line
  exist as a moment. Bar_engraver reads what Timing maintains and hands the
  candidate glyph strings to scm/bar-line.scm, which decides everything visual.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE DEFECT THIS GROUP FOUND, AND IT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily-guile.cc's value_type_check begins with "'(), #f and *unspecified* always
  succeed". The port's SchemeUtilities.TypeCheckAssignment does not: it applies
  the predicate to everything, so upstream's ubiquitous set-to-'() idiom -- which
  Timing_translator performs on whichBar and measureStartNow at EVERY timestep --
  is refused with a programming error AND the stale value stays behind. Grob
  properties survive by accident (Grob.SetProperty ignores the check's verdict
  and stores anyway, with noise); context properties do not.

  Until the central three-line fix lands in SchemeUtilities.TypeCheckAssignment,
  this group's translators spell upstream's set-to-'() as an UNSET
  (TranslatorSchemeHelpers.SetContextProperty), which reads identically unless an ancestor
  context sets the same property -- for the affected properties (whichBar,
  measureStartNow, alternativeNumber, currentBarLine, timeSignature,
  beamHalfMeasure, autoBeaming) that ancestor would have to be Global, so the
  divergence is theoretical outside hand-built trees. Every such call site is
  marked and reverts to a plain SetProperty after the fix.

  The same defect class, second instance: TextInterface.IsMarkup filters
  markup-command-signature's answer through ToBool, which is true only for the
  C# boolean -- but a command's signature is a LIST, so every non-string markup
  reads as "not a markup" and InterpretMarkup refuses it ("Trying to interpret a
  non-markup object"). The group's engravers carry a correct scheme-truth
  IsMarkup
  in TranslatorSchemeHelpers until TextInterface is fixed centrally.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (bars, meter, keys, marks)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Missing-seam carriers, each a faithful port of code whose home file predates
  this session and may not be edited by it:

  * Translation/MeasureTiming.cs carries lily/context.cc's measure_length,
    measure_position (three overloads) and scaled_measure_position free
    functions. They belong beside Context.cs.
  * BarEngravers.cs carries grob.cc's get_root_vertical_alignment,
    get_vertical_axis_group and get_vertical_axis_group_index as the internal
    SpanBarVerticalOrder class. They answer by interface SYMBOL, so they work the
    moment VerticalAlignment grobs exist without referencing vertical-
    organization code.
  * TranslatorSchemeHelpers carries grob-array.cc's grob_list_to_grob_array and
    simple-event-listener.hh's Boolean_event_listener.
  * BreathingSign.cs carries staff-symbol-referencer.cc's staff_span and
    directional-element-interface.cc's get_strict_grob_direction as private
    helpers.

  Behavioural notes:

  * Timing_translator computes the minimum of volta-numbers with a plain fold
    where upstream applies guile's `min` over the list; same arithmetic.
  * Jump_engraver drops upstream's `bool first_time_` member: it is assigned and
    never read, and an assigned-but-unread private field is compiler warning
    CS0414 against a 0-warning gate.
  * Span_bar_engraver's stable sort is List.Sort with an index tiebreak
    (List.Sort is unstable; upstream uses std::stable_sort). Until vertical
    organization lands,
    every sort key is -1 and announcement order is preserved.
  * Bar_engraver's BarType enum is spelled BarLayerType: it is a file-scoped
    implementation detail upstream, and "BarType" invites collision with the
    bar-type event property naming.
  * Caesura_engraver creates CaesuraScript grobs but cannot configure them:
    make_script_from_event lives in script-interface.cc (scripts group). Each
    script
    raises a programming error naming the owner rather than throwing, so caesura
    regression files keep engraving their BreathingSign halves. The
    script-priority comparator (two lines upstream) is repeated locally for the
    stop-timestep sort and marked for deduplication when the scripts group
    lands.
  * Measure_spanner::print throws (loudly, naming bracket.cc) when a
    bracket must actually be drawn; the text half is complete.
  * [DISCHARGED by line breaking's close-out.] The pure-height entry points
    this group
    registered (ly:grob-pure-height, ly:axis-group-interface::pure-height)
    answered the ORDINARY extents. Both are registered for real now, in
    LineBreakingCallbacks, and the two stand-ins here are gone. What the entry
    understated:
    an ordinary extent does not merely approximate a pure one, it asks for a
    STENCIL -- so these two were pulling stencils out of grobs during spacing.
  * The alignment callbacks this group's grobs carry (self-alignment,
    side-position, break-align anchors) are stood in with 0.0 -- the
    force-hara-kiri-callback device. Without them the FIRST multi-measure score
    died in Paper_score::process, because the stubs' inert values reached Scheme
    arithmetic. Bar numbers and marks therefore EXIST at x=0/y=0 rather than
    aligned; that is visible, not silent, and the real callbacks overwrite the
    stand-ins when their groups land.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ACCIDENTALS AND PITCH MACHINERY
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The whole group landed: accidental.cc, accidental-placement.cc,
  accidental-engraver.cc, ambitus-engraver.cc, pitch-interval.cc,
  relative-octave-check.cc, relative-octave-music.cc, pitched-trill-engraver.cc,
  pitch-squash-engraver.cc, note-name-engraver.cc, clef-modifier.cc and
  cue-clef-engraver.cc. The two relative-octave files merged into one .cs
  (Music/RelativeOctaveMusic.cs), each keeping its provenance on the namespace
  line.

  NAMING

  Pitch_interval derives from Drul_array<Pitch> upstream; the port's DrulArray
  is a struct and cannot be a base class, so PitchInterval HOLDS one instead.
  Same state, same operations, and the same for Pitch_lexicographic_interval.

  DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES

  * Accidental_placement's sorts (ape_less over columns, acc_less inside one)
    are STABLE in the port where upstream uses std::sort. Same reason as
    Skyline and SimpleSpacer before it: acc_less ties every pair of naturals,
    .NET's introsort validates its comparer where the C++ carries on, and a
    stable order is a reproducible one.
  * uniquify over the heads-and-stems list dedupes keeping FIRST OCCURRENCE;
    upstream sorts BY POINTER first, an address-dependent order the algorithm
    never relies on -- the list only ever becomes a set of boxes.
  * The stagger key: upstream hashes the acknowledging engraver's ADDRESS to
    separate same-name accidentals from different voices; the port hashes the
    object's identity hash. The value only has to differ between sources, and
    does.
  * build_heads_skyline reads the PURE Y extent upstream; the port reads the
    real extent -- the standing pure-machinery divergence, same as
    every earlier group.
    [DISCHARGED by the staff-line root-cause work: reads the pure extent now,
    with the rest of its class -- see the staff-line close-out entry near the
    end.]
  * Accidental_engraver writes `forced' through the entry's accidental with a
    null-conditional where upstream writes unconditionally; the flags imply the
    accidental was just created, so the `?.` only changes what happens in a
    state upstream would have crashed in.

  AN UPSTREAM ODDITY, PORTED AS IT STANDS

  Accidental_placement::get_relevant_accidentals accumulates its break-reminder
  and real-accidental lists across the element loop WITHOUT clearing, so a
  second element re-contributes the first one's accidentals to the result.
  Kept: the faithfulness rule says a plausible tidy-up is a parity bug, and a
  column carries one placement grob in practice. The engraver's own
  where-does-localAlterations-get-written TODO comment is carried verbatim too.

  PULLED FORWARD, AND WHY

  * Side_position_interface::add_support (side-position-interface.cc, PARTIAL
    port) -- one line over PointerGroupInterface.AddUnorderedGrob. The
    positioning algorithms stay with their owning group and the ledger row
    stays `group'.
  * Tie::head (tie.cc, the tie group's file) as a private static TieHead in
    Translation/AccidentalEngraver.cs: the tie-forgetting protocol needs it
    and no Tie machinery exists yet. The tie group should lift it out when
    tie.cc lands.
  * measure_number, note_end_mom and preorder_walk (lily/context.cc) live as
    `internal static class MeasureCounting` in
    Translation/AccidentalEngraver.cs, because context.cc's ported half does
    not carry them and group sessions may not edit already-ported files. They
    belong in Translation/Context.cs.
  * ly:grob::x-parent-positioning (grob.cc -- marked ported, binding still a
    stub) is registered from AccidentalCallbacks: it is the X-offset of exactly the
    grobs the accidentals group makes, and its whole job is to DEMAND the
    placement's
    positioning-done. Left stubbed, the packing algorithm never ran at all --
    silently, the exact defect class the callback-fence comment in
    GrobCallbacks.cs warns about. y-parent-positioning stays with grob.cc's
    binding sweep; nothing demands it yet.

  WHAT THE ERROR-FAMILY DIAGNOSIS FOUND (out of lane, documented, not fixed)

  * The accidental-* ERRORs are `(- to from)` on two PITCHES inside \transpose
    (ly/music-functions-init.ly line 2569). scm/operators.scm's GOOPS methods
    DO load, into (lily); but LilyScheme's define-method rebinds the name in
    the DEFINING module instead of extending the PRIMITIVE-GENERIC globally
    the way Guile does, and a parser scope searches the root module first --
    faithful to upstream's ly_make_module -- so `-' still resolves to the raw
    numeric primitive there. Measured: (lily)'s `-' is a generic procedure,
    the parser scope's is #<primitive ->, and (- moment moment) fails at .ly
    toplevel too.
  * The cue-clef-* ERRORs never reach the (now ported) Cue_clef_engraver: they
    die in \addQuote. add-quotable's recording-group-emulate registers Scheme
    listeners through ly:add-listener -- an unported stub from
    dispatcher-scheme.cc -- so its context list comes back empty,
    (member raw-voice context-list) answers #f, and the find-non-empty loop
    cars it. Behind that same door: ly:quote-iterator::constructor is also
    still on the iterator worklist.

===============================================================================
CENTRAL INTEGRATION OF THE PARALLEL GROUPS
===============================================================================

  Five groups landed in one parallel wave (columns through accidentals, 64
  ledger rows, 33 new
  translators, 82 new files) and were merged by a single integrator. What the
  merge changed beyond the union of the groups' own reports:

  DEDUPLICATIONS (same binding pulled forward independently by several groups):
  * ly:grob::x-parent-positioning / ly:grob::y-parent-positioning -- pulled by
    the columns, vertical-organization and accidentals groups (x) with
    identical semantics. The ONE registration
    lives in VerticalLayoutCallbacks over Objects/GrobClosure.cs; the other two
    copies were removed.
  * ly:unpure-call / ly:pure-call -- pulled by two of the groups, semantically
    identical (both keep upstream's dropped-start/end asymmetry). VerticalLayoutCallbacks'
    registrations stay; ColumnAndRestCallbacks' were removed.
  * The bars/meter group's nine zero stand-ins for vertical-organization names
    (self-alignment x6, side-position
    x-aligned-side/y-aligned-side/move-to-extremal-staff) were removed: the real
    REAL implementations landed in the same wave. Its three
    break-alignment-interface.cc anchors stay as zero stand-ins (line
    breaking's).
  * StemTremolo's private note-collision note_head_positions copy and
    SeparationItem's RelevantAccidentals placeholder now DELEGATE to the
    canonical NoteCollisionInterface / AccidentalPlacement that landed in-wave.
  * NoteHead's private direction helpers now delegate to the ported
    DirectionalElementInterface -- closing the twice-reported defect where the
    strict copy warned with the wrong text and never stored the direction.
  * The seam file still carries its faithful private copies (stem statics,
    direction trio, add_support, tie heads, script/measure helpers): re-pointing
    every columns-group caller at the in-wave canonical homes is deliberate
    follow-up
    work, listed in the plan, so the copies do not silently drift -- each names
    its upstream original, and the canonical classes now exist to diff against.
    Same for BarEngravers' SpanBarVerticalOrder, BreathingSign's private
    helpers, AccidentalEngraver's MeasureCounting/TieHead and the stems
    group's BeamHelpers (whose owner, beam.cc, is still the beams group's).

  CENTRAL DEFECT FIXES (each found by a Wave A group in already-ported code,
  reported per the rules, applied once by the integrator):
  * SchemeUtilities.TypeCheckAssignment now begins with upstream's "'(), #f and
    *unspecified* always succeed" rule (lily-guile.cc value_type_check). The
    long-standing pop-first/instrumentName/stencil type-check noise came from
    its absence, and refused CONTEXT writes left stale values behind.
    TranslatorSchemeHelpers.SetContextProperty (the workaround) is deleted and its call
    sites are plain SetProperty again.
  * TextInterface.IsMarkup tests markup-command-signature's answer with Scheme
    truth instead of the C# boolean, so non-string markups are markups again.
    TranslatorSchemeHelpers.IsMarkup (the workaround) is deleted.
  * GrobCallbacks' ten skyline registrations return the Scheme cons
    (.ToScheme()) instead of the CLR SkylinePair; VerticalLayoutCallbacks' repair
    re-registrations and ReadSkylinePair's CLR-acceptance branch are retired.
  * StaffSymbolReferencer.GetStaffSymbol has upstream's identity branch (a
    staff symbol IS its own staff symbol).
  * OriginTests joined the engine-global-state collection and its two
    Warn-recording tests filter for their own messages (the pre-existing
    parallel-collection race the stems group proved with a pristine-control
    run).

  STILL OPEN, INHERITED KNOWINGLY (owners unchanged):
  * The pure-* family stubs on axis-group-interface.cc (except pure-height,
    which the bars/meter demand gave an ordinary-extent stand-in) -- line
    breaking's.
  * AxisGroupInterface.GenericGroupExtent's missing skyline trigger loop --
    load-bearing when outside-staff grobs exist; the scripts group's or
    earlier.
  * The Grob constructor defaults (X/Y-extent, skylines) -- first-light note;
    ReadSkylinePair's computed fallback stands in at the vertical-organization
    read sites.
  * finite? / euclidean-remainder live in MeterAndKeyCallbacks until the next
    LilyScheme-touching session moves them into NumericPrimitives.

===============================================================================
REMAINING ITERATORS & MUSIC PLUMBING
===============================================================================

  All fifteen of the group's ledger rows landed, plus two pull-forwards the work
  demanded. The group's PURPOSE was reachability: nearly everything it touches was
  already implemented somewhere and simply never reached, because the music tree
  was never iterated far enough to ask for it.

  THE FILES

  * music-wrapper.cc -> Music/MusicWrapper.cs. Forty-two lines, pure delegation,
    and the single highest-leverage file left anywhere: every wrapper music type in
    scm/define-music-types.scm names ly:music-wrapper::length-callback, so without
    it \relative, \fixed, \transpose and \context all reported ZERO length. The
    sweep at the time recorded 19,940 failures of that one callback across ~1,374
    files, and an expression of zero length engraves an empty page.
  * context-specced-music-iterator.cc, initial-context-music-iterator.cc,
    change-iterator.cc, apply-context-iterator.cc -> Translation/ContextIterators.cs.
    The first of these is what makes \new X { } instantiate a context at all, which
    is why the whole vertical stack and the \partial/\time delivery had been
    invisible in sweeps and provable only by tests.
  * property-iterator.cc -> Translation/PropertyIterators.cs (all four iterators:
    \set, \unset, \override, \revert as MUSIC).
  * quote-iterator.cc -> Translation/QuoteIterator.cs.
  * part-combine-iterator.cc + part-combine-engraver.cc -> Translation/PartCombine.cs.
  * articulations.cc -> Translation/Articulations.cs.
  * grob-interface.cc -> Objects/GrobInterface.cs.
  * The four sibling-provenance rows closed as documentation, not new code:
    event-iterator.cc and simple-music-iterator.cc ride Translation/MusicIterator.cs
    (which carries the whole iterator base family), listener.cc rides
    Translation/Dispatcher.cs, and includable-lexer.cc rides
    Parsing/Lexing/ModalScanner.cs, whose input stack IS Includable_lexer. All four
    namespace lines now name their upstream file.

  PULLED FORWARD

  * dispatcher-scheme.cc (a long-tail file) -> Bootstrap/DispatcherPrimitives.cs,
    the WHOLE
    binding surface rather than just the one entry point that demanded it. \addQuote
    cannot run without ly:add-listener: scm/part-combiner.scm's
    recording-group-emulate makes a dispatcher, connects it to the context's event
    source, and registers Scheme procedures on it -- every step a binding in that
    file. ly:broadcast moved here from MeterAndKeyCallbacks, which had pulled it
    forward alone; its DispatcherPrimitives registration must therefore be installed
    AFTER MeterAndKeyCallbacks.
    Note on listener equality: upstream stores the raw procedure in the dispatcher's
    list where the port's list holds Listeners, so the port makes the procedure the
    listener's TARGET. Nothing removes a Scheme-added listener -- dispatcher-scheme.cc
    binds no ly:remove-listener -- so the removal-by-equality path is unreachable
    from Scheme in either upstream or the port.
  * break-substitution.cc's DIRECTION half (line breaking's file) ->
    Objects/BreakSubstitution.cs, with Grob::handle_prebroken_dependencies and
    Item's override beside it. The ledger row stays with line breaking (its
    recorded group tag) with a note, because the System half -- line breaking
    proper -- is still that group's.

  DEFECTS FOUND IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE

  1. System::pre_processing SKIPPED handle_prebroken_dependencies entirely, and the
     note here said it "only matters once lines are actually broken". MEASURED AND
     DISPROVEN: a clone starts with an EMPTY object alist (upstream's copy
     constructor leaves it that way on purpose), and ly:span-bar::before-line-breaking
     reads a SpanBar's elements with NO default, so it threw on every span bar --
     87 files in the sweep, all of them files whose \new PianoStaff / \new StaffGroup
     had only just started creating contexts. The pass now runs, in upstream's place.
  2. The same method ran before-line-breaking and springs-and-rods BACKWARD over the
     element list. Upstream walks those two FORWARD; only handle_prebroken_dependencies
     goes backward, and the reverse-order comment belonged to it. Both loops corrected.
  3. get_strict_grob_direction reported through a bare warning, so its diagnostic
     named no file and no bar. Upstream reports through me->warning (), i.e. at the
     grob's origin. Grob::warning / Grob::programming_error / Grob::origin are now
     ported (grob.cc's Diagnostics hooks, previously not carried) and
     DirectionalElementInterface uses them.

  SEAM RETIREMENT (the Wave A follow-up)

  The seam file went from 746 lines to the three families whose OWNING file
  was still unported: script-engraver.cc's make_script_from_event and the tie
  pair. Everything else was re-pointed at the canonical class -- Stem,
  DirectionalElementInterface, SidePositionInterface, MeasureTiming, GrobClosure --
  and the statics that had no home in their own already-ported file were MOVED into
  it rather than copied again:
    * pure_get_position / pure_get_rounded_position / set_position /
      pure_set_position / staff_span / pure_position_less -> StaffSymbolReferencer.
    * add_bound_item -> Spanner.
    * intlog2 -> Misc.
    * robust_symbol2string -> SchemeUtilities (lily-guile.cc's home).
  BreathingSign's three private copies (staff_span, get_strict_grob_direction and its
  grob-scoped warning wrapper) are gone the same way. The stems group's
  BeamHelpers stays: its owner, beam.cc, is the beams group's and has not
  landed.

  STILL OPEN, KNOWINGLY

  * fixup_refpoint's pass in System::pre_processing is still absent. Unlike
    handle_prebroken_dependencies it really does only matter once lines are broken,
    so it stays with line breaking.
  * scm/part-combiner.scm needs a procedure-with-setter that LilyScheme does not
    provide: \partCombine now reaches it and errors on
    (set! (split-index ...) ...). The part-combine family is the visible cost.
    CLOSED by the LilyScheme gap fixes -- see the block below.
  * The type check in Grob::SetProperty runs UNGATED where upstream gates it on
    do_internal_type_checking_global. That is older than this group and
    untouched here;
    grob-interface.cc's own check keeps its faithful gate (check-internal-types).
    ✅ CLOSED at PARITY 13. Both checks now sit under the one gate upstream puts
    them under, which scm/lily.scm defaults to #f. It was not free: upstream
    SETS grob properties it never DECLARES -- `in-note-padding' goes onto a
    System grob from Page_layout_problem and appears in no
    define-grob-properties.scm entry and in no interface list -- so the ungated
    check made the port warn "the property 'in-note-padding' does not exist"
    where the oracle is silent, and it did the same for every other undeclared
    or mistyped grob write in the corpus. Neither check's RESULT is used on
    either side (upstream discards the return value too), so gating them changes
    diagnostics and nothing else; the full sweep confirmed no geometry moved.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FOUR LilyScheme GAPS THE ITERATOR SWEEP MEASURED
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  All four fixes landed in CodeBrix.LilyScheme, not here; this block records what
  the PORT was doing wrong-by-proxy and what changed underneath it. The
  interpreter's own AGENT-README carries the mechanisms in full.

  1. define-method now extends a generic-capable PRIMITIVE GLOBALLY.

     lily/operators.scm specializes -, +, * and / on <Moment>, <Duration> and
     <Pitch>. LilyScheme was defining a fresh generic in the DEFINING module, so
     the extension was invisible from every other one -- and a parser scope
     evaluating (- pitch pitch) for \transpose resolved the raw numeric '-' and
     threw wrong-type-arg. 28 files in the group's sweep, the whole accidental and
     \transpose family.

     Upstream's mechanism is not a module operation at all: oop/goops.scm's
     add-method! calls enable-primitive-generic! on the subr, which hangs the
     generic off the PRIMITIVE OBJECT. Every module shares that object, so the
     extension is global by construction. LilyScheme now does the same.

     NOT fixed by reordering LilyModules.Make. That was the tempting shortcut and
     the work order explicitly ruled it out: it diverges from ly_make_module
     shadowing for every binding, and it would not have made the extension global.

  2. GOOPS #:accessor carries a setter.

     scm/part-combiner.scm's (set! (split-index state) idx) expands to
     ((setter split-index) state idx). GOOPS's #:accessor makes an <accessor> --
     a generic whose setter is a generic -- but LilyScheme's define-class emitted
     a bare lambda, so `setter' threw wrong-type-arg: 28 files, the whole
     \partCombine family. The prelude now emits a make-procedure-with-setter.

     Measured before changing it: no caller anywhere in scm/ writes through the
     accessor's two-argument form, and no accessor name in scm/ is declared for
     two DIFFERENT slots, so the port's accessors did not also need to become
     generics. If a future upstream sync introduces either, that changes.

  3. Five unbound Guile-core names, all now in LilyScheme:
       ceiling-quotient (47 files), string-capitalize (22), substring/shared (7),
       mkdir (1), and ice9-format (1).

     floor-quotient was a SIXTH, hidden: lily-library.scm:1484 picks between
     ceiling-quotient and floor-quotient in one `if', so both arms evaluate as
     variable references and the log could only ever name the first. Counting
     from the log alone would have left it for the next sweep.

     ice9-format was not a missing procedure at all. scm/lily.scm opens with
     ((ice-9 format) #:select ((format . ice9-format))) and LilyScheme's
     use-modules dropped #:select's RENAMING form -- it took the module head and
     imported everything, which is a superset for bare selects and a silently
     unbound name for renamed ones. Renames are honoured now. The RESTRICTION
     still is not: scope stays wider than Guile's, never narrower, and narrowing
     it wants a session that can sweep behind the removal.

  4. finite? and euclidean-remainder MOVED out of Bootstrap/MeterAndKeyCallbacks.cs into
     LilyScheme's NumericPrimitives, where a Guile-core name belongs (the
     bars/meter group's FINDINGS 3, carried by this work order). They are
     DELETED here rather
     than duplicated: a copy would shadow the interpreter's own and re-create the
     divergence the move removes. The LilyScheme versions compute over that
     library's numeric tower instead of through this port's Rational, whose
     biased-by-one denominator is a documented trap.

     euclidean-remainder arrived with the floor/ceiling/truncate/euclidean
     quotient-remainder family, because Guile defines them as one family and the
     remainder is derived from the quotient so the division identity holds
     exactly. Guile's round-* and centered-* are deliberately absent: nothing in
     LilyPond names them, and an undemanded rounding rule is a parity bug waiting
     for its first caller.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
REPEATS AND GRACE, FIRST SLICE: FOUR ITERATORS AND grace-music.cc
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The repeats/voltas territory is 24 upstream files / ~4,900 lines. This pass
  landed the part that
  is genuinely SELF-CONTAINED and left the rest, rather than stubbing toward it:

    fine-iterator.cc              -> Translation/MeasureControlIterators.cs
    premeasure-iterator.cc        -> Translation/MeasureControlIterators.cs
    measure-remainder-iterator.cc -> Translation/MeasureControlIterators.cs
    grace-iterator.cc             -> Translation/GraceIterators.cs
    grace-music.cc                -> Translation/GraceIterators.cs

  Iterator constructors: 18 of 28 -> 22 of 28. Still owed are this group's
  remaining five and the lyrics group's one:
    volta-repeat, alternative-sequence, volta-specced, percent-repeat, tuplet
    lyric-combine (lyrics group)

  WHY THOSE FIVE WERE HELD BACK, and it is not arbitrary. Three of them are
  built on Repeat_styler (repeat-styler.cc, ~300 lines, an abstract base with
  null / segno / volta subclasses), and volta-specced reads volta_bracket_depth
  and volta_brackets_enabled OFF Alternative_sequence_iterator, which in turn
  needs the styler. Landing any one of them alone means writing a stand-in for
  exactly the machinery the group exists to port -- the kind of half-reproduced
  declaration this project's standing rule 4 is about. They go together, with
  the styler, in the group's next pass.

  ONE NEW-IN-FAMILY FILE: Music/MusicFactory.cs, the managed stand-in for
  upstream's Lily::make_music / Lily::make_span_event.

  It exists because of a distinction that is easy to miss and silent when
  missed. Upstream announces events two different ways, and the port has to
  match each:

    send_stream_event (context, "GraceChange", origin)
        -> a Stream_event built directly. Port: Context.MakeEvent +
           SendStreamEvent. Grace_iterator uses this one.

    Lily::make_music (sym) ... ev->send_to_context (ctx)
        -> a MUSIC object built through Scheme's make-music, which reads
           scm/define-music-types.scm and initialises `name' and `types'.
           Port: MusicFactory.MakeMusic + MusicObject.SendToContext.
           Premeasure_iterator and Measure_remainder_iterator use this one.

  MusicObject.ToEvent derives the stream event's CLASS from `name'/`types'. A
  music object built with `new MusicObject' carries neither, so the event it
  becomes matches NO engraver's listener -- and nothing fails, because a
  broadcast nobody hears is silent by design. The first draft of these
  iterators took the Context.MakeEvent shortcut for all of them and would have
  shipped exactly that.

  FAITHFULNESS NOTES on the four:
  * Fine_iterator derives from Simple_music_iterator, NOT Event_iterator, and
    upstream says why: there are conditions under which it does not send its
    event (inside LyricCombineMusic) and complicating Event_iterator for them is
    not worth it. Kept as upstream has it.
  * Grace_iterator converts between two clocks -- the outside moment and the
    wrapped music's -- and announces GraceChange so Grace_engraver can tell
    \stemNeutral \grace { ... apart from \grace { \stemNeutral ... . The
    Grace_engraver that consumes it is NOT yet ported (still this group's),
    so the
    event is currently announced to nobody. That is upstream's own ordering, not
    a gap: the iterator is correct on its own terms and the engraver is the next
    file in the same group.
  * Measure_remainder_iterator SKIPS its start event when the music has zero
    main length -- upstream's comment explains that it would otherwise try to
    set measureLength = 0 at a measure boundary, which is invalid and warns.
  * grace-music.cc's start callback returns a moment whose GRACE part is the
    negated total length and whose main part is zero. Getting the two parts the
    wrong way round is the classic Moment error and would place every grace note
    a whole note early instead of in grace time.


================================================================================
REPEATS, VOLTAS, PERCENT, TUPLETS, GRACE
================================================================================

  The group's other nineteen files, landed in one session after the first
  slice's five. The five remaining ITERATORS had to go together and this is
  why: Volta_repeat_iterator OWNS the Repeat_styler, Alternative_sequence_
  iterator BORROWS its enclosing repeat's, and Volta_specced_music_iterator
  reads volta_bracket_depth / volta_brackets_enabled off the alternative
  sequence. Landing any one alone means writing a stand-in for exactly the
  machinery the group exists to port.

  NEW-IN-FAMILY FILE: Music/MomentInterval.cs, the third concrete
  instantiation of the C++ template Interval_t<T>. Flower's Interval.cs
  realises the other two and its own note explains why the template is not
  carried across generically -- the empty sentinels differ in KIND per element
  type. It lives in the Engine, not in Flower, because Moment is an Engine type
  and Flower may not depend on the Engine; upstream has no such constraint
  because interval.hh is a header template.

  It carries Flower's "assigned" flag for the same load-bearing reason, and
  the reason is not theoretical here: Repeat_styler initialises spanned_time_
  to {infinity, infinity}, NOT to the empty interval, and
  Alternative_sequence_iterator::analyze compares the current moment against
  BOTH ends to decide whether an alternative group is aligned with its repeat.
  An empty interval's right end is MINUS infinity, so the empty default would
  have made every alternative group read as end-aligned before report_start
  ever ran. Fenced by Epg17RemainderTests.

  THREE PULL-FORWARDS, each demand-driven and each recorded on the group it
  was taken from:

  * lily/bracket.cc WHOLE, from the scripts group. Both of this group's
    bracket grobs draw through Bracket::make_bracket. The file declares no
    Scheme callbacks, so the owner's
    own bracket grobs (HorizontalBracket, BassFigureBracket, ottava, piano
    pedal) are untouched and the ledger row flips to ported.

  * lily/bezier-bow.cc IN PART, from the slurs group -- the closed-form shape
    half only
    (slur_shape and its three helpers), for \tupletSlur. Bezier_bow itself is
    the curve-fitting the faithfulness rule is about and stays the slurs
    group's, so that ledger row STAYS GROUPED. Same shape as the iterator
    group's partial pull of break-substitution.cc's Direction half from line
    breaking.

  * lily/spanner-scheme.cc WHOLE, from the long tail, under standing rule 3: a
    *-scheme.cc file is never a work item of its own, and whoever ports a type
    owes its LY_DEFINE surface. Spanner.cs landed long ago without one, and
    this group is the first to NEED it. Measured, not assumed: before this,
    `\times 2/3 { c8 d e }' died on #<unported ly:spanner-bound>.

  FINDINGS -- defects in already-ported code, all found by running the new
  grobs rather than by reading:

  1. ly:grob-array-filter HAD ITS ARGUMENTS REVERSED. Upstream's signature is
     (PREDICATE, ARRAY) -- like Scheme's own `filter', and unlike every other
     ly:grob-array-* binding, which take the array first. The port read them
     the other way round. VoltaBracketSpanner is the first grob whose
     define-grobs.scm callback calls it, so nothing had reached it and nothing
     had failed; the moment a volta bracket existed, the whole file died on
     wrong-type-arg ("ly:grob-array-filter" ... #<procedure grob::is-live?>).
     Fixed in Bootstrap/GrobPrimitives.cs with the reason in a comment.

  2. Grob::check_cross_staff was never ported, though grob.cc's ledger row
     says `ported'. Tuplet_bracket::calc_cross_staff needs it. Added to
     Objects/Grob.cs. Same class of gap the iterator group found three of.

  3. check_repeat_count_visibility was never ported, though context.cc's row
     says `ported'. Both percent engravers ask it before making their counter
     grobs, which is what makes
     \set repeatCountVisibility = #(every-nth-repeat-slash-visible 3) work.
     Added to Translation/Context.cs beside break_allowed, its neighbour
     upstream.

  4. LilyScheme was missing `->bool', which Guile defines in boot-9.scm as
     (define (->bool x) (not (not x))). scm/ reaches it through the tuplet
     path. Added to CorePrimitives; this is a CodeBrix.LilyScheme change.

  5. MusicObject did not implement IDiagnostics, though upstream declares
     `class Music : Preinit_Music, public Prob, public Diagnostics' and the
     port had declared the interface up front with no implementors at all.
     Alternative_sequence_iterator needs `alt->warning (...)' to point at the
     offending alternative rather than at nothing. Implemented EXPLICITLY,
     because the interface's accessor and MusicObject's existing Origin
     property cannot both carry that name unqualified.

  6. Spanner::spanner_length WAS NOT THE UPSTREAM ALGORITHM. Upstream tries
     three sources in order -- the X-positions property, then the X entries of
     left-bound-info / right-bound-info, then the bounds' own coordinates
     RELATIVE TO NULL, i.e. absolute. The port had only a variant of the third,
     and that variant referenced each bound to ITS OWN X parent, which is a
     different number for each bound and collapses to zero whenever the two sit
     at the same offset within their parents. VoltaBracket is the first grob to
     call it. Fixed in Objects/Spanner.cs with all three tiers.

  7. ly:wide-char->utf-8 ANSWERED NUL TO EVERY REAL CALL. Upstream is
     scm_c_make_string (1, scm_integer_to_char (wc)) -- the argument is an
     INTEGER CODEPOINT. The port read it as a SchemeChar and fell back to
     codepoint ZERO otherwise, and every caller in scm/ and ly/ passes an
     integer: the hair spaces around boxed rehearsal-mark numbers (#x200a), the
     IJ/ij ligatures, and the whole chord-modifier set. Nothing failed, because
     a NUL is a perfectly good string to the engine; it surfaced only when this
     group
     put enough marks on the page for the comparator to report a new verdict
     class, UNPARSEABLE -- NUL is not a legal XML character, so the SVG was not
     well-formed. Fixed in Bootstrap/GeneralPrimitives.cs.

     THE LESSON FOR LATER SESSIONS: the comparator grades what it can parse. Six
     files went GLYPHS-DIFFER -> UNPARSEABLE the moment this became visible, and
     a handful more went MISSING -> UNPARSEABLE, which the ratchet counts as an
     improvement. Watch for UNPARSEABLE in a verdict summary; it means the port
     is emitting bytes that are not XML, which is a different and worse failure
     than drawing the wrong thing.

  FAITHFULNESS NOTES:

  * Grace_engraver registers its GraceChange listener on the context's EVENT
    SOURCE by hand, in initialize, and removes it in finalize -- exactly as
    upstream does. It deliberately does NOT use Translator.ListenTo, which
    registers on EventsBelow: that dispatcher additionally carries events
    announced by DESCENDANT contexts, and switching this context into grace
    mode because a descendant entered grace is not upstream's behaviour.

  * Alternative_sequence_iterator's alt_restores_ is a typed list of
    (context, symbol, value) rather than a Scheme list of the same three.
    Upstream needs the Scheme form so the GC marks it and so scm_apply_0 can
    splat it onto ly:context-set-property!; neither applies here, and the
    restore calls Context.SetProperty directly, which is what that binding
    does. The measurePosition special case -- rebuilding the moment with the
    CURRENT grace part and the SAVED main part -- is kept exactly, because
    without it a repeat whose alternatives have different grace timing
    restores the first alternative's grace position into the second.

  * Tuplet_engraver's four upstream acknowledgers (note_column, script,
    finger, string_number) become interface tests inside one AcknowledgeGrob,
    since the port has no acknowledger macro layer. The dynamic-interface
    exclusion on scripts is upstream's and is kept.

  * Volta_engraver keeps upstream's per-depth LAYER list, including layer 0
    for manual \set repeatCommands, and the nested-return suppression in
    Repeat_styler::report_return. Both exist so that nested folded repeats do
    not print two D.S. instructions; the suppression is fenced directly by
    Epg17RemainderTests because no single concrete styler exercises it.

  * Tuplet_description measures a tuplet on the GRACE clock whenever either
    end carries a grace part, and on the main clock otherwise. A tuplet that
    starts in grace time but whose length has no grace part is still measured
    in grace time -- reading the main part there would put its end a whole
    note away from its start.

  KNOWN LIMIT, deliberate: Tuplet_number's knee-against-beam placement is
  ported in full but cannot take effect until the beams group lands Beam,
  because it
  asks Stem::get_beam and then reads the beam's `knee' and
  `quantized-positions'. With no beam it takes the bracket-based branch,
  which is upstream's own behaviour for unbeamed tuplets, so nothing is wrong
  today and nothing needs revisiting when beams arrive.

================================================================================
LYRICS & MELODY
================================================================================

  All ten of the group's files, in one session, with NO pull-forward from any
  other group -- the first group on this board that needed no neighbour's file.
  What it did need from elsewhere was not files but THREE FREE FUNCTIONS that
  already-ported files declare and had never carried. Those are the findings
  below; the ledger rows for context.cc and item.cc stay `ported', because the
  file was ported and the statics were the gap.

  The ten rows land in six C# files, because upstream splits by class where the
  port groups by subject:

    lyric-combine-music-iterator.cc  Translation/LyricCombineIterator.cs
    lyric-combine-music.cc           Bootstrap/LyricCallbacks.cs (one callback)
    lyric-engraver.cc                Translation/LyricEngravers.cs
    extender-engraver.cc             Translation/LyricEngravers.cs
    hyphen-engraver.cc               Translation/LyricEngravers.cs
    lyric-extender.cc                Objects/LyricExtender.cs
    lyric-hyphen.cc                  Objects/LyricHyphen.cs
    vowel-transition.cc              Objects/VowelTransition.cs
    melody-spanner.cc                Objects/MelodySpanner.cs
    melody-engraver.cc               Translation/MelodyEngraver.cs

  G5 IS CLOSED. ly:lyric-combine-music-iterator::constructor was the twenty-
  eighth and last, so IteratorPrimitives.NotYetPorted is EMPTY and its fence is
  assert-empty from here on (Epg18Tests, Epg22IteratorTests,
  Epg17RemainderTests). The list itself is KEPT rather than deleted: a music
  type with no registered constructor falls through to a default iterator that
  silently drops its meaning, so anything that ever leaves the ported table has
  to be named there instead of vanishing.

  THREE STATICS THAT WERE NEVER CARRIED, all found by porting their callers:

  1. melisma_busy (lily/context.cc). The function that decides whether lyrics
     hold the current syllable or start a new one. Two rules, both upstream's,
     and the FIRST one is the one worth stating: when a context has children,
     they are the authority and EVERY one of them must be busy -- a melisma in
     one voice of a divided staff must not hold the whole staff's lyrics. Only
     a leaf reads melismaBusyProperties. Three callers demanded it in this
     session alone (Lyric_combine_music_iterator::start_new_syllable,
     Lyric_engraver::stop_translation_timestep, Extender_engraver). Added to
     Translation/Context.cs.

  2. find_context_below (lily/context.hh). The DOWN-only sibling of
     find_context_near, which the port already had. Lyric_combine_music_
     iterator uses it twice and the difference matters: searching downward from
     the top context is how \lyricsto finds a Voice ANYWHERE in the score,
     where a near search would stop at the first match on the way out.
     find_top_context needed no port -- Context.Root already is it.

  3. spanned_time_interval (lily/item.cc). Vowel_transition::set_spacing_rods
     is its first caller here. Its collapse rule is load-bearing and easy to
     get wrong: an end with no item, or an item with no column, takes the OTHER
     end's moment rather than leaving the interval empty, so the answer is
     always a real span and a zero-length one means "same moment". Vowel
     transition tests that length against zero to decide whether to reserve
     space at all after a line break; an empty interval there would have
     reserved space for every broken transition. Its neighbour
     Spanner::spanned_time is a one-liner over it and is STILL not carried --
     no caller demands it yet, and it is recorded here so the next session that
     meets it knows the shape of the gap rather than re-deriving it.

  DIVERGENCES, all mechanical:

  * Lyric_combine_music_iterator::derived_mark has no counterpart. It exists to
    keep four SCM members and the child iterator reachable for Guile's
    collector; the CLR reaches them through the fields.

  * Lyric_extender::print calls Lily::unbroken_spanner_p, which is
    (eq? spanner (ly:grob-original spanner)) and nothing else. ly:grob-original
    answers the grob itself when it has no original, so the port tests
    Original == null directly rather than crossing into Scheme for a pointer
    comparison.

  * Vowel_transition reads bound-details through SchemeUtilities.Assq rather
    than a port of ly_assoc_get. Every key it looks up is a SYMBOL, for which
    upstream's assoc and assq agree exactly. Interval_t<Moment>::length () has
    no counterpart on MomentInterval, so its one call is written out as
    right - left, which is what that method is.

  * Melody_spanner::calc_neutral_stem_direction walks a SIGNED index with -1
    standing in for VPOS. Upstream's vsize is unsigned and the algorithm relies
    on TWO wraps -- last_nonneutral + 1 == 0 on the first pass, and
    last_nonneutral-- stepping 0 back to VPOS -- both of which are ordinary
    arithmetic on a signed index. The loop conditions are rewritten to match
    and nothing else about the walk changes. Fenced with hand-computed
    expectations in Epg18Tests, because a wrong answer here is a stem pointing
    the wrong way, not a missing glyph, and no sweep would isolate it.

  * The three lyric engravers' acknowledge_lyric_syllable (Grob_info_t<Item>)
    and Melody_engraver's acknowledge_stem / acknowledge_slur become interface
    tests at the top of one AcknowledgeGrob, since the port has no acknowledger
    macro layer. Same treatment as Tuplet_engraver's four.

  FAITHFULNESS NOTES:

  * Extender_engraver creates an auto-extender SPECULATIVELY and kills it when
    the next lyric event turns out not to be an underscore. That is upstream's
    design, not an accident of translation, and it is why
    pending_autoextender_melisma_busy_ exists and why the melisma test is
    delayed to stop_translation_timestep -- a melisma can start during the
    time step's own processing.

  * Extender_engraver and Hyphen_engraver deliberately do NOT use
    assign_event_once on the lyric and hyphen events they merely OBSERVE.
    Upstream says why in both files: the engraver that OWNS the event warns
    about conflicts, and warning twice about one input error is worse than not
    warning here at all.

  * Lyric_engraver treats a syllable whose text is a single SPACE as "hold the
    previous one": it prints nothing and re-aligns the PREVIOUS syllable by
    lyricMelismaAlignment. The comparison is against a Scheme string, so it
    goes through SchemeUtilities.IsEqual rather than a C# string compare.

  * get_current_note_head finds the head sounding NOW by walking busyGrobs and
    keeping the one whose recorded END equals now plus its own event's length.
    busyGrobs records only ends, so that arithmetic is upstream's way of asking
    "did this grob start here". Its own comment records that includeGraceNotes
    cannot be obeyed through this mechanism; kept verbatim.

  KNOWN LIMIT, deliberate and not the lyrics group's to close:
  Lyric_hyphen::print and
  Vowel_transition::set_spacing_rods both branch on break_status_dir and on
  find_prebroken_piece, so their broken-line halves could not take effect until
  line breaking landed. [It has since landed, so both branches are now
  reachable; neither was re-measured by that session and both are worth a look
  the next time a lyric file's verdict moves.]

  ONE ENTRY POINT IS DEAD UPSTREAM AND IS PORTED ANYWAY:
  ly:lyric-combine-music::length-callback. lyric-combine-music.cc declares it
  with MAKE_SCHEME_CALLBACK, so it is one of the 737 in the closure and G3
  counts it -- but NOTHING references it. scm/define-music-types.scm gives
  LyricCombineMusic `length . INF-MOMENT' rather than a length-callback, and
  the callback reads `elements' off the music while
  scm/ly-syntax-constructors.scm's make-lyric-combine sets `element'
  (singular) and no `elements' at all. Called, upstream would dereference a
  null Music; the port throws a wrong-type error naming the procedure instead.
  Ported faithfully otherwise, and recorded here so a later reader does not
  take its silence in the sweep for a gap.

  WHERE EACH ENGRAVER IS REACHED FROM, because they are not alike:
  ly/engraver-init.ly consists Lyric_engraver, Extender_engraver and
  Hyphen_engraver in the Lyrics context, so any file with lyrics reaches all
  three. Melody_engraver is DELIBERATELY COMMENTED OUT in the Voice definition
  upstream (`%  \consists Melody_engraver'), so it is opt-in and exactly three
  regression files ask for it -- stem-direction-context.ly and its two
  siblings. That is why the melody half is fenced by hand-computed unit tests
  rather than by sweep movement: three files is not a signal.

  TWO DEFECTS IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE, both silent until the lyrics group ran:

  1. THE BIG ONE — Music_iterator did not hold its context through a
     Context_handle, so EVERY CONTEXT IN THE SCORE READ AS REMOVABLE.

     Upstream's music-iterator.hh declares `Context_handle handle_;' and routes
     get_own_context/set_own_context through it. That is not bookkeeping: the
     handle's client count is exactly what makes Context::is_removable answer
     FALSE for a context an iterator is still reporting to. The port stored a
     plain `Context _context;' field, so client_count_ stayed 0 everywhere and
     a freshly created Voice — no children, no clients — reported itself
     removable while its own notes were still being iterated.

     Nothing failed loudly for four sessions because almost nothing ASKS.
     Lyric_combine_music_iterator::run_always is the first caller in the port
     to depend on the answer, and the consequence was total:

       run_always () = lyric_iter_ && lyric_iter_->ok ()
                       && !(music_context_ && music_context_->is_removable ())

     find_voice succeeded, set music_context_ to the Voice, the Voice reported
     itself removable, run_always went FALSE, and
     Simultaneous_music_iterator::create_contexts — which erases any child that
     is not ok () right after init_context — DELETED THE ENTIRE \lyricsto
     BRANCH before a single syllable was iterated. `\addlyrics' happened to
     survive because its generated wrapper kept the branch ok, which is why the
     two spellings behaved differently and why this looked like a \lyricsto
     parsing problem at first.

     Fixed in Translation/MusicIterator.cs: OwnContext now reads and writes
     through a ContextHandle. Context.CheckRemoval and everything else that
     consults IsRemovable now get upstream's answer. Expect this to move the
     sweep beyond lyrics — it changes when contexts die across the whole suite,
     which is what upstream intends.

  2. Grob did not implement IDiagnostics, though upstream declares
     `class Grob : public Smob<Grob>, public Diagnostics'. The iterator group
     added
     Warning/ProgrammingError/Origin to Grob as plain members and stopped
     there, so a grob had the diagnostic surface but not the TYPE: an
     `(IDiagnostics)' cast on a Spanner compiled and threw
     InvalidCastException at run time, which is how spanner-break-beyond-parent
     went SVG -> NOOUT the moment Hyphen_engraver warned about an unterminated
     hyphen. Same family as the repeats group's finding that MusicObject did
     not implement
     it either. Fixed by declaring the interface on Grob and mapping
     IDiagnostics.Origin() to the existing Origin property.

  ONE PULL-FORWARD: lily/grob-pq-engraver.cc WHOLE, from the beams group.

  Demand-driven twice over. `busyGrobs' has exactly one writer in the whole
  engine and this is it, so with the engraver unported the property was
  permanently '() -- which meant get_current_note_head could never answer, no
  extender ever collected a head, and Extender_engraver's completize step could
  never set a right bound. Lyric_extender::print then reached
  `common->common_refpoint (...)' on a null common and took FOURTEEN files down
  with a NullReferenceException. Separately, Grob_pq_engraver was the
  SECOND-MOST-DEMANDED unported translator anywhere in the sweep (5,017 "unknown
  translator" warnings), behind only Font_size_engraver.

  It is 172 upstream lines, declares no grobs, and its only Scheme surface is
  ly:grob-pq<? -- which comes with it under the bindings rule. Beams lose
  nothing: the file has no beam code in it at all, and was grouped there only
  because Beam_engraver is its most conspicuous consumer.

  ONE DIVERGENCE, recorded rather than reproduced: upstream's
  stop_translation_timestep walks the queue past the entries ending NOW into a
  local it then discards. It reads a property, binds two locals and returns,
  with no side effect of any kind, so the port does not carry it.

  WHAT IS STILL GATED, and on what -- worth knowing before reading a lyrics
  page and calling it wrong:
  * TEXT SCRIPTS in a lyric score (`d8.^"melisma"') need Text_engraver
    (scripts group).
  * MELISMATA FROM SLURS need Slur_engraver, because automaticMelismas
    works by having the slur set a melismaBusyProperty. \melisma / \melismaEnd
    (the manual form) work today. Until slurs land, a slurred passage offers
    MORE syllable slots than upstream gives it, so a lyric-combine file can show
    extra syllables rather than missing ones -- the opposite of the usual
    unported-code symptom, and easy to misread.
  * The broken-line halves of Lyric_hyphen::print and
    Vowel_transition::set_spacing_rods need line breaking, as noted above.

  A SECOND PULL-FORWARD, forced by the first: lily/bezier-scheme.cc COMPLETED,
  from the long tail, under standing rule 3.

  The context-handle fix let two tablature files reach their own tie-drawing
  code for the first time, and they died on #<unported ly:bezier-extent>. The
  file is 55 upstream lines and TWO LY_DEFINEs, both one-line wrappers over
  Flower's Bezier -- which has been ported since the Flower milestone. So this
  surface was owed from that day; it simply had no caller.

  ONLY ONE OF THE TWO WAS ACTUALLY MISSING. ly:bezier-extract was already
  implemented, in Bootstrap/GeneralPrimitives.cs, landed by whoever needed it
  without the ledger row being flipped -- so that row had been OVERSTATING the
  remaining work, which is the same failure the source-locations group found
  in six other
  bindings-complete *-scheme.cc files. EntryPointClosureTests caught it here by
  ARITHMETIC: the closure moved by nine where ten were expected. That is the
  reason that test asserts a NUMBER and not merely a floor, and it is worth
  keeping in mind when scoping any other leaf binding file -- check what is
  already registered before counting the work.

  ONE LilyScheme ADDITION came with them, in the interpreter repo:
  make-rectangular, plus a narrow ComplexNumber value for it to return.
  scm/stencil.scm and scm/define-markup-commands.scm both spell the length of a
  vector as (magnitude (make-rectangular dx dy)), and magnitude/angle already
  existed in LilyScheme as REAL-only approximations. The new type is deliberately
  narrow: only make-rectangular builds one, only magnitude / angle / real-part /
  imag-part understand one, and arithmetic on one RAISES rather than silently
  dropping the imaginary part -- which is the failure mode a half-built numeric
  tower would otherwise have. complex? accepts one; number? and real? do not,
  because number? is what the arithmetic layer tests.


================================================================================
BEAMS
================================================================================

  All ten of the group's files. The eleventh, grob-pq-engraver.cc, had already
  landed with the lyrics group, so this one was one file lighter than its work
  order says.

  The rows land in seven C# files:

    least-squares.cc               Layout/LeastSquares.cs
    interval-minefield.cc          Layout/IntervalMinefield.cs
    beaming-pattern.cc             Translation/BeamingPattern.cs
    beam.cc                        Objects/Beam.cs
    beam-quanting.cc               Objects/BeamQuanting.cs
    template-engraver-for-beams.cc Translation/BeamEngravers.cs
    beam-engraver.cc               Translation/BeamEngravers.cs
    chord-tremolo-engraver.cc      Translation/BeamEngravers.cs
    auto-beam-engraver.cc          Translation/AutoBeamEngraver.cs
    beam-collision-engraver.cc     Translation/BeamCollisionEngraver.cs

  THE SCORER, AND THE ONE PLACE FAITHFULNESS NEEDED EXTRA WORK

  beam-quanting.cc is a scorer, so standing rule 2 applies: it is translated
  literally, including the several places where upstream computes a value it
  never uses (beam_width in init_instance_variables is the clearest). One point
  needed more than literal translation to STAY faithful, and it is worth
  understanding before touching this file.

  Upstream picks the winning configuration out of a std::priority_queue whose
  comparator inverts, so the top is the SMALLEST demerits. Beam quanting
  routinely produces configurations with EQUAL demerits -- symmetric music is
  the normal case, not the exotic one -- and which of those equal configurations
  comes off the queue IS the beam's final position on the page. std::priority_
  queue is not stable, and neither is System.Collections.Generic.PriorityQueue,
  but two unstable heaps do not have to break a tie the same way. Only the same
  heap does. ConfigurationHeap is therefore a direct replica of libstdc++'s
  push_heap / pop_heap / adjust_heap over a List, with upstream's comparator, so
  the sequence of pops is identical. Do not replace it with a library queue.

  A second, smaller trap in the same file: round_halfway_up is
  floor (x - 0.5) + 1.0, NOT floor (x + 0.5). Upstream says so in
  flower/libc-extension.cc and the two differ on exact .5 boundaries, which is
  precisely where a quant sits.

  BEAMING PATTERN COUNTS ARE UNSIGNED

  Beam counts in Translation/BeamingPattern.cs are uint, not int, and that is
  deliberate. beamify's third loop subtracts from a beam count:

    infos_[i].beam_count_drul_[opposite] -= max (count(i) - count(neighbor), 1u)

  guarded by count(i) >= count(neighbor). A stem with count 0 can still reach
  this, because the flag-direction CORRECTION loop assigns a non-CENTER
  direction to stems the first loop skipped. When it does, upstream computes
  0 - 1 in unsigned arithmetic and WRAPS, and every later comparison reads the
  wrapped value. Using int would quietly turn that into -1 and change which
  branch subdivide_beams takes. The port wraps where upstream wraps.

  BEAM PRINT EXTREME INDEX

  Beam::print computes

    vsize extreme = (segments[0].vertical_count_ == 0
                       ? segments[0].vertical_count_
                       : segments.back ().vertical_count_);

  and then INDEXES segments with it. vertical_count_ is a signed beam rank, and
  storing a negative one in a vsize is undefined behaviour upstream. In practice
  it evaluates to 0, because the segments are ordered by ascending vertical
  count so either the first is 0 or the last is. The port clamps the index into
  range rather than letting C# throw where C++ reads garbage: a faithful
  translation must not turn silent undefined behaviour into an exception.

  BEAM BOUNDS BEFORE LINE BREAKING

  Beam::print and Beam::calc_beam_segments both refine their common horizontal
  reference point with me->get_bound (d)->common_refpoint (...), with no null
  check. Upstream does not need one: Spanner::do_break_processing walks away
  from a spanner missing either bound, so such a spanner is never assigned to a
  system and never typeset. That guarantee lives in line breaking, which is
  line breaking's and page layout's, and the port reaches print for an
  unbounded beam -- it is what
  made tablature-slurs-with-beams and ten other files stop producing a page,
  and it is the regression the ratchet caught on this session's first sweep.
  Both walks go through Beam.CommonWithBound, which skips a missing bound. A
  beam with no bounds has no stems either, so it yields no segments and draws
  nothing, which is the same page upstream produces after calc_direction removes
  it. REVISIT WHEN LINE BREAKING AND PAGE LAYOUT LAND: the guard should
  become unnecessary.

  THE STEM-GROUP BEAM SEAM IS RETIRED

  Objects/Stem.cs carried a private BeamHelpers class holding faithful copies of
  six beam.cc statics, pulled forward because their callers landed with the
  stems group while beams were their own group. Beam.cs now owns all six under
  their upstream names, the
  Stem and StemTremolo call sites point at Beam, and BeamHelpers is deleted.

  THREE FREE FUNCTIONS THAT WERE NEVER CARRIED

  All three live in files whose ledger rows say `ported', and all three were
  found by porting a caller. Their rows stay `ported' -- the file was ported and
  the function was the gap. This is the fourth session running to find some of
  these; budget for it.

    Grob::pure_relative_y_coordinate (grob.cc) and the free function
    call_pure_function (grob-property.cc) that it needs. Beam::consider_auto_knees
    is the caller. call_pure_function has semantics worth reading: an unpure-pure
    container whose pure half is OMITTED answers through its unpure half, but a
    BARE procedure answers #f rather than being called, because calling it would
    not be pure.

    Grob::spanned_column_rank_interval (grob.cc, item.cc, spanner.cc) and
    Grob::less (grob.cc). Beam_collision_engraver::finalize walks beams and
    covered grobs in parallel in horizontal order and needs both.

    Engraver::announce_grob (inf, ctx), announce_end_grob (inf, ctx),
    announce_grob_locally_only and announce_end_grob_locally_only
    (engraver.cc). Auto_beam_engraver announces its beam LOCALLY and then again
    in the context the beam started in, which may no longer be its own by the
    time the beam ends.

  TWO LilyScheme GAPS, BOTH MEASURED

  Neither is a beam bug; both are Guile-compatibility gaps that no earlier group
  had reached. Fenced by NaryEqualityTests in the LilyScheme repo.

    eq?, eqv? and equal? are declared SCM_DEFINE (..., 0, 2, 1) in
    libguile/eq.c -- ZERO required arguments, two optional and a rest -- so they
    are N-ARY: fewer than two arguments answer #t, and longer lists compare
    ADJACENT pairs. LilyScheme fixed them at exactly two. ly:beam::calc-knee
    decides whether a beam is kneed with (apply eqv? <one stem direction per
    stem>), so EVERY beam died on wrong-number-of-args, including the four
    eighth notes of { c'8 c'8 c'8 c'8 }.

    member and assoc take an OPTIONAL equality predicate, called in the caller's
    own argument order as (predicate key element). LilyPond's
    default-auto-beam-check finds a beat setting with (assoc type sorted-alist
    <=). Guile's C assoc is two-argument; the three-argument form comes from
    SRFI-1, which the port resolves through the core binding, so the core one
    now accepts it. Scope stays WIDER than Guile's here, never narrower, which
    is the same posture the LilyScheme gap fixes recorded for #:select.

  WHAT BEAMS STILL DO NOT DO, AND WHY IT IS NOT THIS GROUP'S

  Beams draw with correct thickness and correct beam counts, and the polygon
  count matches the oracle file for file. They are close to ZERO LENGTH in most
  swept files, because a beam segment runs from one stem's x to another's and
  the port's columns are still at x = 0 in most files -- horizontal spacing
  measured 108 of 915 files placing at real x, and that has not moved. When
  line breaking and page layout land the
  real line-breaking and spacing pipeline, beam length follows without further
  work here. Nothing in this group papers over it.

  SINGLE-STEM CHORD TREMOLO BEAMS

  Chord_tremolo_engraver gives a stem its `beaming' only when a PREVIOUS stem
  exists, so the first stem of a tremolo group is beamless until the second
  arrives. In five places in the sweep the port ends up with a tremolo Beam
  holding exactly ONE stem, and that stem's beaming is '(). Upstream's
  Beam::calc_beam_segments then reads scm_car of the empty list; upstream's own
  Beam::calc_beaming, one function earlier, guards the identical data with
  `if (scm_is_pair (this_beaming))', so the port applies that same guard at the
  sibling site and the stem contributes no segments -- which is correct, since
  beaming is exactly what says where a stem's segments are.

  That is the SYMPTOM, and it is fixed. The CAUSE -- why the port produces a
  one-stem tremolo beam where upstream evidently produces a two-stem one -- was
  measured but not chased down: it shows up only in the full sweep and not when
  the file is run alone (whole-note-tremolo-direction.ly is the one that caught
  it, and it is the last regression the ratchet reported this session). The
  suspects are the tremolo engraver's span-stop handling and the sweep's known
  cross-file state leak; distinguishing them wants a session that owns tremolo
  work. Left for the chords/figured-bass group or Phase 4, recorded here
  rather than in a comment so it
  is findable.

================================================================================
TIES AND SLURS
================================================================================

Ported together, as the condensed plan proposed. They pair because they are the
same problem twice: both are SCORERS, both draw through Lookup::slur, both go
through bezier-bow.cc's closed-form shape, and slurs have to be shaped around
ties, so the second group cannot be tested properly without the first.

Eighteen ledger rows, both buckets emptied. No pull-forward from any group.

STANDING RULE 2 GOVERNS FOUR OF THESE FILES.  tie-formatting-problem.cc,
slur-scoring.cc, slur-configuration.cc and the demerit arithmetic in
tie-configuration.cc are decades-tuned heuristics. Nothing is tidied, reordered
or simplified, including the places where upstream computes a value it does not
use and the places where it does the same thing twice.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT THE ALREADY-PORTED FILES HAD NEVER CARRIED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIVE gaps in files whose ledger rows have said `ported' since the ledger was
built. All five are
the same shape, and it is the shape standing rule 4 describes: something upstream
declares was half-reproduced, and NOTHING FAILED.

THE HEADLINE ONE IS NOT A TIE OR A SLUR DEFECT AT ALL, AND IT AFFECTED EVERY
GROB IN EVERY SCORE.

  0. lily/grob.cc's CONSTRUCTOR installs four DEFAULT callbacks on any grob whose
     description does not name them:

         if (scm_is_null (get_property_data (this, "X-extent")))
           set_property (this, "X-extent", Grob::stencil_width_proc);
         if (scm_is_null (get_property_data (this, "Y-extent")))         ...
         if (scm_is_null (get_property_data (this, "vertical-skylines"))) ...
         if (scm_is_null (get_property_data (this, "horizontal-skylines"))) ...

     The port's constructor stopped after the meta/interfaces/object-callbacks
     block and installed none of them. Every one of the four procedures was
     REGISTERED and working; only the defaulting was missing, which is why no
     unbound-name warning ever appeared.

     What it cost: most grobs name X-extent and Y-extent explicitly in
     scm/define-grobs.scm, so most were unaffected. NoteHead does not name
     X-extent. So every note head in every score the port has ever engraved has
     been answering an EMPTY horizontal extent, and the empty answer was CACHED
     the first time anything asked.

     Nothing asked, for eleven groups. Horizontal spacing goes through
     NoteColumn's own extent callbacks; beams measure stems; lyrics measure
     syllables. Tie_formatting_problem is the first code in the engine to ask a
     NOTE HEAD for its width -- and it feeds the answer straight into a Skyline,
     where an empty interval turns into a NaN roof height and the whole page
     dies. That is how a defect this size stayed invisible: it needed a caller
     that both wanted the value AND could not survive a wrong one.

     Found by the ratchet, not by reading: the tie group's first sweep
     reported 23
     regressions, all of them semi-tie files, all with the same
     "Skyline building slope is not finite".

The other four are the ordinary kind -- ties and slurs are simply the only
callers anywhere in the engine, and neither existed until now.

  1. lily/misc.cc's peak_around and convex_amplifier, and misc.hh's inline
     linear_interpolate and normalize. These are the two penalty CURVES the tie
     and slur scorers charge every demerit through, plus the two interpolators.
     Now in Objects/Misc.cs.

     A fifth thing came out of the same look: Layout/StencilIntegral.cs had its
     own PRIVATE copy of linear_interpolate, associated as
     ((x2-x) y1 + (x-x1) y2) / (x2-x1) where upstream's misc.hh writes
     (x2-x)/(x2-x1) y1 + (x-x1)/(x2-x1) y2. Algebraically identical, not
     bit-for-bit identical, and upstream's stencil-integral.cc calls that very
     inline -- so the copy was the divergence. Re-pointed.

  2. lily/axis-group-interface.cc's staff_extent. One caller in the whole engine:
     Tie_formatting_problem::set_column_chord_outline, for a tie whose bound is a
     broken column. Now in Objects/AxisGroupInterface.cs.

  3. lily/grob.cc's pure_y_extent. Slur::pure_height and
     Slur::pure_outside_slur_callback are the port's first callers. Now in
     Objects/Grob.cs, reading Y-extent through the SAME minimal pure stand-in the
     rest of the port uses -- the full unpure-pure-container.cc treatment is
     line breaking's, and until it lands a grob with no pure callback answers
     its ordinary
     extent. Recorded as a divergence below.

  4. lily/pitch.cc's five alteration constants (NATURAL, FLAT, DOUBLE_FLAT,
     SHARP, DOUBLE_SHARP). Slur_score_state::get_extra_encompass_infos shifts an
     accidental's collision box by a different amount for each and tells them
     apart by comparing to these. Now on Music/Pitch.cs.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A ROW THAT NEEDED NO CODE: bezier-bow.cc WAS ALREADY WHOLE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The repeats group pulled bezier-bow.cc's "shape half" forward for \tupletSlur
and recorded that the slurs group still owed `Bezier_bow itself -- the class
that fits a curve around
encompassing points', described as the decades-tuned code the faithfulness rule
is about. That is what the file looked like in older LilyPond.

THERE IS NO SUCH CLASS IN THE PINNED 2.27.2. There is no lily/include/
bezier-bow.hh, no definition anywhere under lily/, and no reference from scm/ or
ly/. bezier-bow.cc is 132 lines and holds exactly four functions: F0_1,
slur_height, get_slur_indent_height and slur_shape -- all four of which landed
with the repeats group. The file was already complete; only the ledger row and
the notes were wrong, and they had been overstating the remaining work ever
since.
