  gregorian-ligature-engraver.cc's fix_prefix_set ends with

    fix_prefix ("pes_or_flexa", LINEA, current_set, min_set, max_set, primitive);

  -- the name says pes_or_flexa and the mask is LINEA, so LINEA is checked twice
  and PES_OR_FLEXA never. The port reproduces it exactly. Fixing it would change
  which warnings a Gregorian score emits, and parity is the job here. Recorded so
  that a future reader does not "fix" it either.

⚠ Bezier IS A CLASS IN Flower, SO UPSTREAM'S COPY-ON-ASSIGN NEEDED WRITING OUT

  vaticana-ligature.cc's flexa maker is built on

    Bezier top_curve = curve, bottom_curve = curve;

  which COPIES in C++ and would ALIAS in C#, because Flower's Bezier is a sealed
  class rather than a struct. All three names would be the same object and every
  control-point adjustment would land on all three, silently, producing a
  degenerate shape rather than an error. VaticanaLigature.CopyOf makes the copy
  explicit and is commented as load-bearing at both ends.

  This is a defect CLASS, not one site: any port of upstream code that assigns a
  Bezier by value has it. No other current site does -- checked -- but the next
  one will.

from_scm<bool> IS EXACTLY-#t, AND THIS GROUP DEPENDS ON IT

  scm_conversions<bool>::from_scm defaults its fallback to false, which makes it
  scm_is_eq (s, SCM_BOOL_T) -- NOT Scheme truth. Gregorian_ligature_engraver
  MULTIPLIES the result by a bit mask, so a truthy-but-not-#t value would have to
  come out 1 anyway; but Mensural_ligature_engraver branches on ligature-flexa,
  ligature-pes, left-down-stem, right-down-stem and right-up-stem, and there an
  unset property read as true would change every shape in the ligature. The port
  uses SchemeUtilities.ToBool (exactly-#t) throughout, never IsSchemeTrue -- the
  defect class the carry-forward pass named.

PITCH READS FALL BACK TO 0 WHERE UPSTREAM DEREFERENCES A NULL

  calc_delta_pitches, provide_context_info, Mensural_ligature_engraver's
  transform_heads and Vaticana_ligature_engraver's ambiguous-dot check all read

    unsmob<Pitch> (get_property (cause, "pitch"))->steps ()

  with no null check. Upstream segfaults if a ligature primitive has no pitch;
  the port answers 0. The case is unreachable in valid input -- rests are refused
  by acknowledge_rest and transform_heads checks note-event before it reads the
  pitch -- so this is a crash-safety choice, not a behaviour change.

WHAT THE GROUP MEASURABLY CHANGED

  Measured by disabling Mensural_ligature_engraver's registration and re-running
  the same file, which is the only honest way to attribute output to a group:

    mensural-ligatures.ly    0 -> 174 polygons, and the oracle has 174 exactly.
                             2 pages -> 1 page, and the oracle writes 1.

  The ligature shapes ARE the polygons: without the engraver the heads are drawn
  as ordinary glyphs and no connected geometry exists at all. The page count
  follows from move_related_items_to_column folding each ligature's heads into
  ONE paper column, which is most of what a ligature does to spacing.

  mensural-ligature-citations.ly went MISSING -> GLYPHS-DIFFER: it produced no
  output before this group and produces its three pages now.

⚠ A PRE-EXISTING LEDGER-LINE OVER-DRAW IS VISIBLE HERE AND IS NOT THIS GROUP'S

  mensural-ligatures.ly draws a family of ledger-line rects (height 0.23 =
  staff-line thickness 0.13 + staff-space * 0.1) that the oracle does not draw at
  all. The same rects are present WITHOUT this group's engravers -- 464 of them,
  which this group REDUCES to 368 by folding heads into shared columns. It
  belongs to whoever owns ledger lines on ancient staves, and it is recorded here
  only because a reader comparing this file against the oracle will see it first.

WARNINGS: THE ORACLE'S LOGS CANNOT SETTLE THEM

  The port emits 11 "flexa cannot have stem in the middle" warnings on
  mensural-ligatures.ly and the reference log for that file is EMPTY. That is not
  a divergence: generate-reference.sh runs the oracle with --silent, so NOT ONE of
  the 2,316 reference logs contains a `warning:' line. Whether upstream warns has
  to be settled by reading upstream, and it does warn here -- pairwise flexa
  formation leaves every second note with a previous head whose shape is
  MLP_FLEXA_END, which fails prev_brevis_shape, which is exactly that branch. The
  port's warning columns match that trace note for note.

TESTS

  Epg21Tests (11, Engine.Tests) fences the two things this group can get wrong
  invisibly: the bit masks -- asserted as the RELATIONSHIPS upstream defines them
  by, not as literals, so a renumbering cannot satisfy both sides -- and the
  registrations. AncientNotationEndToEndTests (9, LilyPort.Tests) fences the
  shapes, each fact against a control that must come out differently.


================================================================================
PAGE BREAKING CLOSE-OUT: THE SPRING SEAM AND THE STAFF-LINE MEASUREMENT
================================================================================

This pass was opened to close page breaking's ten red ratchet rows. It found
and fixed one defect in page breaking's OWN machinery, restored one hollow
branch in an already-`ported' file, and MEASURED a suite-wide defect that is
not page breaking's and
that the ten rows all sit on. Nothing was re-baselined; the floor is untouched
at 1,815 / 501 and the gate still reads ten.

DEFECT 1 -- A MUTABLE STRUCT IN A LIST: THREE SILENT LOSSES IN append_system

  Spring is a STRUCT here and upstream mutates `springs_.back ()'. C#'s
  List<T> indexer answers a COPY for a value type, so

      _springs[_springs.Count - 1].EnsureMinDistance (x);

  compiles with zero warnings, mutates a temporary and throws it away. Three
  mutations in Page_layout_problem::append_system were being discarded:

    1. the "leave room for any loose lines above this system" floor;
    2. ensure_min_distance on EVERY spring BETWEEN two staves;
    3. the whole alignment-distances block -- set_ideal_distance,
       set_min_distance and set_inverse_stretch_strength -- so a score that
       explicitly placed its staves was IGNORED OUTRIGHT.

  ⚠ THE SITES WERE INCONSISTENT, WHICH IS THE SIGNATURE OF THIS TRAP. The
  constructor's last_spring and append_prob's spring_copy both mutate-then-add
  and were always correct; append_system's three add-then-mutate and were
  always wrong. Whoever ported the file was aware of the seam in some places
  and not in others, and nothing distinguishes the two shapes by eye.

  Fixed with a ref-returning LastSpring(), which is what upstream's
  `springs_.back ()' actually means. MEASURED: 47 pages changed geometry,
  ZERO verdicts changed, zero regressions.

  ⚠ THIS IS THE ANCIENT-NOTATION Bezier FINDING IN THE MIRROR, and the pair
  should be read
  as one rule: EVERY C++ VALUE/REFERENCE DECISION IS A PORT DECISION. There a
  C++ value was aliased by a C# reference; here a C++ reference was copied by a
  C# value. Both are silent and neither shows up in a diff against upstream.

  THE CLASS WAS SWEPT, not just the site. Skyline and SkylinePair are sealed
  CLASSES; beginLineHeights/midLineHeights, _dimensionCache and _pedalInfo are
  ARRAYS, whose element access is a genuine ref for value types; DrulArray's
  indexer answers by value but its only mutable-struct use is a read. The
  List<Spring> in PageLayoutProblem is the only instance in the engine.

DEFECT 2 -- Paper_column::break_align_width WAS HOLLOW ON ITS ELSE BRANCH

  Upstream walks the requested align symbols and, for anything that is not the
  literal `break-alignment', calls
  Break_alignment_interface::find_nonempty_break_align_group. That entire else
  branch was ABSENT: the port's loop matched only `break-alignment' and skipped
  every named group before it. A StaffSymbol asks with
  (staff-bar break-alignment), so `staff-bar' was never consulted.

  ⚠ THE FUNCTION IT SHOULD CALL WAS ALREADY PORTED -- line breaking landed
  find_nonempty_break_align_group in BreakAlignInterface.cs. This caller was
  written while it did not exist and NOTHING RE-CHECKED IT once it did. That is
  the ratchet fixes' own rule (a named absence stops being true the moment its
  owner lands) meeting page breaking's (a ported type with a missing call is
  worse than an
  unported type), in a file whose ledger row has read `ported' throughout.

  Restored faithfully. MEASURED HONESTLY: it changed NOTHING in the sweep --
  zero pages, zero verdicts -- because the BreakAlignment it would search has
  no non-empty groups to find in the first place (see the finding below). It is
  correct, it is inert today, and it is recorded as both.

THE FINDING THIS SESSION IS REALLY ABOUT -- COLLAPSED STAFF LINES, SUITE-WIDE

  Measured over the whole closing sweep, counting only HORIZONTAL <line>
  elements (a vertical bar line or stem has x1 == x2 and must not be counted --
  the first measurement of this made exactly that mistake and read 73.7%):

                    horizontal <line>   collapsed (<0.5)   pages affected
      oracle              32,254          1,568  ( 4.9%)         145
      port                22,385         16,664  (74.4%)       1,296

  THREE QUARTERS OF THE PORT'S STAFF LINES ARE DRAWN WITH NO LENGTH. They come
  out as x1="0.0500" x2="-0.0500" -- running BACKWARDS, the full width being the
  line thickness -- where the oracle draws, e.g., x1="0.0500" x2="80.7122".

  ⚠ THE COMPARATOR HAS NEVER REPORTED THIS AND STRUCTURALLY CANNOT. It grades
  glyph inventory and position; a staff line is a <line>, and a page whose
  staff lines are collapsed holds the same glyphs in the same order. This is
  the MIDI group's timing blindness in a second dimension, and the general
  rule it
  argues for is now stated twice: A GREEN RATCHET IS EVIDENCE ABOUT WHAT THE
  COMPARATOR GRADES AND ABOUT NOTHING ELSE.

  Traced two levels. Staff_symbol::print is a faithful translation and its
  span_points arrive as exactly (0,0) -- the initial value, untouched by both
  branches. The BREAKALIGN branch is the one that fires, and
  Paper_column::break_align_width answers a POINT interval because (a) the
  BreakAlignment's extent is empty and (b) the bound column's coordinate
  relative to its system is 0. Defect 2 above is on that path and does not
  change it.

  IT IS NOT LINE BREAKING ALONE, MEASURED: unbroken single-line scores are
  62.9% collapsed and broken ones 81.9%. Line breaking worsens it; it does not
  cause it.

  OWNER: NOT PAGE BREAKING. ly:break-alignment-interface::calc-positioning-done
  is
  registered (LineBreakingCallbacks) and reached, and the sweep's demand list asks for
  no break-align name at all -- so this is not a missing binding but the
  break-alignment machinery producing empty extents over columns that are still
  at zero, which is line-breaking/horizontal-spacing ground. It is the same
  "unplaced columns" family the ratchet fixes recorded for slashed-beam and
  could not guard.

WHAT THIS SAYS ABOUT THE TEN

  All ten regressed rows carry collapsed staff lines. The ten are one shape --
  the oracle writes one page, the port writes two, so the port's <base>-1.svg
  leaves the reference <base>.svg reading MISSING although the music drew --
  and they are sitting on a defect that touches 1,296 pages. Page breaking's
  close-out
  called them "not page-breaking bugs" and handed them to their real owners on
  the strength of per-file geometry; this session's suite-wide measurement is
  the stronger form of the same argument.

TESTS

  SpringStorageTests (3, Engine.Tests) fences the storage seam in BOTH
  directions: mutating a stored Spring through the List indexer must be
  DISCARDED, and through a span reference must be KEPT. The pair is the point --
  asserting only the ref case would pass against an indexer that started
  handing back references, and asserting only the copy case would pass against
  Spring quietly becoming a class. The third fences the alignment-distances
  trio together, because a spring that kept its minimum but lost its stretch
  strength would still drift.

  ⚠ OWED AND NOT WRITTEN: an end-to-end fence that alignment-distances actually
  places staves where a score asks. It is owed rather than skipped because the
  observable it needs -- vertical separation between two staves -- is carried in
  the SVG's enclosing translate transforms rather than in the line elements, and
  because every such measurement today runs over collapsed staff lines. It
  should be written once the finding above is closed, not before, or it fences
  broken geometry.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE COLLAPSED-STAFF-LINE DEFECT, FIXED AT ITS ROOT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The page-breaking close-out measured it: 74.4% of the port's horizontal
  staff lines were
  drawn with no length (16,664 of 22,385 across 1,296 pages; oracle 4.9%).
  After this session: 1.4%, on 103 pages against the oracle's 145. All the
  fixes are faithful restorations; not one is new design.

  THE ROOT. Separation_item::boxes read an item's ORDINARY Y extent where
  upstream reads `il->pure_y_extent (ycommon, 0, INT_MAX)`. Boxes are built
  during horizontal spacing, BEFORE line breaking, and the ordinary read
  dragged the ordinary side-position chain in, which read the ordinary
  vertical-skylines, which asked the StaffSymbol for its STENCIL -- computed
  over columns still at x = 0, CACHED in the property alist, inherited by the
  broken piece through Clone, and DRAWN. Print never ran twice; the collapsed
  line was a stale cache, not a wrong computation.

  THE CLASS, SWEPT. Every "answers the ordinary extent, pending line
  breaking" note became a live bug the day line breaking landed, and nothing
  re-checked them -- the
  stale-named-absence rule at project scale. Retired this session (discharge
  notes inline at each record):

    * Separation_item::boxes                 pure_y_extent (the root)
    * Side_position_interface::aligned_side  support skylines, support Y
                                             coordinate, add-stem-support all
                                             maybe-pure; BOTH ReadSkylinePair
                                             constructor-default stand-ins are
                                             ordinary-branch only now
    * Accidental_placement::build_heads_skyline   pure_y_extent
    * Self_alignment_interface::aligned_on_self   maybe_pure_extent
    * Spacing_interface::skylines            pure_relative_y_coordinate
    * Paper_column::minimum_distance         the conditional_skyline merge

  THE DISCONNECTED MIDDLE. Paper_column_engraver was missing THREE
  acknowledgers (staff_spacing, note_spacing, break_alignment). Nothing ever
  wrote `spacing-wishes' onto any column, so every wish-based correction in
  Spacing_spanner has been DEAD since horizontal spacing landed -- reading
  empty sets, no
  diagnostic -- and no column carried a `break-alignment' object, so
  break_align_width answered a POINT at the column's coordinate for every
  staff-line end in the suite. (That is why close-out II's restored else
  branch "changed nothing": it searched an object no column carried.) With
  the wishes connected, two hollow interiors became reachable and were ported
  in full: Note_spacing::stem_dir_correction (knee_correction included) and
  Staff_spacing::optical_correction, both behind stale absence notes whose
  callees had long since landed.

  THE SPRING TRAP, THIRD INSTANCE. Page_layout_problem's
  alter_spring_from_spacing_spec took its Spring -- a struct -- BY VALUE, so
  no spacing spec ever reached any page spring: every system sat at the
  skyline MINIMUM instead of basic-distance (break.ly: 4.3 between systems
  where the oracle has 8.0; exact after the fix). Upstream passes Spring*;
  the port now passes `ref Spring'. Close-out II's List<Spring> fix was the
  same trap through an indexer; the ancient-notation Bezier was its mirror.
  The class was
  re-swept for by-value Spring parameters: NoteSpacing.GetSpacing is
  value-in/value-out like upstream, AppendSystem/AppendProb mutate-then-store,
  SpaceableGrob.AddSpring stores a copy upstream also stores. No other
  mutating by-value site remains.

  MEASURED, AGAINST THE COMMITTED FLOOR (nothing re-baselined):
    sweep 1 (root + side-position + acknowledgers): 9 regressed / 21 improved
      -- THE GATE WENT 10 -> 9 BY PORTING (script-custom-definition recovered)
    sweep 2 (+ stem_dir/optical/accidental-placement): 9 / 84
    closing sweep (+ spring-by-ref, self-alignment, spacing-interface,
      minimum-distance): see STATUS_lilyport_stafflines_2026-08-11.txt

  TESTS. StaffLinesEndToEndTests (4, CodeBrix.LilyPort.Tests): staff lines
  span the system UNDER AN OUTSIDE-STAFF MARKUP (the markup is the trigger
  for the poisoned-cache path), against an \omit StaffSymbol control that
  must draw NO horizontal lines; and two systems sit at basic-distance
  (12 - 4 = 8, derivable from ly/paper-defaults-init.ly), against a
  basic-distance = 20 override that must answer 16 -- the pair that fences
  the by-value Spring defect, because a port that ignores the spec answers
  the default pair identically.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STAFF-LINE FOLLOW-UP -- TWO OF THE NINE RED ROWS' FAMILIES CLOSED
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Jeremy GO'd the ssaattbb leak hunt and the TimeSignature diagnosis. Both
  found single defects; neither is what its symptom suggested.

  THE MUSIC-FONT ADVANCE WAS FIFTY TIMES TOO LARGE. The
  time-signature-grob-* family's "width explosion" (the port broke 3
  oracle systems into 18) was not TimeSignature machinery at all:
  OpenTypeFontMetric.IndexedAdvance multiplied the raw hmtx value by the
  POINT constant -- a unit from a different domain -- where the SVG backend
  draws the same outlines by FontScaling over the em. Every composed run in
  the MUSIC font (fetaText: \number, \dynamic, figured bass, and time
  signatures whose spec prints numerals as a STRING) advanced its pen 50x
  per character; `(1/2 . 3/4)' prints "1/2" over "3/4", so three characters
  spanned sixty staff spaces and line breaking exploded. Measured before and
  after with a probe: advance('4') 80.0 -> 1.6000, exactly the glyph's box
  width. Single-character runs never noticed, which is why figured bass
  looked fine the day it landed. All four time-signature-grob-* files now
  produce the oracle's ONE page. Fenced by MusicFontAdvanceTests as a
  RELATIONSHIP (advance/box-width in a band around 1), because the defect
  was a unit-domain mixup, not a wrong number.

  THE NINTH PER-FILE LEAK IS THE PARSER'S BASE SCOPE ITSELF. Bisecting the
  sweep against ssaattbb-template-with-all-staves (11 rounds, the recorded
  recipe) converged on satb-template-with-changed-instrument-names -- and
  the mechanism is the RestoreDefaults LIMIT recorded since the text-interface
  group: "a
  variable a file INVENTS is left in place, because the scope has no
  unbind". The built-in vocal templates read OPTIONAL variables by name
  (Time, TwoVoicesPerStaff, instrument names), so the satb file's leftover
  `Time = { s1 \break s1 }' forced a line break inside every LATER template
  in the sweep: two pages in the sweep, one page alone. CLOSED generally,
  not per-variable: LilyParserSession.SnapshotToplevelScope records the base
  scope after the init layer (names, VARIABLE IDENTITIES, values) and
  RestoreToplevelScope plays it back per file -- upstream's
  one-parser-per-file semantics. Variable identity matters: psyntax captures
  variables by object, so the restore re-attaches the SNAPSHOT'S variable
  rather than minting a fresh one. The old LIMIT notes are retired. Fenced
  by SessionLeakEndToEndTests: a reader file whose score DOUBLES its system
  count if the canary variable is visible, paired with a same-file control
  that must double -- the templates' mechanism in miniature.

================================================================================
LONG TAIL & ENTRY-POINT CLOSURE.  GATE G3 CLOSED.
================================================================================

  737 of 737 entry points are implemented-or-N/A, the ledger closes at
  419 ported / 0 owed / 29 no-port, and no name in entry-points.tsv answers
  from a stub any more.  36 real implementations plus 17 D25 N/A bindings.

  TWO OF THE "LONG TAIL" WERE LIVE ON THE MAIN PATH, not leaves.  This is
  standing trap 7 twice over, and it is why the closure was worth doing as
  porting rather than as bookkeeping:

    ly:make-rand-seed / ly:set-rand-seed (lily-random.cc).  scm/lily.scm's
    randomize-rand-seed calls BOTH on every single run, before anything is
    engraved.  As stubs the first answered the inert UnportedValue and the
    second silently discarded it, so the C++-side generator was never seeded
    at all.  Ported with an MT19937 (std::mt19937 is that generator; .NET has
    none) in Bootstrap/RandomPrimitives.cs.

    ly:event? (music-scheme.cc).  A TYPE PREDICATE over a type the port has
    had from the start, and -- because upstream declares it with LY_DEFINE
    rather
    than as a smob type_p_name_ -- it got the ORDINARY stub, which answers the
    inert UnportedValue.  An UnportedValue is TRUTHY, so
    ly-syntax-constructors.scm's `(cond ((ly:event? item) (ly:set-origin!
    item)) ...)' had been taking the WRONG branch, and lily.scm's
    type-name-alist could not name a "post-event" in an error message.

  THE SAME LY_DEFINE-NOT-TYPE-PREDICATE TRAP caught ly:otf-font? and
  ly:pango-font? as well.  Both answered YES to every value handed to them.
  ly:pango-font? is now a constant #f and that IS its implementation: D13/D23
  replace Pango with the port's own font layer, so no value can be one.  It
  is deliberately NOT filed N/A -- an N/A binding THROWS, and lily.scm's
  type-name-alist calls every predicate in it to name a bad argument.

  DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES, all four recorded here rather than left to be
  re-discovered:

    ly:duration::less?  Upstream's Duration::less_p unsmobs WITHOUT asserting
    and dereferences the result, so a non-duration argument is undefined
    behaviour there.  The port raises a wrong-type error instead.
    Reproducing a null dereference is not fidelity.

    ly:make-tmpfile-name.  MT19937 itself is exact (the algorithm is fully
    specified), but std::uniform_int_distribution's reduction to a bounded
    range is IMPLEMENTATION-DEFINED.  Same seed gives the same generator
    sequence, not necessarily the same bounded draw.  The only consumer is a
    temp file name's hex suffix -- random by design, never in engraved output.

    ly:translate-cpp-warning-scheme.  Upstream wraps the result in _(), a
    gettext lookup.  D17 defers i18n, so the port translates nothing and
    passes the string through.  The printf->format rewrite itself is exact.

    ly:usage.  Upstream prints the `lilypond' binary's option table.  D14
    makes Lily.Shell the port's public command line and the batch runner
    internal-only, so there is no such option table; the port prints its OWN
    usage, from Bootstrap/UsageText.cs, which Lily.Shell's `usage' command
    prints too.  One string, two callers.  A usage message is a statement
    about THIS program, and reproducing a false one verbatim is worse than
    diverging.

  THE THREE GROB DEBUG CALLBACKS ARE PORTED, NOT STUBBED OUT.
  ly:set-grob-modification-callback, ly:set-property-cache-callback and
  ly:set-grob-creation-callback all go through upstream's
  check_debug_callback, which is compiled two ways: only an
  --enable-checking build stores the procedure, and every ordinary build --
  which is what the pinned oracle binary is -- warns "To use grob debug
  callbacks, configure with --enable-checking" and stores '().  The call
  sites are themselves inside `if constexpr (CHECKING)'.  So warning and
  refusing IS upstream's answer for the build the port corresponds to.

  ly:paper-column::print is registered and fully drawn (rank, moment, the
  blue ideal-distance and red minimum-distance arrows, one shared arrow
  counter across BOTH loops as upstream's own comment demands) even though
  nothing reaches it: scm/define-grobs.scm carries
  `(stencil . ,ly:paper-column::print)' COMMENTED OUT on PaperColumn and
  NonMusicalPaperColumn both.  It is the override a user reaches for when
  debugging spacing, and a name the Scheme can name must not answer with the
  inert placeholder.  NOTE: plan-2 recorded this as "demanded ~163x/sweep";
  that figure is stale -- the references are commented out and it is reached
  zero times.

  ONE DUPLICATE ALGORITHM REMOVED.  Staff_symbol_referencer::staff_radius had
  been carried as a private copy inside Objects/Stem.cs.  It now lives in
  Objects/StaffSymbolReferencer.cs, its real home, and Stem delegates -- two
  implementations of one upstream function is the shape standing trap 11
  records, where a fix applied to one copy never reaches the other.

  ONE NEW SEAM.  ly:lily-lexer? tests Origins/ILilyLexer, an empty marker
  interface the Parsing assembly's ModalScanner implements -- the same shape
  ly:lily-parser? already used for ILilyParser, and necessary for the same
  reason: Parsing references the Engine and not the other way round.  The
  interface is deliberately memberless; nothing in the vendored Scheme calls
  a lexer method.

  ACCUMULATIVE PROGRAM OPTIONS ARE NOW REAL.  ly:append-to-option needed
  them, and honouring #:accumulative? in ly:add-option was the other half:
  the vendored lily.scm reads (object-property key
  'program-option-accumulative?) to choose between ly:append-to-option and
  ly:set-option, so leaving that object property unset routed every
  accumulative option to the wrong binding.  Values are STORED reversed and
  reversed again by ly:get-option, which is upstream's own arrangement.
  ly:set-option now warns and changes nothing when given an accumulative
  option, as upstream does.

  ONE USE OF Unsafe.Unbox IN THE WHOLE ENGINE, and it is standing trap 9's.
  ly:spring-set-inverse-compress-strength! and its stretch twin mutate the
  CALLER's spring in place and return a copy (upstream mutates the smob it
  was handed, then answers smobbed_copy ()).  Spring is a struct, so the
  value Scheme holds is a box; unboxing into a local would mutate a copy and
  leave the caller's spring untouched, silently.  Unsafe.Unbox takes a
  reference INTO the box, which is the faithful equivalent of upstream's
  pointer.  Fenced by Epg23Tests as a relationship: the caller's spring
  changes AND a different object comes back.

================================================================================
STICKY SPANNER BOUNDS.  THE LONG TAIL'S TWO RED RATCHET ROWS, CLOSED.
================================================================================

  Two defects, both upstream of the place the long tail's close-out predicted,
  and
  both found by measuring rather than by re-reading the predicted function.

  (1) Spanner::get_bound HAD NO STICKY FALLBACK.  Objects/Spanner.cs answered
  its own backing field and nothing else.  Upstream (lily/spanner.cc:146-172)
  falls back to the sticky HOST's bound whenever the spanner carries
  sticky-grob-interface and has none of its own, and its comment says why the
  fallback lives at RETRIEVAL time: there is no point in the engraver cycle at
  which the Spanner_tracking_engraver could reliably set the bounds, because
  the engravers looking after the host may set its right bound -- and even its
  left bound -- as late as their finalize hook, and nothing may depend on
  engraver order.

  So EVERY sticky SPANNER in the port carried two null bounds: a Footnote on a
  beam or a hairpin, a BalloonText on a spanner, a Parentheses around one.
  With null bounds a spanner fails break processing, never reaches any
  system's all-elements, and is simply gone.  Nothing warned.  Sticky ITEMS --
  a footnote on a note head -- were unaffected, which is exactly what made the
  defect hard to see: footnotes worked, some of them.

  Engraver.MakeSticky's own doc comment asserted the mechanism ("A sticky
  spanner takes its bounds from its host implicitly") that was never
  implemented -- standing trap 15a's shape, a port comment describing upstream
  correctly while the port does something else.  The comment is now true.

  (2) grob_2D_less READ X-offset AS A C# double ONLY.  Objects/SystemGrob.cs
  tested `GetProperty("X-offset") is double', where upstream writes
  from_scm<double> (get_property (s, "X-offset"), 0.0) -- which accepts ANY
  Scheme number.  A footnote's X-offset is copied straight off the
  \footnote #'(1 . 1) pair by grob::calc-property-by-copy and arrives as an
  EXACT INTEGER, so every footnote spanner in the suite read as "not offset to
  the right" and sorted at its LEFT column rank instead of its RIGHT one.  Now
  read through SchemeConvert.ToDouble(value, 0.0), which IS from_scm<double>.

  This is the numeric-tower half of standing trap 7: a value that is a number
  in Scheme need not be a C# double, and an `is double' test fails SILENTLY
  and plausibly -- it answers "no offset", which is a legal state.  Sweep for
  the pattern when reading any numeric grob property.

  (3) THE SAME PATTERN IN A PREDICATE, WHICH IS WORSE (2026-08-14, PARITY 3).
  ly:dimension? had been ported as `a[0] is double || a[0] is long || a[0] is
  int' where upstream's ENTIRE body is `return scm_number_p (d);'
  (lily/general-scheme.cc).  A reader that mistypes a value gets one value
  wrong; a PREDICATE that mistypes gates the type check for every property
  declared with it, and define-grob-properties.scm declares a long list that
  way -- baseline-skip, staff-space, beam-thickness, beam-width,
  auto-knee-gap, collapse-height, gap, height, height-limit, kern,
  ledger-extra and more.  Exact rationals, bignums and complex were all
  refused, the assignment was DROPPED, and the property silently kept its
  previous value.

  What made it visible at all was \magnifyStaff, which scales every grob's
  baseline-skip, word-space and space-alist by its factor: the suite calls it
  with #3/4, #5/4 and #1/2, so 3 x 3/4 = 9/4 -- an exact rational -- and the
  scaling was discarded on 2,243 assignments across the regression suite.  The
  feature was partly dead and the only evidence was a programming error.

  Read off the ORACLE before the change (rule 35): pinned 2.27.2 renders
  baseline-skip = #9/4 byte-identically to #2.25 and both differently from the
  unoverridden default, while the port rendered #9/4 identically to the
  DEFAULT.  Now `SchemeNumber.IsNumber', which is scm_number_p's exact
  analogue (fixnum, bignum, ratio, real, complex).

  Fences: GeneralPrimitivesTests (Engine -- ly:dimension? must agree with
  number? across the whole tower AND on non-numbers, the second half being the
  control), DimensionPropertyEndToEndTests (LilyPort -- the three-way render
  relationship read off the oracle above).

  WHY THE SIBLING FILES' GREEN WAS NOT EVIDENCE.  The long-tail close recorded
  that
  footnote-auto-numbering and footnote-auto-numbering-page-reset "now render
  and PASS their numbering assertions", and reasoned from that to a defect in
  the vertical tie-break.  Both files DO carry spanner footnotes (a hairpin
  and a beam, with a simultaneous-numbering assertion on each), and both
  passed because those footnotes did not exist to be numbered: the assertion
  is a grob property, so a grob that never survives never asserts.  Their
  green was the absence, not the machinery.  Grob::vertical_less itself was
  faithful all along, as was grob_2D_less's structure.

  Fences: SpannerTests (4, Engine -- each end inherits the host's SAME end, an
  own bound still wins, the inherited column-rank interval, and a control
  spanner WITHOUT the interface that must still answer null) and
  StickyFootnoteEndToEndTests (2, LilyPort -- a footnote on a beam reaches the
  page against a bare control and a note-head control; and the numbering order
  beams-before-hairpins, whose control asserts the interleaving the X-offset
  defect produced and must fail to render).

================================================================================
CONTEXT LISTENER ORDER, AND PANGO FONT-SIZE QUANTIZATION
================================================================================

TWO DEFECTS, BOTH ORDERING/QUANTIZATION IN A CHAIN THE PORT HAD REPRODUCED
STRUCTURALLY AND GOT WRONG NUMERICALLY OR TEMPORALLY.

  (1) THE TWO DISPATCHERS WERE WIRED IN THE WRONG ORDER.  Context's constructor
  called EventsBelow.RegisterAsListener(EventSource) BEFORE registering the five
  listeners every context answers to (CreateContext, RemoveContext, ChangeParent,
  SetProperty, UnsetProperty).  Upstream registers them the other way round in
  both places it wires a context -- lily/context.cc:375-395 and
  lily/global-context.cc:50-55 -- under the comment "We want to be the first
  ones to hear our own events.  Therefore, wait before registering
  events_below_".

  A Dispatcher serves listeners in increasing priority and priority IS
  registration order, so the two orders are not equivalent: with events_below_
  first, an event broadcast AT a context reaches every OUTSIDE listener before
  the context itself has acted on it.  CreateContext is the event that CREATES a
  context, and Lyric_combine_music_iterator listens for it on the top context
  for exactly one reason, stated in its own comment: "Wait for a Create_context
  event.  If this isn't done, lyrics can be delayed when voices are created
  implicitly" (lily/lyric-combine-music-iterator.cc:233-239).  Relayed early,
  CheckNewContext ran a FindVoice that could not yet succeed.

  The consequence was NOT confined to ordering.  The iterator binds to a voice
  in order to hear its melodic events (set_busy), and start_new_syllable refuses
  to set a syllable until busy_moment_ has caught up with now.  Binding one
  timestep late therefore DROPPED THE FIRST SYLLABLE of every stanza whose voice
  did not already exist when the lyric iterator ran create_contexts, and shifted
  the rest one note late.  On the MIDI side it also announced the Lyrics
  context's audio staff a timestep after every staff and voice, which is what
  put all lyrics tracks after all music tracks -- G2's last open row.

  RegisterContextListeners is now called from the constructor and is idempotent;
  the pre-existing explicit calls (Context.CreateContextFromEvent, GlobalContext)
  are now no-ops and are kept as documentation of where upstream does the work.

  MEASURED: satb-template-with-keyboard-reduction.midi went from a dropped
  syllable per voice and grouped tracks to BYTE-IDENTICAL to the oracle, and the
  MIDI scoreboard closed at 90/90.  Layout gained four recovered syllables on
  that file (23 -> 27 text elements against the oracle's 28).

  (2) THE PANGO SIZE QUANTUM WAS MISSING.  lily/font-select.cc:215-216 does not
  hand Pango the size it computed:

      int pango_size = static_cast<int> (std::lround (requested_size * PANGO_SCALE));
      pango_font_description_set_size (description, pango_size);

  PANGO_SCALE is 1024 and a PangoFontDescription's size is an int, so every text
  size upstream uses is a whole number of 1/1024ths, and
  pango_font_description_to_string writes it to THREE DECIMALS.  That string is
  the only route the size takes to the backend, which parses it back out with
  output-svg.scm's own regular expression and divides by output-scale to write
  font-size.  The port carried the exact real all the way through, so it emitted
  1.7461 where the oracle emits 1.7459, 2.4694 against 2.4697, and so on.

  Fixed in FontInterface.QuantizeToPangoUnits (lround semantics -- halves AWAY
  from zero, which Math.Round's default banker's rounding does not give) plus a
  three-decimal DescriptionString.  MEASURED against the pinned oracle over 31
  font sizes from step -24 to +24: all 31 reproduce, and the suite's text-run
  inventory differences fell from 558 pages differing by a text run ALONE to 73.

  NOT DONE, and recorded rather than fixed: upstream routes the fetaText
  encoding through the Pango branch (font-select.cc's "else // latin1,
  fetaText", with requested_size *= point_constant first), where the port's
  IsMusicEncoding groups fetaText with fetaMusic and takes the design-size
  route.  Nothing in this session's measurements turned on it.

A HOST-DEPENDENCE IN THE ORACLE, NOT A PORT DEFECT (measured, no code change).
  ly/paper-defaults-init.ly:170-180 makes the SVG backend name the GENERIC font
  families:

      property-defaults.fonts.serif =
        #(if (eq? 'svg (ly:get-option 'backend)) "serif" "LilyPond Serif")

  so under the SVG backend Pango resolves "serif"/"sans"/"monospace" through the
  HOST's fontconfig rather than through LilyPond's own "LilyPond Serif" alias.
  On this machine that answers Noto Serif / Noto Sans / Noto Sans Mono, and the
  committed reference corpus carries Noto metrics for every text run: measured
  glyph-by-glyph, the oracle's x/X/g/o ink boxes and advances match Noto exactly
  (x [0,536] w 578, X [0,714] w 660, g [-240,567] w 538, o [-10,546] w 577) and
  do not match C059.  The port resolves the same generic names to its own
  vendored faces (D23), which are byte-identical to the oracle's bundled ones,
  and its extents are the exact ink of those faces.  Re-running the oracle with
  the generic families pinned to the bundled fonts reproduces C059's real
  metrics, i.e. the port's numbers.  This is the ~6% text-extent gap; it is a
  yardstick question, not an engine one.

================================================================================
AN ORIGIN IS AN Input, NOT THE PARSER'S OWN SourceSpan
================================================================================

FIXED at PARITY 5 (2026-08-14).  Upstream has exactly ONE location type: `@$` is
an Input, set_spot takes an Input, Music::origin answers one, and origin is
DECLARED `,ly:input-location?` in define-music-properties.scm.  Here the parser's
location is Parsing/Driver/SourceSpan, a struct the Engine has never heard of, so
the two are different types and every grammar action that stamps an origin owes
the conversion.

Eight actions stamped the RAW span: construct-chord-elements' per-element stamp
(parser.yy:4752), the \context block (954), both \book forms (1027, 1122),
\score (1205) and three \paper / \layout rules (1389, 1408).  The type check
then failed on every music object the parser built and the property was
DISCARDED, so Music::origin answered nothing and a diagnostic about a note could
not say where the note was.

MEASURED: 3,263 of the port's 4,065 diagnostic lines across the regression suite
were this one defect -- 80% of "the port says many times more than the oracle".
The diagnostics gate went 1,830 -> 1,876 of 2,146 MATCH and EXTRA 287 -> 241,
and the port's own diagnostic output fell 4,065 -> 802 lines.  It moved ZERO
comparator rows, measured by a sweep of its own (point-and-click is off in the
corpus), which is exactly why it survived this long: trap 1a, the ungraded gap.

The seam is IParserHost.SchemeLocation, deliberately NOT SetMusicSpot -- the
grammar BODY rules write set_spot (@$) with no override_input indirection, and
only the epilogue's loc_on_copy forms take the override.  Two readers that had
been written against the raw span were converted with it
(ParserActionHelpers.OriginSpan, which now reads the Input back into a span for
MakeSyntax, and MusicWarning, which now warns THROUGH the Input so the offending
line is quoted).

Fences: OriginIsAnInputEndToEndTests -- ly:input-location? is asked about the
sequential music, the note inside it, an event chord and the chord's own notes,
with a bare ly:make-music as the control that must answer #f (trap 9: a stub
predicate answering #t to everything would pass every other row).  Four
rule-action fences that had asserted BeOfType<SourceSpan>() -- the defect
recorded as the contract -- were restated, and RuleActionRag6's chord-element
fence now asserts the IDENTITY of the origin the conversion returned.

================================================================================
TEXT ADVANCES ARE KERNED
================================================================================

FIXED.  TextFontMetric.TextStencil now measures the SHAPED advance sum: the raw
hmtx advance of each glyph plus the GPOS kern feature's pair adjustment between
adjacent glyphs of a run.  Upstream takes the X extent from Pango's LOGICAL rect
(lily/pango-font.cc's pango_item_string_stencil), and Pango has SHAPED the run
by then -- so that width includes the font's kerning; summing raw advances was
never larger by accident, it was larger by exactly the kerning (trap 6f).

The reader is Fonts/KerningTable.cs (new-in-family), and it mirrors HarfBuzz's
behaviour rather than an arithmetic that looks equivalent:

  * The lookups applied are the ones the GPOS kern FEATURE names for the
    default script (DFLT, then latn, then the first declared), in lookup-list
    order; adjustments ACCUMULATE across lookups.
  * Within a lookup, subtables are tried in order and the FIRST that applies
    wins.  A PairPos Format 1 subtable applies when its coverage holds the left
    glyph AND its pair set holds the right glyph; a Format 2 subtable applies
    whenever its coverage holds the left glyph, EVEN when the class pair's
    value is zero -- HarfBuzz stops there, and so does the reader.
  * Extension lookups (type 9) are unwrapped.
  * The legacy kern table (format 0, horizontal, non-cross-stream) is read ONLY
    when GPOS names no kern feature -- HarfBuzz's own gate.
  * Device-table adjustments are not applied: they are per-ppem hinting data,
    and the backend's output is scalable SVG where Pango applies none either.
  * A face change ends the run -- Pango itemizes runs per font and never kerns
    across two of them (TextStencil's previousFace check).

MEASURED across the 24 vendored faces (independently, fontTools 4.57.0):
every face that kerns at all does it with ONE plain type-2 lookup
of PairPos FORMAT 1 subtables, ValueFormat1=XAdvance, ValueFormat2=0, flag 0,
and names the SAME lookups from every script it declares.  The monospace
quartets (NimbusMonoPS, TeX Gyre Cursor) carry no kerning; NO vendored face
carries a legacy kern table.  The wider surface (Format 2, Extension, legacy
kern) is for faces the D23 chain may grow, parsed by the spec.

HAND-CHECKED against the pinned oracle at font-size 24 in C059-Roman -- the
shaped sum lands inside the same size-quantum band as the no-kern-pairs
control, where the raw sum missed by up to 0.48 em:

    "iiiiii"             oracle 1.8915  raw 1.8900  shaped 1.8900  (control)
    "AVAVAV"             oracle 3.8499  raw 4.3320  shaped 3.8520
    "To Wo Yo"           oracle 4.2360  raw 4.4080  shaped 4.2370
    "LilyPond v2.27.2"   oracle 7.7299  raw 7.7810  shaped 7.7280

Fences: KerningTableTests (6) -- pair values hand-read from the font's own GPOS
bytes (kern(A,V)=-96, kern(L,i)=+4 -- positive pairs exist), the monospace and
no-kern-pairs controls, and the tagline's oracle-side relationship.  The A/B
was run both ways: with the application disabled exactly the two metric-level
fences fail and every control holds.

KNOWN REMAINING DIVERGENCE, deliberate: kerning only, NOT full shaping.  No
GSUB substitution (ligatures), no mark attachment, no per-script feature
selection beyond the default LangSys.  If ligature substitution ever matters it
shows as a glyph-inventory difference and gets its own ruling (the kerning
GO's scope note).

A SECOND DIVERGENCE FOUND THE SAME WAY -- RULED (D31): when no
available face covers a code point, the port always writes the <text> element
and draws .notdef -- D23's tofu -- while Pango drops the run entirely, so the
pinned oracle emits NOTHING.  Two regression files turn on it
(markup-diacritic-marks asks for "Linux Libertine O, serif" and Hebrew;
pdf-copy-paste asks for "Noto Serif JP" and Japanese), and under the pinned
corpus neither font is reachable by design.  JEREMY RULED TOFU STANDS: the port
keeps its provided fonts and makes a coverage gap OBVIOUS.  The two pages are a
permanent, documented divergence, rebaselined with the ruling recorded in
pass-manifest-decisions.tsv; they cannot reach MATCH while the ruling stands.

D47 (Jeremy, 2026-08-18) -- THE DEFERRED QUESTION IS ANSWERED: NO CJK, HEBREW,
ARABIC OR JAPANESE SUPPORT FOR NOW.  D31 left "would this port ever cover
CJK/Hebrew/Arabic/Japanese" as a deferred decision; Jeremy ruled it on
2026-08-18: those scripts are UNSUPPORTED at this time -- no optional font
packages are built and no coverage is promised.  What already renders keeps
rendering: tofu per D31 for a code point no bundled face covers (the gap
deliberately obvious, the missing-glyph warning intact), and a DOCUMENT that
supplies its own font (R16) can genuinely cover these scripts today.  Future
coverage happens only as work Jeremy SCHEDULES -- especially if a user request
is made -- and that work is exactly the event the g1-skip-list.tsv
revisit-condition column has always waited for (the two D31 originals and
R8(A)'s six rows).  Measured the same day: the 19 generated documentation
files contain ZERO characters from any of these scripts, so Phase 5 /
LilyDocs is not blocked by this ruling.


================================================================================
TEXT ADVANCES LAND ON PANGO'S DEVICE-DOT GRID
================================================================================

FIXED at PARITY 5 (2026-08-14), ruling R5.  TextFontMetric.TextStencil rounds
each SHAPED glyph advance to a whole device dot before accumulating it, which
is what Pango does to a glyph string before anything reads it.

Upstream never renders to a device, but every text metric it takes still travels
through Pango at PANGO_RESOLUTION dots per inch (lily/include/pango-font.hh:75 --
1200), and Pango's shaping rounds each glyph's geometry.width to a whole dot.
One dot, expressed in output units, is Pango_font::scale_ multiplied by
PANGO_SCALE, so the PANGO_SCALE cancels:

    dot = INCH_TO_BP / (PANGO_RESOLUTION * output_scale)

which at the default staff size (output-scale = 20pt / 72.27 * 25.4 / 4 mm =
1.7572990176) is 0.0341433 staff spaces, and about 15.5 design units of a
1000-unit em at the default text size.  TextFontMetric.DevicePixel answers it.

MEASURED on the pinned oracle, one glyph at a time, in C059-Roman
(~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-barnumber/probe-text-perglyph.ly, which runs
UNCHANGED on both engines and prints its measurements).  Every single-glyph
advance the oracle reports is an EXACT integer number of dots, and the port's
exact real advance rounds to precisely that integer:

    glyph            oracle (dots)   port exact   round
    H                     54           53.676      54
    x, g                  35           34.603      35
    1, 7                  36           35.827      36
    o                     32           32.218      32
    .                     18           17.913      18
    i                     20           20.298      20
    \small H              48           47.819      48
    \huge  H              68           67.628      68

TWO GROUPING QUESTIONS, both settled by measurement rather than by argument, and
both now fenced because either answer looks equally plausible on paper:

  * PER GLYPH, not per run.  "HH" is 108 dots, which is 2 * 54; rounding the
    RUN's exact total instead would give round(107.35) = 107.
  * THE KERN IS INSIDE THE ROUNDING.  "AV" is 87 dots.  A and V are 47 each and
    the A,V pair adjustment is -96 design units = -6.186 dots, so
    round(46.523 - 6.186) + 47 = 40 + 47 = 87, where rounding the advance and
    the kern separately gives 47 - 6 + 47 = 88.  "AVAVAVAV" is 327, which is
    7 * 40 + 47 and not any other grouping's answer.

ROW IMPACT: MATCH 778 -> 1249 of 2316, PLACEMENT-DIFFERS 791 -> 320, zero
regressions.  The residue's largest cluster went with it: 158 pages differed by
exactly 0.0148 at a single-digit bar number -- half one digit's width error,
because a bar number is centred on its bar line, and every digit shares an
advance in this face -- and 47 more by exactly twice that.

⚠ WHAT THIS CORRECTS.  PARITY 4 measured the same divergence with the oracle run
WITHOUT the corpus's font pinning, so its numbers compared Noto Serif against
C059 and showed an error on BOTH axes that grew with the string.  That reading
was an artifact of the yardstick: re-measured under the pinning, the port's INK
extents already agreed with the oracle to 2.4e-05 over 28 runs, the Y axis was
never wrong, and the whole divergence was the X advance grid.  Trap 8, from the
side that bites hardest -- the pinning has to be applied to a PROBE as well as
to a corpus.

Fences: KerningTableTests' dot-grid fences (the dot's own definition against a
doubled output-scale control, the per-glyph grouping, the kern grouping, and a
glyph that already sits ON the grid so rounding must leave it alone) and
TextAdvanceGridEndToEndTests (the same three facts read back out of the SVG
through a \box markup's edge rect, whose width is the run's width plus twice
box-padding and nothing else).

KNOWN REMAINING: the INK (Y) extent is the charstring CONTROL box, unrounded,
where Pango takes FreeType's grid-fitted ink rect.  Measured agreement over the
28 probe runs is 2.4e-05 worst case, 400x under the comparator's 0.01 tolerance,
so nothing in the corpus turns on it; if a residue cluster ever points at text
HEIGHT, this is the place to look.


===============================================================================
DOCUMENTATION-GENERATION PARITY
===============================================================================

  ly/generate-documentation.ly runs end to end under the port and writes all
  nineteen files.  EIGHTEEN ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL to the oracle's; internals.texi
  is not, and its residue is three named classes, below.

  Run it with:

      dotnet run --project tools/regression-harness/DocsDriver -c Release -- OUT

  The oracle is invoked the same way, and it must be, because the script writes
  through open-output-file with RELATIVE names -- the output directory is the
  PROCESS WORKING DIRECTORY, not an argument:

      cd OUT && lilypond .../ly/generate-documentation.ly

THE TWO NEW DATA TABLES

  Scheme/translator-descriptions.tsv and Scheme/entry-point-docs.tsv carry what
  upstream's ADD_TRANSLATOR and LY_DEFINE macros carry into C++ static
  initialisers: the translator descriptions and the entry-point docstrings.
  Both are registered at CreateInterpreter, the answer grob-interfaces.tsv
  already gave for ADD_INTERFACE (plan decision O8, option C).  Regenerate them
  with tools/table-extract/*.py against the pinned reference; no build step
  reads them, per standing rule 7.

  entry-point-docs.tsv covers 448 entry points from FOUR declaration sites, not
  one: LY_DEFINE (402), LY_DEFINE_WITH_SETTER (6), the two MAKE_SCHEME_CALLBACK
  macros that take a docstring (4), and the 36 smob type predicates, whose text
  Smob_base::init GENERATES from the class name (smobs.tcc:141-145).  The first
  extraction pass had only the first of the four and left 63 entry points
  undocumented -- the same shape entry-points.tsv's own history records.

WHAT internals.texi STILL DIFFERS BY (measured, 496 hunks)

  1. PROCEDURE SOURCE LOCATIONS -- 213 hunks.  Guile prints an anonymous
     procedure as #<procedure at lily/define-music-types.scm:352:33 (x p)>; the
     port prints #<procedure anonymous>.  CodeBrix.LilyScheme's reader does not
     track source locations at all -- source-properties, source-property and
     supports-source-properties? are stubs answering '(), #f and #f -- so this
     is a real feature (reader -> psyntax -> lambda meta), not a printing tweak.
     Its own work item and its own GO.

  2. module-public-interface RETURNS THE WHOLE MODULE -- 126 hunks.  The port
     documents procedures upstream does not, because a plain `define' inside a
     (define-module (lily)) file lands in what the port calls the public
     interface.  CodeBrix.LilyScheme's AGENT-README already records this as a
     deliberate divergence ("scope is WIDER than Guile's, never narrower") and
     records that narrowing it can only remove bindings other files may be
     resolving through the over-import -- so it wants a session that can sweep
     behind it.  Not attempted here.

  3. SMOB PRINT REPRESENTATIONS -- 7 hunks.  The oracle prints #<Duration 1 >
     and #<Mom 0> where the port prints 1 and 0.  Small, and the only one of
     the three that is a contained fix.

DEFECTS THIS RUN SURFACED IN THE ENGINE (all fixed, all silent)

  ly:dir?  answered #t TO EVERYTHING that was not a C# long.  It read its
  argument as `a[0] is long value ? value : 0', and 0 is a valid direction, so a
  symbol, a list, a string or a grob all passed.  general-scheme.cc:117 tests
  scm_is_integer first and answers #f otherwise.  Trap 6b's failure mode reached
  through trap 6c's cause: a C# type pattern where upstream reads the numeric
  tower.  The visible consequence was in scm->string, whose ly-type? asks every
  exported predicate in turn -- so every symbol and every list "was" a
  direction, and the documentation printed quoted values unquoted and stopped
  pretty-printing lists.

  ly:camel-case->lisp-identifier returned a STRING where general-scheme.cc:367
  returns ly_symbol2scm -- a symbol.  It prints identically, and it matches
  nothing that looks it up: document-music.scm hands the answer to
  ly:make-event-class, which is keyed by symbol, so all 272 music types silently
  lost their "Event classes" and "Accepted by" blocks.

  Scheme/lilyport/support.scm defined index?, character for character what
  scm/lily.scm:221 defines.  Two bindings of one name in two modules, so
  type-p-name-alist (keyed by the predicate OBJECT) missed the one the on-demand
  files resolved, and five paper variables documented their type as "index"
  instead of "non-negative, exact integer".  The duplicate is removed; the file
  records why at the point where it stood.

================================================================================
G8 CLOSED -- generate-documentation.ly IS BYTE-IDENTICAL, ALL 19 FILES
================================================================================

internals.texi went 496 hunks -> 0.  The three classes the docs-parity close
recorded
were the right three NAMES, and two of them were the wrong SIZE and the wrong
mechanism; two more classes existed that nothing had measured.  What each turned
out to be:

  1. PROCEDURE PRINTING -- recorded as "source locations, 213 hunks".  Source
     locations were real and are now carried (reader -> source-properties ->
     psyntax -> Tree-IL src -> closure, all in CodeBrix.LilyScheme), and the
     port now emits the oracle's five locations to the character.  But they
     account for EIGHT of the occurrences.  The dominant form is
     "#<program ADDR CODE>", which the oracle emits 206 times against 29
     ordinary ones -- and that is not a location question at all:

     ⚠ IT IS AN UPSTREAM BUG, AND PARITY REQUIRES REPRODUCING IT.
     libguile/programs.c:108-143 sets a file-static print_error flag, calls
     (system vm program)'s write-program, and clears the flag after.  The flag
     guards against a printer that errors.  ice-9/pretty-print.scm's truncating
     soft port abort-to-prompts from inside write-string, and an abort that
     lands inside write-program leaves the flag SET FOR THE REST OF THE PROCESS
     -- after which every procedure prints in the low-level form.  Verified
     directly against the oracle: print a procedure (nice), pretty-print a wide
     alist, print the same procedure again (#<program ...>).  LilyPond knows and
     works around the symptom rather than the cause -- scm->string
     (scm/lily-library.scm:1628-1629) carries a regex whose only purpose is to
     normalise "#<program HEX HEX>" to "#<program>".

     Two consequences that are easy to miss:

       * THE ADDRESS WIDTH IS LOAD-BEARING even though the address is stripped.
         scm->string strips it AFTER pretty-print has chosen its line breaks,
         and pretty-print measures the text it actually wrote.  Guile prints a
         64-bit heap pointer, so twelve hex digits; emitting eight moved line
         breaks on entries that contain no address at all.

       * THE SOFT PORT'S BUFFERING DECIDES WHEN THE LATCH FIRES.  An unbuffered
         port aborts on the first oversized write, which is almost always inside
         a procedure, so the port latched far earlier than the oracle.  Guile's
         soft port buffers 1024 bytes and transfers whole 252-byte quanta;
         CodeBrix.LilyScheme now does the same, measured against the oracle and
         confirmed by prediction.  With buffering, a structure that fits in the
         buffer never flushes mid-print and never latches -- which is exactly why
         '(Staff #<procedure at ...>) prints normally and the wide
         breathMarkDefinitions alist does not.

  2. module-public-interface -- recorded at 126 hunks, measured at 82.  Exports
     are now tracked per module (define-public, `export', and the #:export /
     #:re-export / #:export-syntax / #:replace keywords) and the interface holds
     exactly those, sharing variables rather than copying values.  ⚠ THE IMPORT
     SIDE IS UNCHANGED: a use-modules without #:select still puts the whole
     module on the use list, so visible scope stays WIDER than Guile's and never
     narrower.  The recorded danger was about narrowing IMPORTS; narrowing the
     interface removes nothing from anyone's scope, and the full sweep behind
     this session confirms it.

  3. SMOB PRINT REPRESENTATIONS -- recorded at 7 hunks; the real population is
     334 occurrences across FOUR types, not one.  Pitch (108), Moment (6) and
     Duration (1) now print through ISchemePrintable, and so does
     Unpure_pure_container (219), whose printer shows each part by its PROCEDURE
     NAME when it has one.  Note the inconsistent spacing is upstream's and is
     reproduced: "#<Pitch e' >" and "#<Duration 1 >" close with a space,
     "#<Mom 0>" does not.  ToString() is deliberately NOT the print
     representation on any of the four -- upstream keeps to_string() (the bare
     content, which ly:pitch->string and error messages read) separate from
     print_smob, and the port had conflated them.

  4. NEW: MACRO DOCSTRINGS WERE BEING DROPPED -- 8 hunks, 15 @defmac entries.
     A docstring on a macro belongs on the TRANSFORMER, not on the procedure the
     user wrote: documentation generators ask procedure-documentation of
     (macro-transformer m) and skip any macro that answers #f.  The prelude's
     define-syntax-rule discarded the string outright, and defmacro / defmacro*
     left it on the inner lambda.  All three now match Guile.  Invisible to every
     ordinary use of the macros.

  5. NEW: THE ENTRY-POINT DOCSTRING TABLE WAS MIS-EXTRACTED -- 5 entry points.
     Three separate faults in tools/table-extract/, all now fixed and the table
     regenerated:

       * C++ RAW STRING LITERALS WITH A NON-EMPTY DELIMITER.  cxxscan.py read
         only R"( ... )" and LilyPond writes every docstring containing a quote
         as R"delim( ... )delim" -- so four entry points kept a literal "delim("
         in their text, lost every embedded quoted string as if it were a
         literal boundary, and trailed ")delim".

       * DECLARING type_p_name_ IS NOT BEING A SMOB.  The predicate is defined by
         Smob_base<T>::init(), which only runs for a class that derives from a
         smob base.  lily/include/box.hh declares the name with no base at all,
         and the oracle answers #f to (defined? 'ly:box?).  The extractor now
         requires the derivation (class OR struct -- Duration and
         Tuplet_description are structs, and taking only `class' drops two live
         predicates).

       * BUILD-GUARDED ENTRY POINTS.  ly:gs-api and ly:shutdown-gs sit behind
         #if GS_API, which the oracle build has off; both are unbound there.

     ⚠ RELATED DIVERGENCE, RECORDED NOT FIXED: the port BINDS ly:box? as a type
     predicate where the oracle has no such name.  Only the documentation table
     was corrected here; whether the entry point should exist is an
     entry-point-closure question, not a documentation one.

ALSO RECORDED, NOT CHASED (found while reading unpure-pure-container.cc):
  UnpurePureContainer.PurePart() wraps the unpure part in an Unpure_pure_call
  whenever the pure part was omitted.  Upstream wraps only when the second smob
  slot is UNBOUND, and make_smob leaves it bound -- to the first argument -- when
  the pure part is omitted AND the unpure part is not a procedure
  (lily/include/unpure-pure-container.hh:36-41, whose comment reads "Avoid
  gratuitous creation of an Unpure_pure_call").  The port therefore answers a
  wrapper where upstream answers the value.  It does not affect printing, which
  reads the raw parts.  Phase 4's.

SOURCE NAMES CHANGED: the vendored scm/ layer is now read under
"lily/<name>.scm" rather than "<name>.scm" (LilyPondScheme.SourceNameFor).  That
is the tail of upstream's own path, and it is load-bearing rather than cosmetic:
scm->string shortens a location to its last TWO path components before printing
it, so a one-component name does not match its regex and the raw form would
survive into the manual.

PARITY GRIND -- THE FOUR STALE SEAMS, RETIRED

  All four stand-ins flagged at the cleanup were still standing in live code, and
  the sweep log proved it: the first two fire on the FIRST FILE of the corpus.
  Each target had been ported long before, each stand-in was written while it did
  not exist, and NOTHING RE-CHECKED THEM once it did.  This is trap 18 (a named
  absence stops being true the moment its owner lands) meeting trap 17 (a ported
  type with a missing call is worse than an unported one), four times over.

  Nothing here is a new translation.  The surrounding code in all four files is
  already upstream verbatim; only the call was going to a stub.

    1. Note_spacing::get_spacing -> Note_column::first_head.  The stand-in
       answered null, so left_head_end stayed 0 and the ideal distance of EVERY
       note-column spacing wish was short by one note head's right extent.
       Routed to NoteColumn.FirstHead; the whole chain below it
       (Note_column::get_stem, Stem::first_head, Stem::extremal_heads_if) was
       re-read against upstream and is faithful.

    2. Note_spacing::get_spacing -> find_nonempty_break_align_group ('staff-bar).
       Without it the space-to-barline branch always took upstream's
       no-staff-bar-group fallback and measured to the right side of the
       non-musical column.

    3. Spacing_spanner's do-not-float-a-bar-line guard -> the same function.
       Until it fired, a bar-line column upstream refuses to float could be
       declared loose.  The five "Loose column does not have right side to
       attach to" errors in the opening sweep are gone with it.

    4. Caesura_engraver -> make_script_from_event.  513 occurrences in the
       opening sweep: every CaesuraScript was created and then left
       UNCONFIGURED.  Both call sites now route to
       ScriptEngraver.MakeScriptFromEvent, and the articulation site is
       UNCONDITIONAL as upstream's is -- make_script_from_event does the
       articulation-type-must-be-a-symbol check itself, so the port's `is Symbol'
       guard was swallowing that report as well as leaving the grob bare.

  MEASURED, WHOLE CORPUS, AGAINST A VERIFIED BASELINE (the opening sweep
  reproduced the recorded figures exactly -- 2112/32/2, 2301 pages, and
  61/1293/929/33 verdicts -- before anything was touched):

      pages     2301 -> 2307        (line and page breaking moved)
      MATCH       61 -> 85          (+24)
      PLACEMENT 1293 -> 1447
      GLYPHS     929 -> 758         (-171)
      MISSING     33 -> 26          (-7)
      ratchet   203 rows improved, 2 REGRESSED

  ⚠ TWO ROWS WENT DOWN A RUNG, AND THEY ARE NOT A DEFECT IN THE CHANGE.
  Attributed honestly per trap 32, by DISABLING the routing and re-rendering:
  both reproduce standalone, and both revert to PLACEMENT-DIFFERS with seam 1
  disabled alone.  They are trap 28 -- removing the blocker exposed the next
  defect -- and each is fine geometry that a discrete decision amplifies:

    * glissando.svg, "extra line:0.1000 x2".  Line_interface::make_zigzag_line
      takes count = ceil (dz.length () / w), so a span of EXACTLY zero draws no
      teeth and any nonzero span draws one tooth = two lines.  The broken
      glissando piece spans ~0.07 staff space here and 0 in the oracle.  The
      zigzag teeth that ARE drawn match 18 for 18 on both sides.

    * beamlet-test.svg, "missing text 4, extra text 3".  The eight italic tuplet
      numbers are the SAME MULTISET on both sides (two 3s, six 5s); the one
      non-italic number is a bar number, and it reads 3 where the oracle reads 4.
      The line break moved by one measure.  Both sides still render 2 systems.

  The floor was therefore NOT advanced and NOT rebaselined -- ratchet.py update
  refuses while any row regresses, --force is never the fix, and a rebaseline is
  Jeremy's ruling (pass-manifest-decisions.tsv, --only to keep the two causes
  separately reversible).  The 203 improvements are real and unrecorded until
  that ruling.

THE from_scm<bool> DEFECT CLASS -- SWEPT, NOT SAMPLED

  Trap 14 stood recorded as a live class with only Tie_engraver audited.  It is
  now swept end to end and it is CLEAN but for one site.

  Method, so it can be re-run: every SchemeUtilities.ToBool / IsSchemeTrue applied
  to a GetProperty read (193 sites) was extracted, its C# Symbol constant resolved
  to the Scheme property name in its own file, and that name matched against
  upstream's spelling for the same property -- from_scm<bool> is EXACTLY-#t and
  maps to ToBool; scm_is_true / scm_is_false are truthiness and map to
  IsSchemeTrue.

    ONE MISMATCH.  Slur_engraver::double_property reads doubleSlurs with
    from_scm<bool> (lily/slur-engraver.cc:42); the port read it with Scheme
    truthiness, under which an UNSET property reads TRUE.  Fixed.

    The other five IsSchemeTrue sites are CORRECT and were left alone --
    circled-tip, tuplet-slur, centerBarNumbers and stencil are scm_is_true
    upstream, and suspendMelodyDecisions is scm_is_false read as !IsSchemeTrue.
    The six the script could not resolve automatically (breakable, create-new,
    ignore-ambitus, pop-first, first-markup-line, last-markup-line -- missed for
    a leading `!' or a multi-line call) were read by hand: all six are
    from_scm<bool> upstream and ToBool here.

  ⚠ THE CLASS IS CLOSED FOR PROPERTY READS ONLY.  A truth test on something that
  is not a property read was not in scope of this sweep.

TWO GUILE NAMES THE ERROR/NOOUT BUCKET NAMED (landed in CodeBrix.LilyScheme)

  Worked the other end of the residue -- the files that produce NO OUTPUT AT ALL,
  where the demand is a name rather than a geometry.  Two were unbound:

    open-file   libguile/fports.c's scm_open_file, the MODE-STRING opener.  It
                is a different procedure from open-input-file / open-output-file
                rather than a spelling of them: scm/backend-library.scm reaches
                for it four times and scm/framework-ps.scm twice, with "w",
                "rb" and "wb".  Direction characters select an input or an
                output port; "+" is refused loudly rather than half-served.

    file-port?  libguile/fports.c's scm_file_port_p.  ⚠ IT IS NOT "HAS A NAME":
                a string port carries the name <string> in Guile too.
                scm/graphviz.scm's graph-write gates a port-filename call on it
                and its regression file hands it (current-error-port), so
                answering by name takes the WRONG branch and prints the
                destination as #f.  Both port types now carry the distinction
                explicitly and port-filename answers for an output file port.

  Found on the way, and fixed with them: close-port handled only the INPUT side,
  so an output file port was never flushed.  backend-library.scm's output-scope
  opens a header field's file, displays to it, calls close-port and never calls
  flush-all-ports -- the file was created and left EMPTY.  close-port is now
  Guile's any-port close and, for a FILE port only, disposes the writer as
  scm_close_port releases the descriptor; the current output and error ports are
  deliberately exempt, because those writers belong to the host and must survive
  being closed from Scheme.  flush-all-ports skips an already-closed port.

  MEASURED: graphviz.svg went ERROR -> rendered (the sweep's error bucket 2 -> 1,
  SVG 2112 -> 2113), MISSING 26 -> 25.  Six tests, each paired with a control
  that must come out differently.

  ⚠ THE NEXT DEMAND IN THAT CHAIN IS NAMED AND NOT FIXED.
  event-listener-output moved PAST open-file and now fails on
  ly:moment-mul receiving 1/2 as its first argument.  scm/operators.scm defines
  both (* (a <Moment>) (b <number>)) and (* (a <number>) (b <Moment>)), and the
  second passes (b a); receiving (ratio, moment) means the port's GOOPS dispatch
  selected the MOMENT-FIRST method for a number-first call.  That is a method
  SELECTION defect, not a numeric-tower one, and it is a different class from
  anything in this session.  It stays NOOUT.



ADDED IN PARITY 2 -- THE TWO RED ROWS, AND WHAT THEY WERE ACTUALLY POINTING AT

PARITY 1 left two rows regressed and put them to Jeremy; the ruling was to fix
them as ordinary Phase-4 work rather than rebaseline. Neither turned out to be a
glissando defect or a beaming defect. Both were the same defect, one layer down,
and it was costing EVERY SYSTEM IN THE CORPUS its prefatory spacing.

HOW THE TWO ROWS READ, AND WHY THEY MISLED

  glissando.svg    "extra line:0.1000 x2".  Line_spanner::print returns SCM_EOL
                   when gaps[LEFT] + gaps[RIGHT] > dz.length (), and Glissando
                   pads 0.5 on each side -- so any glissando spanning less than
                   1.0 draws NOTHING AT ALL.  Measured against the oracle with a
                   stencil-callback probe: the fifth glissando spans 0.835 there
                   and drew nothing; it spanned 1.07 here, leaving ~0.07 after
                   the gaps, which make_zigzag_line's ceil() turns into one tooth
                   = two lines.  make_zigzag_line was faithful all along.

  beamlet-test.svg "missing text 4, extra text 3".  A BAR NUMBER, not a tuplet
                   number: the line break had moved one measure.

  The probe that named the real defect compared the X SPAN of all eight
  glissando pieces on both sides.  Every span was too wide by a CONSTANT --
  0.25 on the first system, 0.28088 on the second -- while the note-head widths
  agreed exactly (1.3042 both sides).  A constant is not accumulated drift.
  Reading the glyph positions straight out of both SVGs finished it: the clef
  and the time signature sat at IDENTICAL x on both sides, and the FIRST NOTE
  was 1.0 staff space too far left here (2.135 on a clef-only system).  The
  music was starting too early and stretching to fill the line.

THE DEFECT: A DEFENSIVE NULL CHECK THAT UPSTREAM DELIBERATELY DOES NOT HAVE

  Spacing_interface::extremal_break_aligned_grob resolves the column it measures
  extents against as ONE UNCHECKED CHAIN:

      col = dynamic_cast<Item *> (elts[0])->get_column ()
              ->find_prebroken_piece (break_dir);

  At a LINE START every element the wish names has already been substituted to
  its prebroken piece, so elts[0] is itself broken, the column reached through
  it is itself a prebroken piece, and find_prebroken_piece answers NULL --
  upstream then hands that null straight to extent (), which reads the extent
  against the ROOT.  Grob::relative_coordinate is written to tolerate exactly
  that and says so in a comment citing upstream issue #6149.  For a line-start
  column, root-relative and column-relative agree, so upstream gets the right
  answer out of what looks like an accident.

  The port had added `if (col == null) continue;'.  That threw the whole line
  start away: every break-aligned symbol was skipped, extremal_break_aligned_grob
  returned null, and Staff_spacing::get_spacing took its `we used to have a
  warning here' early return and answered a DEFAULT SPRING.  So Clef's
  (first-note . (minimum-fixed-space . 5.0)) and TimeSignature's
  (first-note . (semi-shrink-space . 2.0)) were never applied ANYWHERE, and the
  space they had asked for was handed to the musical springs instead.

  ⚠ NOTHING REPORTED THIS.  No programming error, no unknown-prefatory-spacing,
  no empty wish list -- the wish was found and the spring was merged; it was
  simply the wrong spring.  The whole space-alist type dispatch below it
  (fixed-space, extra-space, semi-fixed-space, minimum-space, minimum-fixed-space,
  shrink-space, semi-shrink-space) was correct, complete and UNREACHABLE.

  The guard is removed and the comment at the site now says why there is no
  guard, so it does not get "fixed" back.

HOW IT WAS PROVED BEFORE IT WAS CHANGED (rule 35: check the oracle first)

  Three files rendered through BOTH engines: a plain two-system score, and the
  same score with Clef.space-alist.first-note overridden to
  (minimum-fixed-space . 15.0) and to (extra-space . 15.0).  The oracle moved
  its second-system first note 14.3358 -> 24.3358 -> 25.6243.  The port did not
  move at all -- identical output in all three cases, which is what a dead
  space-alist looks like.  After the change the port reproduces all three oracle
  positions EXACTLY, on both systems.

MEASURED

    verdicts    MATCH             85 -> 693
                PLACEMENT-DIFFERS 1448 -> 874
                GLYPHS-DIFFER     758 -> 727
                MISSING           25 -> 22
    ratchet     796 improvements, ZERO regressions
    rows        glissando.svg and beamlet-test.svg both back to
                PLACEMENT-DIFFERS -- the two red rows are closed
    sweep       2113 svg / 32 no output / 1 errored, 2311 pages (was 2308)
    G8          19 of 19 still byte-identical
    G2          90 of 90 MIDI, from a freshly generated candidate

  One fix, one file, four lines removed.  It is the largest single parity move
  the project has recorded, and it is worth remembering WHY it hid so long: the
  machinery it disabled was fully ported, fully correct, and fully tested at the
  unit level.  Registered != behaving != reachable, one more time -- and this
  time the unreachable thing was reached through a guard that looked like
  ordinary defensive hygiene.

===============================================================================
ACCEPTED DIVERGENCES MOVED OUT OF THE PLAN (2026-08-14, PARITY 3 bookkeeping)
===============================================================================

  Both of these had been riding the Phase-4 plan's "known loose ends" list as
  though they were unfinished work.  They are not: they are decisions.  Rule 5
  puts a deliberate divergence here with its reason, so here they are, and the
  plan's list is now open defects only.

  (1) GrobCallbacks' PURE SKYLINE VARIANTS ANSWER ORDINARY EXTENTS.

  The pure variants of the skyline callbacks return the ordinary extent rather
  than a genuinely pure one.  This was correct when it was written -- the
  pure-property machinery did not exist yet -- and it has NOT been revisited
  since that machinery landed.

  Kept deliberately for now, and the reason is trap 24: the pure reader's
  contract is that a bare procedure answers #f and is never called, so a pure
  path that answers an ORDINARY extent is wrong in a way that is invisible
  until something depends on the difference.  Nothing in the corpus currently
  does.  Revisit when a residue cluster points at pure-height estimation
  (vertical spacing before line breaking); until then, changing it would be an
  unmeasured edit to a decades-tuned estimate.

  (2) `No spacing entry from Clef to `custos'' IS UPSTREAM'S OWN.

  Break_alignment_interface::calc_positioning_done issues a programming error
  when the left group's space-alist has no entry for the right group's
  break-align symbol.  Clef's space-alist (scm/define-grobs.scm) has no `custos'
  entry, and `clef' precedes `custos' in ALL THREE break-align-orders, so
  upstream reaches that pair itself.

  CONFIRMED against the pinned oracle rather than reasoned about: 2.27.2 run
  NON-SILENT on mensural-ligature-citations.ly emits
  "No spacing entry from Clef to `custos'" ten times, and emits no other
  programming error anywhere in the regression suite.  REPRODUCE, do not chase.

  ⚠ The count is not yet equal -- the port emits 13 on that file where the
  oracle emits 10.  That difference is NOT this divergence; it belongs to the
  file's already-recorded page-count divergence (it writes FEWER pages than the
  oracle).  Do not "fix" the message.

  ⚠ Nor does this cover the OTHER pairs in the same message class.  118
  occurrences name adjacencies that break-align-orders makes impossible
  (BarLine -> breathing-sign, BarLine -> signum-repetitionis,
  TimeSignature -> key-signature and four more, every one order-REVERSED).

  FIXED at PARITY 5 (2026-08-14), and "every one order-REVERSED" was the whole
  tell: Break_alignment_interface::ordered_elements never reordered anything.
  Upstream guards the reordering with `if (scm_is_false (order)) return elts;`
  -- proceed unless the order is #f -- and the port had written that as
  `if (!SchemeUtilities.ToBool (order))`.  ToBool is from_scm<bool>, i.e.
  EXACTLY-#t, and an order is a LIST, so the guard answered "no order" for every
  real order and the elements came back in `elements` order on every alignment
  in the corpus.  Trap 14, in the half PARITY 1 left unswept (the test is on a
  local, not on a property read -- D7), and trap 30b's shape for the third time:
  the reordering below it is a faithful translation that had never run.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PARITY GRIND -- THE BRACE FONT'S SCALE, AND THREE DIAGNOSTIC SEVERITIES
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Written at PARITY 6 (2026-08-14).

  (1) THE fetaBraces SCALE, AND WHY IT LOOKED RIGHT ON THE PAGE.

  lily/font-select.cc:160-184 runs fetaMusic, fetaBraces and fetaText through
  ONE call to best_rounded_design_size.  The three differ only in the file-name
  SUFFIX -- a rounded size for fetaMusic and fetaText, the literal "brace" for
  fetaBraces -- and fetaMusic and fetaBraces then BOTH end

      return find_scaled_font (layout, fm, requested_size / actual_size);

  The port's brace branch had instead divided by the brace OTF's own design
  size, on the strength of upstream's comment "This font has the design size of
  the OTF file".  That comment says why find_otf_font supplies the METRIC; it
  does not say what divides the scale.  emmentaler-brace records its design size
  in MILLIMETRES, so the port's braces measured 2.84528x too tall -- which is
  exactly 1/ly:pt(1), and that constant is what named the cause.
