  THE REASON IT SURVIVED THIS LONG is worth keeping.  \left-brace does not ask
  for a brace of a given size; it BINARY-SEARCHES the font's glyphs by Y extent
  (scm/define-markup-commands.scm:5213-5240).  A font scaled wrongly by a
  constant therefore self-compensates: the search picks a proportionally smaller
  glyph, the transform comes out proportionally larger, and the brace is drawn
  at about the right height.  Nothing about the PAGE looked wrong.  It showed up
  only as a glyph-NAME difference -- the oracle's brace177@0.0040 against the
  port's brace49@0.0114 -- on 136 pages, and NAMED-GLYPH identity (D29) is the
  only reason it was visible at all.  Anything that selects a glyph BY MEASURING
  IT can hide a scale error this way.

  (2) Open_type_font::count IS NOT THE CHARSET SIZE.

  Found by the same probe, in its own output.  Upstream's count() answers
  index_to_charcode_map_.size (), i.e. the glyphs a CHARCODE reaches; the port
  answered the CFF charset size, which also carries .notdef at index 0.  One too
  many -- and \left-brace uses (1- (ly:otf-glyph-count font)) as the TOP of its
  binary search, so the search was handed a glyph that does not exist, whose
  extent printed as (+inf.0 . -inf.0).

  Verified against the pinned oracle for both feta fonts rather than reasoned
  about: 2.27.2 answers ly:otf-glyph-count 667 for emmentaler-20 and 576 for
  emmentaler-brace, and the port now answers both exactly.

  ⚠ The port has no index_to_charcode map of its own; GlyphCount counts the
  DISTINCT glyph indices in the cmap, and falls back to "charset less .notdef"
  for a font with no cmap at all.  That fallback is a stand-in, not a port of
  upstream's mechanism, and it is the thing to revisit if a font ever arrives
  whose charset and cmap disagree by more than .notdef.

  (3) THE THREE ERROR SEVERITIES, TWO OF THEM SWAPPED.

  flower/warn.cc has three prefixes and they are not interchangeable:
  print_error, which the FATAL error/1 goes through, prints "fatal error: "
  (warn.cc:197); "error: " belongs to non_fatal_error (warn.cc:249), the one
  that does NOT stop the run; programming_error prints "programming error: ".
  The port's Warn.Error -- its fatal one, which throws -- printed "error: ", so
  every stopped run reported a severity upstream reserves for runs that carry
  on.  Ruling R1 is about severity as well as wording.  Fenced together in
  WarnTests, deliberately: a future swap moves two of the three lines at once.

  (4) ly:find-file's STRICT FLAG, AND ONE ACCEPTED VALUE DIVERGENCE.

  Upstream's ly:find-file takes an optional strict argument and, when the file
  is missing and strict is #t, raises a FATAL error naming the load path and the
  cwd (general-scheme.cc:55-77).  The port had dropped the argument entirely and
  always answered #f, so \markup \image and \verbatim-file produced nothing
  where the oracle stops the run.

  ACCEPTED DIVERGENCE, and it cannot be closed: the message embeds the load path
  and the working directory, which are properties of the MACHINE and of how each
  engine was invoked.  The port's include path is not upstream's global_path and
  never will be -- upstream's carries its own installed ly/, ps/, scm/ and font
  directories.  The SENTENCE is upstream's verbatim; the two VALUES inside it
  differ, exactly as the "compressing over-full page by N staff-spaces" pair
  does.  The diagnostics gate therefore grades these four files (markup-image,
  markup-image-file-extension-uppercase, markup-verbatim-file-utf8,
  clip-systems) as BOTH rather than MATCH, and that is the correct resting
  state, not a defect to chase.

  (5) \startStaff AND \stopStaff WERE INERT.

  Staff_symbol_engraver holds a Unique_span_event_listener on staff_span and its
  whole process_music is written in terms of it (staff-symbol-engraver.cc:66-78):
  a stop closes the open spanner, a start opens a new one, and first_start_ only
  covers the implicit spanner a staff gets when the music never asks.  The port
  had NO listener registered at all -- it opened one spanner on the first
  timestep and closed it at finalize -- so both commands did nothing and a staff
  had exactly ONE StaffSymbol however many times the music restarted it.

  Found by arithmetic on the residue, not by reading the file: the sixteen
  bar-line-placement-* files drive six \stopStaff ... \startStaff segments per
  staff through one include, and the port drew 21 staff lines where the oracle
  drew 126.  One sixth, on every file in the family.  The ratio is what named it.

  Recorded because of what it says about the SILENT-DEFECT POSTURE (rule 4): the
  engraver was registered, reached, and drawing -- it simply drew one sixth of
  what it owed, and nothing anywhere said so.  `registered != behaving' has a
  quieter form than a missing binding, and this is it.

================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- THE SPANNER-BOUND HANDSHAKE, AND THE DIAGNOSTIC SINK
================================================================================

PARITY 7.  Six defects, and the first two of them are ONE defect seen from two
sides -- D11 (staff-span segments after the first were never drawn) and D2
(`bounds of spanner are invalid', 403 occurrences).

  (1) Spanner::set_bound's DOUBLE DISPATCH WAS MISSING, AND WITH IT THE
      `bounded-by-me' HANDSHAKE THAT KEEPS A PAPER COLUMN ALIVE.

  Upstream's Spanner::set_bound begins by asking the GROB whether it accepts
  being a bound (spanner.cc:245):

      if (!g->internal_set_as_bound_of_spanner (this, d))
        { programming_error (...); return; }

  Grob answers #f, Item forwards to Spanner::accepts_as_bound_item, and
  Paper_column forwards to Spanner::accepts_as_bound_paper_column AND, when the
  answer is yes, ADDS THE SPANNER TO ITS `bounded-by-me' ARRAY.  Upstream's own
  comment at paper-column.cc:50 says what that is for: "Signal that this column
  needs to be kept alive.  They need to be kept alive to have meaningful position
  and linebreaking."  Paper_column::is_used returns TRUE for a column that has
  one, and System::used_columns keeps only used columns.

  The port had the READ side and not the WRITE side.  PaperColumn.IsUsed
  consulted `bounded-by-me' faithfully; nothing ever put anything in it, because
  SetBound never made the dispatch call at all.  So a column whose only reason to
  exist was being some spanner's bound counted as UNUSED, was dropped from the
  system's column list, never had a system assigned, and DoBreakProcessing then
  found bounds with no system, reported "bounds of spanner are invalid" and
  SUICIDED the piece.

  MEASURED, not reasoned (trap 8a).  A synthetic staff of N
  \stopStaff...\startStaff segments produced exactly N-1 of the message, and
  instrumenting the failing branch printed the reason in one line:

      preR=NonMusicalPaperColumn rank=6 sys=NULL
           [used=False breakable=False elts=0 bbm=0]

  elts=0 and bbm=0 is the whole defect.  The column carried nothing and bounded
  nothing, so nothing kept it.

  ONE call restored.  bounds of spanner are invalid 403 -> 0; bounds of this
  piece aren't breakable 228 -> 3; MATCH 1,416 -> 1,450 with 50 ratchet
  improvements and no regression.  D2 had been open since PARITY 3 and had been
  read as a defect in Spanner::BreakIntoPieces, which PARITY 5 verified line by
  line and correctly found FAITHFUL.  It was faithful.  What fed it was not.

  NAMING NOTE.  SystemGrob's own SetBound override, which tested `grob is
  PaperColumn' and reported "system bound must be a paper column", is RETIRED.
  Upstream expresses that rule as System::accepts_as_bound_item -> false plus
  accepts_as_bound_paper_column -> true, so the refusal now travels through the
  base dispatch and reports upstream's wording, "cannot set X as bound of Y"
  (ruling R1, rule 15).

  (2) ly:expect-warning AND ly:check-expected-warnings WERE NO-OP STUBS.

  flower/warn.cc:105-148 holds a three-part mechanism the port did not have at
  all: expect_warning registers a message, is_expected SUPPRESSES it when it
  arrives (printing `suppressed warning: ...' at LOG_DEBUG) and CONSUMES the
  registration, and check_expected_warnings reports whatever was registered and
  never arrived.  122 regression files call it, 298 times.

  The consequence was one-sided and invisible: the ORACLE's log for such a file
  is silent, so every one of those warnings the port emitted was an EXTRA graded
  against nothing.  The matching is a PREFIX match, deliberately -- upstream's
  comment says it is "needed for the Input class, where the message contains the
  input file contents after the real message".

  ly:check-expected-warnings is called from the VENDORED lily.scm:1191, between
  (lilypond-file) and (session-terminate).  The batch runner's equivalent point
  is the END of the file's work, NOT after the parse: placed after the parse it
  fired before engraving and reported tie-unterminated's expected warning missing
  one line before emitting it.

  (3) THE ly: MESSAGE FAMILY WAS WRITING INTO A NULL SINK.

  ProgramOptions.Output is `TextWriter.Null' and NOTHING EVER ASSIGNED IT.  Every
  ly:message, ly:warning, ly:programming-error, ly:non-fatal-error, ly:progress
  and ly:debug had therefore been discarded for the life of the port: no
  diagnostic raised by the vendored Scheme layer had ever been seen.

  They now go where upstream's go -- ly:warning to warning(), ly:programming-error
  to programming_error(), and so on -- with Report kept for its bookkeeping.  This
  also makes the SEVERITIES real: ly:programming-error had shared
  MessageSeverity.Warning with ly:warning.  Warn.Level is LevelWarn by default, so
  message/progress/debug stay unprinted exactly as before; the difference is that
  the LOG LEVEL now decides rather than the sink being dead.

  This is what beam-quant-standard was waiting for.  Its expected output is an
  ly:warning from layout-beam.scm's check-beam-quant, and the port now prints the
  oracle's five lines with the oracle's five pairs of numbers.

  (4) THE ly: MESSAGE FORMATTER APPENDED ITS ARGUMENTS INSTEAD OF SUBSTITUTING.

  Every ly: message entry point upstream runs
  scm_simple_format (SCM_BOOL_F, str, rest) first.  The port appended the
  arguments to the template separated by spaces, so
  (ly:warning (G_ "Bar number is ~a; expected ~a") 3 15) printed the escapes
  intact with "3 15" on the end.  Rule 15 applied to the message BODY rather than
  its prefix -- and a precondition for (2), because an expectation and the
  warning it suppresses must be built by the same rule.  56 of the 298
  expectations pass format arguments.

  (5) version-seen WAS NEVER DEFINED, SO EVERY FILE WAS REPORTED AS HAVING NO
      \version.

  lexer.ll:243-264 defines version-seen in the top scope the moment the MAIN
  input's \version string is read: #f none, #t found-but-unparseable, otherwise
  the parsed version.  The port's lexer recorded the STRING and stopped, so
  version-seen kept ly/init.ly's initial #f and the epilogue announced "no
  \version statement found" for every file in the suite -- including files whose
  first line is a \version.

  Nothing had ever noticed, because that message goes through
  ly:warning-located, which is (3)'s null sink.  This is what (3) exposed: a
  defect can hide behind another defect's silence.  Upstream's main-input test is
  `is_main_input_ && include_stack_.size () == main_input_level_'; the port's
  runner parses the user's file in a ParseText call of its own and never uses
  \maininput, so the equivalent test is the include depth alone.

  (6) THE CYCLIC-DEPENDENCY READ ANSWERED '() WHERE UPSTREAM ANSWERS THE SENTINEL.

  Grob::internal_get_property reports the cycle and FALLS THROUGH
  (grob-property.cc:195-210), so `calculation-in-progress' itself is what the
  caller receives and what try_callback_on_alist then STORES as the property's
  value -- internal_set_value_on_alist lets it past the type check for exactly
  that reason.  The property stays poisoned and every later read reports the cycle
  again.  The port returned the empty list, which quietly healed the grob, so
  debug-property-callbacks reported its cycle ONCE where the oracle reports it
  five times.

  ⚠ NOT CLOSED, AND REPORTED AS NOT CLOSED.  With the sentinel restored the port
  reports the cycle EIGHT times: NoteHead.stencil four times, which is the
  oracle's count, and NoteHead.layer four times where the oracle reports it once.
  The file grades EXTRA +3 where it graded MISSING before -- a different wrong
  answer, not a right one.  The change is kept because it is what upstream does
  (rule 2); the residue is the plan's own carry-forward.

  The `debug-property-callbacks' BACKTRACE is implemented (the callback stack,
  pushed and popped only while the option is set, exactly as upstream guards it)
  but does not appear in the sweep log: upstream prints it through ::message at
  LOG_INFO and the port's default log level is LevelWarn where upstream's is INFO.
  The diagnostics gate drops those lines on both sides anyway.

  DELIBERATELY NOT DONE, WITH ITS REVISIT CONDITION.  Upstream routes a
  programming_error and a non_fatal_error through deferrable_error when
  warning-as-error is set, which prints `fatal error: ' and stops the run.  The
  port must not do that yet: it still REACHES programming errors upstream does
  not (the D2 residue, 3 occurrences of "bounds of this piece aren't breakable"),
  and honouring warning-as-error would turn those into LOST OUTPUT rather than a
  noisy log.  Revisit when that residue is zero.  Related and also left alone:
  Warn.WarningAsError is assigned by nothing, so `ly:set-option 'warning-as-error'
  is inert in the port.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GLYPH SUBSTITUTION -- THE GSUB SURFACE (2026-08-15, PARITY 9, D33)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Fonts/SubstitutionTable.cs is new-in-family.  Upstream reaches glyph
  substitution through Pango: Text_interface::interpret_string reads the
  `font-features' property chain, joins the list with commas
  (text-interface.cc:207-238) and hands the string to
  pango_attr_font_features_new (pango-font.cc:534), after which HarfBuzz applies
  the named features while shaping.  The port owes the LIBRARY'S BEHAVIOUR here
  (trap 15), and until PARITY 9 applied NONE of it.

  WHAT IS IMPLEMENTED.  Lookup types 1 (single), 2 (multiple), 3 (alternate) and
  4 (ligature), plus type 7 (extension) unwrapped to the type it carries; the
  features of the chosen script's default language system; the enabled set being
  HarfBuzz's default-on GSUB features plus the tags the run names minus the tags
  it names with a leading `-'; and every enabled feature's lookups applied ONCE
  EACH in ascending lookup-list index rather than once per feature.  The last of
  those is not an optimisation -- it is what makes tnum, cv47 and ss01 COMPOSE
  into `fattened.fixedwidth.four.alt' instead of one of them winning.

  DELIBERATELY NOT IMPLEMENTED, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.  The CONTEXTUAL
  lookup types (5, 6 and 8) are absent.  Across all 24 vendored text faces and
  all 9 Emmentaler faces, no feature that is default-on or that any LilyPond grob
  or markup command asks for names a contextual lookup; the only contextual
  lookups present at all belong to `ordn' and `frac', which nothing enables.
  REVISIT CONDITION: a face whose enabled features need one would silently get
  less substitution than Pango gives it, so re-measure if the D23 fallback chain
  grows a face.

  ✅ RESOLVED AT PARITY 10 (2026-08-15).  The script chosen for the feature
  lookup is now latn, then DFLT, then whatever comes first.

  What PARITY 9 recorded, and why it was right to record rather than fix: the
  choice had been DFLT-first, copied from Fonts/KerningTable.cs, whose comment
  records that every vendored face names the SAME kern lookups from every script
  it declares, so the choice cannot change a GPOS answer.  THAT MEASUREMENT DOES
  NOT CARRY OVER TO GSUB, and the counterexample was measured: C059-Roman's DFLT
  language system names aalt/dnom/fina/frac/hist/numr/ordn/sups and NOT `liga',
  which only the `latn' script names.  Pango itemizes Latin text under latn, so
  the oracle formed C059's f-ligatures and the port formed none -- `liga' inert
  for every text run, and `\typewriter's "-liga" inert with it.

  THE BLAST RADIUS WAS MEASURED BEFORE THE CHANGE, across every vendored face
  rather than the one that named the defect:

    - the twelve URW faces (C059, NimbusSans, NimbusMonoPS) name `liga' from
      `latn' and from no other script -- these are the faces that move;
    - the twelve TeX Gyre faces name the same features from every script they
      declare, so they cannot move;
    - all nine Emmentaler faces declare ONLY `DFLT', so the music font cannot
      move either, and with it the ss01/cv47/tnum composition D33 landed.

  So the change is exactly "turn `liga' on for the twelve URW text faces", which
  is what Pango does, and `-liga' now has something to turn off.  Fenced in
  SubstitutionTableTests against C059-Roman's own GSUB table: its `liga' lookups
  are ligature substitutions over the glyphs for f, i and l, so f+f and f+i each
  become ONE glyph and the two are different glyphs, with "-liga" as the control.

  MUSIC-FONT KERNING, added in the same pass.  Emmentaler ships a GPOS `kern'
  feature built specifically for the digits (mf/emmentaler_kerning.py, including
  pairs for the fattened and fixedwidth variants; emmentaler-20 carries 1,061
  pair values).  Upstream sets these runs through Pango like any other, so the
  kerning is inside the advance it measures, and TextInterface's music-font path
  now applies it AFTER substitution -- the pair a kern belongs to is the one
  substitution left behind, which is the order HarfBuzz applies GSUB and GPOS in.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ly:message IS NOT PRINTED (2026-08-15, PARITY 9, observed while probing)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  PARITY 7 opened the Scheme-layer diagnostic sink for ly:warning and
  ly:programming-error (ProgramOptions.Output had been TextWriter.Null and was
  assigned by nothing -- trap 1b).  ly:message was NOT covered by that fix and
  still prints nothing: a file whose only output is `#(ly:message "...")' is
  silent on the port and prints on the oracle.

  Found while writing a D6 probe, not by any gate, and it is exactly trap 1a's
  shape -- the diagnostics gate grades the sweep log against the oracle's
  reference logs, and the oracle's reference logs are generated `--silent', so a
  message the oracle would print into a non-silent run is graded by nothing.
  NOT FIXED at PARITY 9: outside that session's GO, and it is a diagnostics item
  rather than a comparator one.  The practical consequence to remember is for
  PROBES: a probe that reports through ly:message is readable on the oracle only
  and cannot be diffed, so probes report through ly:warning.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GLYPH-OUTLINE SKYLINES DIFFER BECAUSE THE TWO EMMENTALER BUILDS DIFFER
(2026-08-15, PARITY 10 -- RE-DIAGNOSED AT PARITY 14, and the first diagnosis
was wrong)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  A grob's vertical-skylines are built from its stencil, and for a music glyph
  that means walking the FONT OUTLINE.  Upstream does it in lily/freetype.cc's
  Path_interpreter, driven by FT_Outline_Decompose: each cubic is cut into
  max(2, int(chord_length / 0.2)) segments and each segment is added as a
  contour segment.  Fonts/GlyphOutlineSkyline.cs uses the SAME formula and the
  same 0.2, over the port's own CFF charstring interpreter.

  WHAT PARITY 10 RECORDED, AND WHY IT WAS WRONG.  This entry used to say the
  port "approximates a curved outline with slightly different buildings",
  naming the two WALKERS as the divergence -- 203 segments against 211 on
  show-skylines.  PARITY 14 measured that claim and it does not hold:

    * Driving the SAME FreeType the oracle uses (ctypes on libfreetype,
      FT_LOAD_NO_SCALE then FT_Outline_Decompose, self-checked against
      fontTools on 663 glyphs) and comparing its command stream against a
      plain charstring interpreter's gives 668 of 668 glyphs IDENTICAL.
      FT_Outline_Decompose adds nothing a charstring walk does not.
    * The port's closing-segment rule (emit only when the contour does not
      already end at its start) matches FreeType's too: of 1219 contours in
      emmentaler-20, 887 already close themselves.

  THE ACTUAL CAUSE IS THE FONT, and it is a decision rather than a defect.
  The port BUILDS Emmentaler from the Metafont sources (assets/fonts/README.txt,
  tools/font-build/) instead of copying LilyPond's binaries, deliberately, for
  end-to-end provenance.  The two builds are not outline-identical: of 668
  glyphs in emmentaler-20, 288 have a different drawing-op sequence and 97
  differ numerically.  Running upstream's own flattening formula over the two
  fonts reproduces the reported symptom exactly -- clefs.G comes out SEVEN
  segments longer on the oracle's build at every scale, while accidentals.sharp,
  whose outline IS identical between the builds, comes out the same.
  show-skylines draws the skylines of exactly those two glyphs.

  THIS WAS PREDICTED IN WRITING ON 2026-08-02.  tools/font-build/README.txt
  section 7 records the same font comparison (bounding boxes differ on ~18% of
  glyphs, median 1 unit, max 3 -- re-verified at PARITY 14 as 123 of 668,
  median 1, max 3, its numbers exactly) and names lily/stencil-integral.cc:555
  get_glyph_outline_bbox, the skyline path, as the ONE place FreeType outline
  data is consulted at all.  Every other glyph dimension comes from the LILC
  table, which IS byte-identical between the builds -- which is why 2,204 rows
  match and only the debug drawing of a skyline does not.

  CONSEQUENCE FOR G1.  The six rows that grade the skyline debug drawing
  (show-skylines, skyline-boxes-ellipses, skyline-debug, skyline-embedded-ps,
  skyline-grob-rotation, skyline-slur-segments) CANNOT reach MATCH while the
  port builds its own fonts.  No walker change closes them.  This is the same
  root fact D29's named-glyph identity exists to neutralise for DRAWN glyphs,
  surfacing in the one place D29 cannot reach: the debug drawing emits plain
  <line> elements, which carry no glyph identity to resolve by name.

  DISPOSITION -- RULED (Jeremy, 2026-08-16, ruling R9).  The six rows are NOT
  skipped and they are NOT closed by reversing the font build.  They are to be
  graded against a COMMITTED PORT-GENERATED BASELINE, and the mechanism is to be
  fenced separately by a unit test.  G1's own definition already reserves this
  shape -- it requires files whose divergence is in an ASSET the port builds or
  ships to "match committed PORT-GENERATED baselines" -- and that clause has never
  been exercised, so the machinery does not exist yet and has to be built.
  (/!\ THAT CLAUSE WAS RE-WORDED ON 2026-08-17.  When this entry was written it read
  "D23's fallback-dependent files", which was untrue about every row it covered:
  these six and R15's two are font-BUILD-dependent, not fallback-dependent, and
  Roboto was never in the fallback chain at all.)

  WHAT EACH HALF CLAIMS, because they are not interchangeable and the split is
  what keeps this honest:

    * THE BASELINE CLAIMS NO DRIFT, AND NOTHING ELSE.  It is a regression
      instrument.  Rule 33 forbids an expected value recorded from the port's own
      output, and this is exactly that -- so it must never be read as a
      correctness result.  What the six rows stop doing is grading the port's
      font BUILD against the oracle's, which is the one comparison that cannot
      succeed and was never informative.  What they keep doing is turning red the
      moment the skyline mechanism moves.
    * THE FENCE CARRIES THE CORRECTNESS CLAIM, and it is the only thing that
      does.  Expected segment counts are HAND-COMPUTED from upstream's own
      expression -- max (2, chord_length / 0.2), the chord measured in OUTPUT
      units after the transform -- with a control that must come out differently.
      The scale dependence is the natural control: one glyph set at two sizes
      must produce different counts in a known ratio, which a table returning a
      constant cannot fake.  It survives a font rebuild; the corpus rows do not.

  /!\ LANDING THE BASELINE WITHOUT THE FENCE IS THE FAILURE MODE.  Together they
  cover R8(B)'s actual concern -- that a mechanism feeding every collision
  computation not go ungraded -- better than the corpus rows ever did, because
  those rows grade the code and the asset FUSED and cannot separate them.

  PROVENANCE TO COMMIT WITH THE BASELINE, since it is the strongest thing that
  can be said at the moment of recording: for every glyph whose outline is
  IDENTICAL between the two builds, the port's skyline already matches the
  oracle's exactly -- accidentals.sharp is the measured control.  The divergence
  is confined to glyphs whose outlines differ.

  /!\ AND THE BASELINE MUST BE FROZEN FROM A FRESH SWEEP, never from whatever
  candidate/svg happens to be on disk (the MIDI scoreboard carries the same
  warning for the same reason).

  ✅ BUILT AT PARITY 15 (2026-08-16).  Both halves, and neither alone:

    * tools/regression-harness/baseline/svg holds the six pages, frozen from the
      full sweep of that morning, with baseline-manifest.tsv carrying the sha256s
      and the provenance.  Graded by
      `compare-output.py --baseline baseline/svg candidate/svg' -> 6 of 6 MATCH.
      --baseline makes BOTH sides resolve against the port's own fonts, and the
      control was run: WITHOUT it the baseline side resolves 5 of 73 glyph paths
      and all six rows read GLYPHS-DIFFER.
    * GlyphOutlineSkylineTests in the Engine suite carries the correctness claim
      -- four cases, expectations computed from upstream's own
      max (2, chord / 0.2) with the chord measured AFTER the transform, plus the
      scale control and a curve-free glyph whose count must NOT move with scale.
      ⚠ The curve-free glyph was MEASURED, and the first choice was wrong:
      accidentals.sharp looks straight-edged and carries twenty curves.  Exactly
      ten glyphs in emmentaler-20 have no curves; noteheads.sM1kievan is the one
      with the most segments.  A/B'd two ways -- with the max(2,...) floor removed
      and with the chord measured before the transform -- and three of the four
      fail under each, the curve-free control correctly holding both times.

  The provenance was re-measured the same day rather than quoted: from the
  committed glyph-identity index, 277 of emmentaler-20's 662 outlines serialize
  identically between the builds and 385 do not; accidentals.sharp is identical
  (the control) while clefs.G and noteheads.s2 differ.

  /!\ REVISIT CONDITION -- CLOSED 2026-08-17 BY RULING R19.  This used to read
  "a reversal of the own-build font decision (D13/D23's neighbour) would close the
  rows against the oracle directly and retire the baseline; it would also change
  every drawn music glyph, and is not planned."  It is no longer merely unplanned:
  Jeremy RULED it out.  The port builds its own Emmentaler fonts from the Metafont
  sources, exactly as LilyPond builds its own from the same sources, and the engine
  must be correct against THE FONTS THE PORT SHIPS.  Do not re-propose a swap.
  See the "PARITY 24" section below for what the decision obliges.

UNICODE SPACE FALLBACK: HARFBUZZ'S RULE, MEASURED (2026-08-15, PARITY 14 -- D38)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Fonts/UnicodeSpaceFallback.cs + Fonts/TextFontMetric.cs

  Upstream measures text through Pango, and Pango through HarfBuzz, which does
  NOT draw .notdef for a Unicode space character a font cannot map: it
  substitutes the ordinary SPACE glyph and then REWRITES the advance from the
  character's space type.  None of the 24 vendored text faces covers U+200A, so
  before this the port measured a hair space as C059's .notdef -- 278 units,
  exactly as wide as an ordinary space -- and format-sign-with-number, which
  puts TWO hair spaces between the two signs of a numbered \segnoMark, set them
  2 x 0.4780 too far apart.  Worth 11 comparator rows.

  THE TABLE IS MEASURED, NOT COPIED.  No HarfBuzz source was read; each entry
  was read off the pinned oracle (trap 8b) by setting the character between two
  music glyphs and diffing the two glyph origins.  At the probe's size one em
  was 64 device dots and the oracle answered: U+2003/U+3000 64, U+2004 21,
  U+2005 16, U+2006 11, U+2009 13, U+200A 4, U+205F 14, U+2007 36 (a digit),
  U+2008 18 (a period), U+202F 9 (half a space).  All 16 space characters now
  agree with the oracle exactly.

  DELIBERATE LIMIT, and it is the part a future session should know about.
  UnicodeSpaceFallback.Canonicalize applies the canonical decompositions
  U+2000 -> U+2002 and U+2001 -> U+2003 and NOTHING ELSE.  HarfBuzz normalizes
  generally; the port normalizes the two characters it has MEASURED, because
  those two change which GLYPH answers (C059 has a real uni2002 at 556 units,
  wider than the half em the fallback rule alone would give, and the oracle
  draws it for U+2000), while the rest of the corpus agrees without any
  normalization.  A general normalizer would be reasoned rather than evidenced.
  REVISIT CONDITION: a residue row whose text contains a composed character the
  oracle appears to decompose.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE MUSIC-FONT RUN IS A glyph-string (2026-08-15, PARITY 11 -- D36)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily/pango-font.cc pango_item_string_stencil  ->  Objects/TextInterface.cs
  scm/output-svg.scm glyph-string/extract-glyph/dump-path
                                                ->  Backends/SvgBackend.cs
  lily/stencil-integral.cc add_glyph_string_segments
                                                ->  Layout/StencilIntegral.cs

  WHAT WAS WRONG.  A run of text set in the MUSIC font (fetaText: \number,
  \dynamic, figured bass, time signatures, volta numbers, cue-clef digits) left
  TextInterface.MusicFontTextStencil as a PILE of separately translated
  named-glyph stencils.  Upstream's is ONE glyph-string expression, and the SVG
  backend draws it by placing each glyph on the PATH's own transform at the
  cumulative advance of the glyphs before it:

      translate(dx + next-horiz-adv, dy) scale(s, -s)

  falling back to a bare scale() when both terms are zero, which is why the
  FIRST glyph of every run is written exactly as a lone named-glyph is.  The
  global's own comment says it carries "the *cumulative* horizontal advance for
  glyph strings, but only if there is more than one glyph".

  The marks landed in the same places -- the geometry AGREED, to about 0.0125 --
  so nothing looked wrong.  What differed was the DOCUMENT: the port emitted the
  compound transform ZERO times across the 2,316-page corpus against the
  oracle's 3,019 on 179 pages, putting the translate on a parent group instead.
  compare-output.py counts a <path> as a font glyph only when its transform is a
  PURE scale(), so the same mark was graded as an engraver-drawn path on the
  reference side and as a named glyph on the candidate side, and 171 rows across
  seven unrelated-looking families read "missing path:Mcsssz / extra
  glyph:period".

  WHAT THE PORT'S glyph-string CARRIES, and where it diverges.  The shape is
  upstream's nine elements -- FONT, PostScript name, size, CID flag, the glyph
  list, file name, face index, the original text, the cluster map -- and the
  glyph list holds upstream's own per-glyph tuple,

      (width (down . up) x-offset y-offset glyph-index glyph-name)

  in run order.  Four fields hold what the port can say rather than what Pango
  would:

    * SIZE is the metric's own FontScaling, where upstream's is a Pango point
      size that output-svg divides by lily-unit-length.  The backend takes the
      drawing scale from the FONT, exactly as it does for a named-glyph, so the
      field is carried for shape and is not read.
    * FILE NAME, FACE INDEX and CLUSTERS are "", 0 and #f -- the values upstream
      itself writes when it has none.  They exist for PostScript/PDF font
      embedding and for PDF copy-and-paste; the SVG backend reads none of them.
    * A glyph the font can INDEX but cannot NAME stays in the list with a #f
      name, where upstream drops it.  Dropping it would drop its ADVANCE with it
      and move every glyph after it; upstream can afford to because its extent
      came from Pango, and the port's comes from the glyphs.

  WIDTH is the font's own advance with the kern to the next glyph folded in,
  which is what Pango's per-glyph geometry width already is (HarfBuzz applies a
  kern by adjusting the LEFT glyph's advance).  That is the same sum the pile of
  translated stencils used, so the run's EXTENT BOX is computed exactly as it
  was and only the expression changed -- which is what made the change
  attributable.

  THE SKYLINE WALK follows add_glyph_string_segments: cumulative_x over the
  widths, each glyph offset by its own (x-offset, y-offset) on top of it, then
  the font scale, then the real outline.  Upstream's fallback to the glyph's
  KERNED BOUNDING BOX -- taken when the ratio of its two metric sources is not
  finite -- has no case to serve here for the same reason DIVERGENCE 1 of THE
  STENCIL EXPRESSION WALK records: the port has ONE source for both.

  FENCED by SvgGlyphTests (the cumulative placement over three glyphs, the
  wrapped/bare split, and a glyph with no outline that still advances) and by
  MusicFontRunEndToEndTests (\time 12/8 draws one compound transform and one
  group; \time 3/4 draws none of either -- both read off the pinned oracle
  before they were asserted).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A SHAPED RUN'S EXTENT COMES FROM TWO RECTANGLES (2026-08-15, PARITY 11)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily/pango-font.cc pango_item_string_stencil  ->  Objects/TextInterface.cs
  lily/include/pango-font.hh PANGO_RESOLUTION   ->  Objects/TextInterface.cs

  Upstream builds a shaped run's box in ONE expression out of TWO sources:

      Box (Interval (PANGO_LBEARING (logical_rect), PANGO_RBEARING (logical_rect)),
           Interval (-PANGO_DESCENT (ink_rect),     PANGO_ASCENT (ink_rect)))

  X from the LOGICAL rectangle -- where the pen starts and stops -- and Y from
  the INK rectangle, what the outlines actually cover.  TextFontMetric has done
  this since the text-interface group; MusicFontTextStencil used the union of the
  DECLARED per-glyph boxes (the LILC table) for BOTH axes.

  WHY THAT IS NOT THE SAME NUMBER.  Emmentaler's LILC table declares ONE height
  for every digit and the font DRAWS them a few units apart -- `fattened.one' is
  about five thousandths of an em taller than `fattened.two'.  A run of digits
  therefore reported the same height whichever digits it held, and
  Volta_bracket_interface::print's `num.align_to (Y_AXIS, UP)' put two runs of
  "different" height at the same baseline.  0.0125 staff spaces at a default
  staff size, on 46 comparator rows -- the largest constant cluster since D0's
  227.

  FontMetric.GetIndexedInkDimensions is new for this and runs the glyph's
  charstring (Fonts/CffFont.GlyphBox).  A LONE named-glyph is still measured by
  the DECLARED box, because that is what upstream's Font_metric does for one --
  the asymmetry is upstream's and is deliberate.

  AND THE ADVANCES NEEDED D10's DEVICE-DOT GRID.  Upstream shapes a music-font
  run through Pango exactly as it shapes a text one, so Pango rounds each shaped
  advance with PANGO_UNITS_ROUND and the logical rectangle is a sum of WHOLE dots.
  TextFontMetric has rounded since PARITY 5; this path summed exact reals.  The
  kern is INSIDE the rounding, as it is there, and for the same reason: the
  shaper had already folded the kern into the pair's first advance.

  MEASURED.  `\markup \box \number \fontsize #6' over "1.", "2.", "1", "2" now
  reproduces the pinned oracle's four boxes EXACTLY on both axes -- tops
  -4.3240 / -4.3000 / -4.3240 / -4.3000 and widths 4.0826 / 4.4923 / 2.9559 /
  3.4680.  MATCH 1,871 -> 2,015 with 146 improvements and no regressions; the
  whole sub-0.02 near-miss tail went with it.

  ⚠ THE PORT'S FONTS ARE NOT BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE ORACLE'S (the md5 of both the
  OTF and the SVG font differ), and the note under THE STENCIL EXPRESSION WALK
  records that the port's own build's outline bounding boxes can differ from the
  official release by one to three units of a thousand-unit em.  That is the same
  order as the defect being fixed, so the fix was VERIFIED against the oracle's
  own numbers rather than reasoned about.  Fenced by
  MusicFontRunEndToEndTests.a_music_font_run_is_measured_by_its_ink_and_its_whole_device_dots,
  which carries those eight numbers with the two controls that make them mean
  something.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE ELEVENTH PER-FILE LEAK: after-session-hook (2026-08-15, PARITY 11)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  scm/lily.scm session-terminate  ->  CodeBrix.LilyPort/LilyPondInit.cs

  Upstream's per-file cleanup is `session-terminate', and it does three things:
  restore the modules recorded at session-init, restore every recorded
  declaration, and RUN `after-session-hook'.  The port drives its own
  RestoreDefaults for the first two and ran nothing for the third, so five resets
  the vendored layer registers with `call-after-session' had never fired:

      ly/toc-init.ly              toc-hashtab, toc-alist, toc-name-id-hashtab
      scm/music-functions.scm     reset-tag-groups
      scm/define-event-classes.scm  ancestor-lookup-initialize
      ly/declarations-init.ly     default-fret-table, chord-shape-table,
                                  musicQuotes

  The visible cost was a table of contents that accumulated every earlier file's
  entries: page-label-loose-column listed a "Second part" and a "Third part" that
  belong to a different regression file entirely.

  Registering a reset and never calling it is trap 17a's shape one more time --
  the write side is faithful, complete and greppable, and the CALLER is missing.
  Recorded as trap 16b: when the port replaces an upstream lifecycle function
  with its own, enumerate what the original did LAST as carefully as what it did
  first.

delay-stencil-evaluation IS FORCED (2026-08-15, PARITY 11)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  lily/stencil-interpret.cc:35  ->  Layout/StencilInterpreter.cs

  Upstream: `interpret_stencil_expression (scm_force (scm_cadr (expr)), ...)'.
  The port interpreted the second element AS IS, under a comment saying "the
  promise is forced upstream; the port carries the value directly".  It does not
  and could not: `delay-stencil-evaluation' is built by VENDORED Scheme --
  \page-ref, \tocItem, the \on-the-fly page predicates -- and vendored Scheme
  writes a real `(delay ...)'.  Interpreting the promise OBJECT as an expression
  matches no head and draws NOTHING, so \page-ref printed its trailing text and
  no page number at all.

  SchemeUtilities.Force is new for it and caches on the promise, which is not an
  optimisation: the same expression is walked by the drawing pass, the skyline
  pass and the footnote collector, and a promise that recomputed would be free to
  answer differently each time.  paper-system.cc's own get_footnotes deliberately
  does NOT force -- its "we likely need to do something here...just don't know
  what..." comment is upstream's and is reproduced verbatim.

TEXT FONT SIZE, LAST DECIMAL: A BOUNDED DELTA IS ACCEPTED ON FOUR NAMED VALUES
(2026-08-16, ruling R10 -- D39)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Fonts/FontInterface.cs (QuantizeToPangoUnits), Fonts/TextFontMetric.cs
  (DescriptionString)  ->  the SVG `font-size' attribute.

  ACCEPTED, BY RULING, ON THESE FOUR FILES AND THESE FOUR VALUES ONLY:

      file                         oracle    port      delta
      ---------------------------  --------  --------  ------
      markup-note-sizes            1.1666    1.1668    0.0002
      page-layout-bottom-padding   1.7460    1.7465    0.0005
      fret-diagrams-size           1.9800    1.9797    0.0003
      tablature-full-notation      2.2000    2.1997    0.0003

  The acceptance is a BOUNDED DELTA of 0.0005, not a frozen pair: the port's
  value may move within 0.0005 of the oracle's and remain accepted; beyond that
  it is a regression and must be chased.

  /!\ THIS IS NOT A GENERAL TOLERANCE, AND IT DOES NOT TOUCH D29.  D29 forbids
  visual and tolerance comparison and states that "a page ever blocked by
  micro-geometry gets its own ruling that day".  This IS that ruling, for four
  named values, and it is the mechanism D29 provides rather than an exception to
  it.  Any other size, any other file, any other element: D29 stands unchanged.
  A future session must not read this entry as licence to widen it.

  WHAT IS KNOWN.  Upstream's SVG backend harvests the font size out of the Pango
  DESCRIPTION STRING -- scm/output-svg.scm:154-156 matches it with the regexp
  `[ -]([0-9.]+)$' and divides by lily-unit-length, which framework-svg.scm:93
  sets once per output FILE from the paper's output-scale.  The string round trip
  is upstream's own construction, not a port shortcut, and SvgBackend.EmitText
  reproduces it with the same expression.  The port's side is fully understood:
  quantize to a whole number of 1/1024ths (PANGO_SCALE, away from zero), format
  to three decimals, divide, print four.  That model reproduces all four of the
  port's values exactly.

  WHAT IS NOT KNOWN, AND WHY THE ROWS WERE RULED RATHER THAN FIXED.  The oracle's
  printed sizes cannot be reproduced by ANY fixed-precision formatting rule over
  that lattice -- tested two independent ways at PARITY 14 and again on
  2026-08-16.  Three decimals, six significant digits (%g) and full precision
  each reproduce SOME of the corpus values and not others; on
  tablature-full-notation three of the four sizes match a three-decimal value and
  are unreachable at six significant digits, while the fourth is the reverse.
  Solving for the unit length from the oracle's own values, rather than assuming
  the port's, leaves fret-diagrams-size and markup-note-sizes consistent with no
  unit length at all.  The leading hypothesis is that
  pango_font_description_to_string uses a SHORTEST-ROUND-TRIP formatter, which
  emits a variable number of digits and would explain the whole pattern including
  its signature -- exactly one size per file diverges while every other size in
  the same file is exact.  That is a hypothesis and NOT a measurement.

  /!\ AND ONE COMMENT IN THE PORT IS WRONG ABOUT THIS.  DescriptionString's
  comment states that pango_font_description_to_string "formats the description's
  size to three decimals" and that the format was "MEASURED against the pinned
  oracle across 31 font sizes ... reproduces every one".  It cannot be right:
  the corpus holds four counterexamples, and no three-decimal value divides to
  the oracle's 2.2000 on tablature-full-notation at any plausible unit length.
  Trap 26 -- a port comment about upstream is not evidence of what upstream does.
  The comment is to be corrected whenever this area is next touched.

  ✅ HONOURED BY THE COMPARATOR SINCE PARITY 15 (2026-08-16), as a POST-PASS --
  ruling R12's shape, not a change to D29's identity function, which stays
  byte-exact.  compare-output.py grades normally and then, on these four files
  only, asks whether the ENTIRE inventory difference is text elements identical
  in family and content whose sizes differ by no more than the bound; if so it
  RE-GRADES the reconciled inventory and reports the upgrade per row and as a
  count.  Re-grading rather than asserting MATCH is deliberate: a placement
  difference on these four files still reads PLACEMENT-DIFFERS.  All four reached
  MATCH, 0 regressions, one transition kind.  Fenced by --selftest (ix) through
  (xiii) and A/B'd with the post-pass fully disabled, which returns all four to
  GLYPHS-DIFFER and fails case (ix) while its four controls hold.

  REVISIT CONDITION.  Measure what pango_font_description_to_string actually
  emits, by driving the library rather than reasoning about it -- the technique
  that settled D43 (~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-glyph-skyline/ft_decompose.py
  drives libfreetype the same way).  The oracle bundles Pango 1.57.0 and this
  host carries 1.56.3, so rest STRUCTURAL claims on such a probe and not
  last-digit numeric ones (the caveat D43's probe carries for FreeType).  If the
  rule is measured and the four values can be made exact, make them exact and
  retire this entry; it exists because the cause is unmeasured, not because the
  divergence is desirable.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A NEGATIVE ZERO COORDINATE IS ACCEPTED WHERE UPSTREAM REACHES AN EXACT ZERO
(2026-08-16, ruled by Jeremy alongside R9)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Backends/SvgBackend.cs -- coordinate emission.

  THE SHAPE, exactly as ruled.  Where BOTH engines print `-0.0000' for a
  coordinate on one axis, and the port prints `-0.0000' on the other axis while
  upstream prints `0', the port's `-0.0000' is ACCEPTED.

  MEASURED on show-skylines.svg while diagnosing D43: five of the skyline debug
  segments carry y1/y2 = "0" on the oracle and "-0.0000" on the port, and on the
  SAME elements both engines print "-0.0000" for x1/x2 (7 occurrences each side)
  and "0.0000" for one more.  So this is NOT a formatting-rule divergence -- the
  two agree on how a signed near-zero is written.  Upstream simply arrives at an
  exact zero on that axis where the port arrives at a tiny negative value.

  IT COSTS NOTHING THE COMPARATOR GRADES, and that is verified rather than
  assumed: compare-output.py reads every coordinate through `_number', which is
  a plain `float()', so "0" and "-0.0000" both parse to 0.0 and compare equal.
  The six skyline rows' verdicts turn on the NUMBER of <line> elements, not on
  this.

  /!\ SCOPE.  This was observed on ONE file, during an unrelated diagnosis, and
  was NOT found by a sweep -- so it is an acceptance of the SHAPE described
  above, not a measured count of how often the shape occurs.  A session that
  wants the count must sweep for it.  It is also not licence to accept a signed
  zero anywhere the two engines DISAGREE about how near-zero is formatted; that
  would be a different divergence and is not covered here.

  REVISIT CONDITION: if a residue cluster is ever attributed to a sign or a
  near-zero coordinate, re-open this -- the acceptance is about the printed
  representation, and says nothing about whether the port SHOULD be reaching a
  negative value on that axis.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE MISSING-GLYPH WARNING, AND WHERE ITS UNICODE NAMES LIVE
(2026-08-16, PARITY 15 -- D40)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Fonts/MissingGlyphWarning.cs, Fonts/TextFontMetric.cs (TextStencil),
  Objects/TextInterface.cs (MusicFontTextStencil).

  Upstream warns from Pango_font::get_glyph_desc (lily/pango-font.cc:253):

      no glyph for character '%s' (U+%04X%s) in font `%s'

  The port drew the tofu D23 asks for and said nothing, on BOTH paths -- the
  text one, where a code point no face in the chain covers falls through to
  .notdef, and the MUSIC one, where an unmapped code point was dropped from the
  run without a word.  Both now warn.  The message is upstream's verbatim
  (standing rule 15 / ruling R1), including the third field's leading SPACE
  before the name and its complete absence when there is none.

  THE NAMES COME FROM CodeBrix.LilyScheme, WHICH IS WHERE GUILE PUTS THEM.
  char->formal-name is (ice-9 unicode), implemented in libguile/unicode.c over
  GNU libunistring; LilyScheme now provides that module from C# for the same
  reason it provides (ice-9 iconv) and (ice-9 soft-ports), and carries the
  34,828-name table derived from UnicodeData.txt 15.1.0.  Both procedures are
  bound, not just the one this warning needs.  See that repository's
  THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt section 6.

  /!\ THE TABLE WAS BRIEFLY IN THE ENGINE AND WAS MOVED (2026-08-16, Jeremy).
  The argument for the Engine was proportion -- one consumer, and LilyScheme's
  whole package is smaller than the table.  The argument that won is that the
  procedure is GUILE'S, not LilyPond's: upstream reaches it through
  lily-imports.cc as an ordinary Scheme import, so an Engine-side copy is a
  layering inversion, and it would collide with (ice-9 unicode) the moment
  LilyScheme grew one (rule 13).  Proportion also stops applying once LilyScheme
  is meant to be a general-purpose Scheme engine, which is the standing intent.

  THE PORT CALLS THE MANAGED ACCESSOR, not the Scheme procedure.  Upstream goes
  through Scheme because Guile's C API is what it has; the port's Engine already
  links the library that owns the table, so it calls
  UnicodeCharacterNames.Of directly.  That is the same table by a shorter road,
  and it does not require an interpreter to be up at the moment a text metric is
  taken.  The Scheme binding exists for Scheme callers, exactly as Guile's does.

  WHAT STAYS IN THE ENGINE: the ZERO-WIDTH exclusion, because it is PANGO's
  behaviour and not Guile's.  Upstream returns from get_glyph_desc before the
  warning for anything Pango answers PANGO_GLYPH_EMPTY to -- "valid Unicode but
  don't have associated glyphs", in its own comment -- and the port spells that
  as Unicode's Default_Ignorable_Code_Point, seventeen merged ranges in
  Fonts/MissingGlyphWarning.cs.  MEASURED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is what makes
  it a rule and not a patch for seven characters: all seven the port over-warned
  about are default-ignorable, and NONE of the 79 the oracle warns about is.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE MISSING-GLYPH WARNING NAMES A DIFFERENT FACE ON pdf-copy-paste
(2026-08-16, PARITY 15 -- D40's remainder, ACCEPTED)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/!\ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 BY R14, AND THE ACCEPTANCE BELOW NO LONGER APPLIES.
    R14 made an unknown family resolve to TeX GYRE SCHOLA rather than to the head
    of the serif chain, so the port now names texgyreschola-regular.otf and BOTH
    ENGINES AGREE -- see "(3) THE DIAGNOSTICS ROW IT CLOSED" in the PARITY 20
    section below, which took diagnostics 2138 -> 2139.  The row is graded MATCH
    and the diagnostics gate is at 2,146/2,146.  What survives from this note is
    the SVG row only: pdf-copy-paste is still GLYPHS-DIFFER and still on
    g1-skip-list.tsv under D31, for the tofu reason and not for this one.
    Kept rather than deleted because the MEASUREMENT below is still the evidence
    for how the family resolves (trap 18: a note that stops being true is not
    re-checked by anything, so it is marked where it stands).

  Of the 316 occurrences in the reference logs, 310 name C059-Roman.otf and 4
  name emmentaler-20.otf; the port reproduces both.  The remaining TWO, both in
  pdf-copy-paste, name texgyreschola-regular.otf and the port names
  C059-Roman.otf.

  MEASURED rather than reasoned (fc-match under the corpus's own pinning, trap
  8b): that file overrides fonts.serif to "Noto Serif JP", a family no bundled
  face provides, and fontconfig's best match for an unknown family under
  reference-fonts.conf is TeX Gyre Schola -- the SECOND face of the serif chain,
  not the first.

      "Noto Serif JP"            -> texgyreschola-regular.otf
      "serif"                    -> C059-Roman.otf
      "Linux Libertine O, serif" -> C059-Roman.otf

  The port does not model fontconfig at all, by design: D23 fixes the text faces
  to the 24 vendored ones and an unrecognised family resolves to the serif chain,
  whose first face is C059-Roman.  So the port really does draw that text from
  C059-Roman, and its message names the face it actually used.  Making it print
  texgyreschola-regular would be naming a font the port never asked for.

  This is the same root fact as D31, whose ruling already makes pdf-copy-paste a
  permanent documented divergence on the SVG side.  REVISIT CONDITION: the
  deferred optional-font-packages decision, or any decision to model fontconfig
  family matching -- neither is planned.
