THREE FINDINGS MEASURED AT PARITY 15 AND DELIBERATELY NOT CHASED
(2026-08-16 -- each has its entry point and its revisit condition)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  None of these was in the GO'd scope with a measured row impact, so each is
  recorded rather than fixed.  All three were found while working on something
  else, which is the usual way (trap 28).

  (1) Layout/PageLayoutSpacing.cs -- GetSpacingSpec IGNORES ITS pure/start/end
      ARGUMENTS.  Upstream's Page_layout_problem::get_spacing_spec reads every
      one of its twelve properties through get_maybe_pure_property (before,
      "...", pure, start, end); the port reads all twelve with a plain
      GetProperty, so the `pure' parameter is accepted and discarded.  This is
      trap 24's family, and 24a is the reason it is worth writing down rather
      than dismissing: an unpure read in a pure path can cost a SIDE EFFECT
      rather than a wrong number.  UNMEASURED for row impact.  ⚠ It is NOT the
      annotate-spacing divergence below -- paper-system-annotate calls
      ly:get-spacing-spec with two arguments, so pure is #f on both engines
      there.

  (2) Objects/AccidentalInterface.cs -- the horizontal-skylines callback reads
      `glyph-name' as `me.GetProperty (...) is MutableString', where upstream
      writes from_scm<std::string>.  Trap 12a exactly: a property's string may be
      a CLR string as well as a MutableString, and the type pattern silently
      answers the empty string for one of the two shapes, which would skip the
      flat/doubleflat stem-padding branch.  PARITY 10 swept the Engine for this
      shape and found five sites; this one reads a property into a C# pattern
      rather than comparing with `is string', which is why the sweep's scanner
      did not name it.  Use SchemeUtilities.IsString/StringText.  UNMEASURED.

  (3) annotate-spacing DRAWS FOUR STAFF-PADDING ANNOTATIONS THE ORACLE DOES NOT,
      and the divergence is NARROWED to two candidates.  MEASURED on
      page-spacing-nonstaff-lines-between: the oracle draws ZERO "padding"
      annotations and the port draws four -- one per adjacent pair of the three
      vertical groups, twice, once per system.  scm/paper-system.scm's
      annotate-padding ends `(if (> padding 0.0) annotation empty-stencil)' and
      is VENDORED, so the guard itself is faithful; what differs is its input.
      Two candidates, both port-side and both cheap to separate by printing them:
      ly:get-spacing-spec answering an alist whose `padding' is positive where
      upstream's is absent or zero, or ly:system::get-staves answering a LONGER
      list (the map is over adjacent pairs, so one extra staff is one extra
      annotation).  Worth 2 rows -- page-spacing-nonstaff-lines-between and
      page-spacing-nonstaff-lines-header-padding.


================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- CONDITIONAL ITEMS, ly:font-name's DEFAULT, AND A DEVICE-DOT TIE
================================================================================

PARITY 16 (2026-08-16).


A TIED ACCIDENTAL IS A BREAK REMINDER AND RESERVES NO WIDTH -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Paper_column_engraver::stop_translation_timestep sorts each timestep's items
THREE ways, and the port had only one of the three:

    if (has_interface<Accidental_placement> (elem)
        || has_interface<Arpeggio> (elem))
      Separation_item::add_conditional_item (col, elem);
    else if (!has_interface<Accidental_interface> (elem))
      Separation_item::add_item (col, elem);

An accidental placement and an arpeggio are CONDITIONAL: whether they occupy
space depends on the column they are measured against, so they go on
`conditional-elements', and Separation_item::boxes filters them through
Accidental_placement::get_relevant_accidentals when it is given a left grob.
That function splits on

    unsmob<Grob> (get_object (a, "tie")) && !from_scm<bool> (get_property (a, "forced"))

-- a tied, unforced accidental is a break REMINDER, and a reminder is only paid
for when it starts a line, where it is actually shown.  An individual Accidental
goes on NEITHER list, because the placement already accounts for it.

The port put every item on `elements', so the filter was never reached and the
width of an accidental that is never drawn was reserved in every column.

WHY IT SURVIVED: the port's own comment at the call site said
add_conditional_item was "not ported, and paired with the skyline half that
Paper_column::minimum_distance also omits".  BOTH halves had landed in earlier
sessions -- SeparationItem.AddConditionalItem exists and three other engravers
already call it, and PaperColumn.MinimumDistance already merges
SeparationItem.ConditionalSkyline -- and nothing re-checked the note.  Trap 18.

MEASURED before the fix, by an A/B on the INPUT (trap 30b), three renders on
each engine, music width of two bars of cis sixteenths in 1/4:

    case                        oracle      port      delta
    tied (unforced)            31.5204   32.6704    +1.1500
    untied                     31.8204   31.8204     0.0000
    tied but forced (cis!)     31.8204        --          --
    tied, no accidental (c)    29.9204   29.9204     0.0000

so the defect needed BOTH a tie and an accidental, and the port placed the tied
note where it would go if its accidental were printed.  Worth 10 rows, all
PLACEMENT-DIFFERS -> MATCH with no other transition: accidental-tie,
accidental-unbroken-tie-spacing, spacing-accidental-tie,
trill-spanner-pitched-consecutive, spacing-horizontal-skyline,
mensural-ligatures, slur-scoring and the THREE arpeggio rows -- the arpeggio
rows are the other half of the same upstream branch and are what confirms the
attribution independently.

Fenced by TiedAccidentalSpacingEndToEndTests, on the regression file's own
material (rule 35b), asserting relationships read off the pinned oracle first.

WARNING FOR ANYONE READING THE COMPARATOR ON THESE ROWS: their worst deltas were
150.7218 and 152.6218, and NONE of that was the defect.  The drift is a flat
+1.1500 from one column onward; the 150 is the comparator pairing a staff mark
with the TAGLINE at the bottom of the page once the drift re-sorts the list.
Trap 7.  ~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity16/drift.py pairs marks WITHIN a
y-band and reports the honest per-column delta.


ly:font-name ANSWERS "unknown" FOR A TEXT FONT -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Font_metric::font_name () has a BODY upstream -- it returns the literal string
"unknown" -- and exactly one class overrides it, Open_type_font, with the face's
PostScript name; Modified_font_metric forwards to what it wraps, and Pango_font
does not override at all.

The port declared FontMetric.FontName ABSTRACT, which threw the base body away
and obliged each subclass to invent an answer.  TextFontMetric answered its own
DESCRIPTION.  That is not a cosmetic difference: ly/property-init.ly's
cross-style -- the callback behind \xNotesOn -- asks the layout for a latin1 font
and tests

    (string=? (ly:font-name font) "unknown")

to find out whether it was handed a text font rather than a music one.  When it
was, it clears font-name and sets font-family to `music', and that switch is the
only reason a cross notehead is drawn at all.  In the port the comparison was
false, the switch never ran, and dead-notes lost ten noteheads.s2cross glyphs.

FontName is now virtual with upstream's default and TextFontMetric's override is
gone.  It is deliberately NOT what a font PRINTS as -- upstream's print_smob
writes description_, not font_name () -- and the docs run was checked before the
change: the only "Font_metric" string in the 19 generated files is the predicate
name in ly:font-metric?, so nothing graded moves.

Fenced by FontNameDefaultTests, with the music font as the control.


THE DEVICE-DOT GRID HAS A TIE, AND NEITHER NAIVE RULE RESOLVES IT -- CLOSED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

NEW at PARITY 16, MEASURED, and deliberately NOT landed.  This is a real defect
with a precisely located mechanism and no correct fix yet; it is D44 in the plan.

/!\ CLOSED AT PARITY 21.  The rule is HarfBuzz's, it was READ off the library
rather than reasoned, and it is recorded under THE HARFBUZZ MULTIPLIER IS TAKEN IN
FLOAT below.  Everything in this section is still true about the SHAPE of the
pipeline; what it could not supply was the multiplier.


TEXT ADVANCES LAND ON PANGO'S DEVICE-DOT GRID (above) is right about the grid and
wrong about how the grid is REACHED.  The port rounds once, in floating point:

    advance += Math.Floor (step / pixel + 0.5) * pixel;

Pango rounds twice.  HarfBuzz hands Pango a glyph advance as an INTEGER in Pango
units (PANGO_SCALE = 1024 to a device unit), computed by hb's own fixed-point
scaling, and Pango then rounds that integer with

    #define PANGO_UNITS_ROUND(d) (((d) + (PANGO_SCALE >> 1)) & ~(PANGO_SCALE - 1))

read out of pango/pango-types.h on this machine, not recalled.

THE DIVERGENCE IS EXACTLY ONE DOT AND IT IS ISOLATED TO A GLYPH AND A SIZE.  A
prefix ladder -- every prefix of "Symbols" set as `\line { PREFIX "|" }' at three
font sizes, run unchanged on both engines, 21 rows -- puts the whole difference
on ONE cell: the glyph `m' (C059, 889/1000 em) at font size 1.5552, where the
oracle gives 41 dots and the port gives 40.  Every other glyph and every other
size agrees exactly.  The generator is
~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity16/ab/prefix-ladder.ly.

TWO CANDIDATE RULES WERE IMPLEMENTED AND BOTH ARE REFUTED, by full sweep:

 (1) Reproducing the two roundings literally -- quantize to integer Pango units
     with round-half-up, then PANGO_UNITS_ROUND -- puts the carry boundary at
     41471.5/1024 = 40.49951171875 dots, a little BELOW the half dot.  It fixes
     eight rows (markup-finger-figuredbass-fontsize, midi-pedal, pedal-bracket,
     repeat-dc-al-coda, repeat-ds-al-coda, repeat-ds-mark-override,
     repeat-ds-return-count, repeat-ds-torture) and REGRESSES eight
     (header-book-multiple, header-book-multiplescores, -1, header-bookpart-
     multiple, header-score-multiple, header-score-reordered,
     header-toplevel-multiple, lyric-repeat-count-format), each by the same one
     dot in the other direction.  Net zero, so it was reverted; the ratchet
     refused it and --force is never the fix.

 (2) Keeping the single rounding but nudging the tie by 1e-9, on the theory that
     the port only loses EXACT ties to floating-point representation error,
     reproduces the pre-existing behaviour on all sixteen rows.  So the eight
     improved rows are not ties: their exact values sit genuinely inside the
     window (40.49951, 40.5), which is where rule (1) carries and rule (2) does
     not.

WHAT THAT PROVES, AND IT IS THE USEFUL PART: the two sets of eight sit in the
SAME narrow window and need OPPOSITE answers, so no threshold on the exact real
advance can separate them.  The rule is not a rounding boundary at all -- it is
hb's INTEGER arithmetic, hb_font_t::em_mult, a 64-bit fixed-point multiply
against a mult derived from the quantized em size:

    x_mult = ((int64_t) x_scale << 16) / upem
    advance = (v * x_mult + 0x8000) >> 16

and the answer depends on the integer font-unit advance v and the integer scale,
not on their real product.  Modelling that is the next attempt; the port already
quantizes the requested SIZE to Pango units (FontInterface.QuantizeToPangoUnits),
so that half is in place.

REVISIT: worth 8 rows at least, and possibly the remaining sub-0.02 tail.  Do not
attempt it without re-running the prefix ladder first -- it is the cheapest
instrument this question has, and it names the failing cell in one run.


THE annotate-spacing PADDING -- NARROWED AGAIN, ONE CANDIDATE ELIMINATED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The previous entry offered two candidates.  MEASURED at PARITY 16, the second is
ELIMINATED: ly:system::get-staves is NOT answering a longer list.  On
page-spacing-nonstaff-lines-between the port draws exactly four extra "padding"
annotations, which is two adjacent pairs of THREE vertical groups, twice, once
per system -- the count upstream's own list length predicts.  The extra
annotations read 0.50, and 0.50 is the Lyrics context's own
nonstaff-relatedstaff-spacing padding (ly/engraver-init.ly), which is the alist
the RELATED branch of Page_layout_problem::get_spacing_spec returns and the
branch both engines should take for these pairs.

So the port takes the right branch, reads the right alist, and the ORACLE somehow
answers a padding of 0.0 for the same pair -- the oracle draws no padding
annotation anywhere in either file, including for the two adjacent Staff groups
in page-spacing-nonstaff-lines-header-padding, whose staff-staff-spacing carries
a default padding of 1.  That is the shape to chase: something makes upstream's
spec EMPTY (get_spacing_spec's own `!before || !after' arm returns a stretchability
-only alist, whose padding is absent) rather than making its padding zero.

Note also, and separately, that PageLayoutSpacing.GetSpacingSpec still accepts
`pure'/`start'/`end' and DISCARDS them where upstream reads all twelve of its
properties through get_maybe_pure_property.  That is NOT this defect -- the
Scheme binding ly:get-spacing-spec passes pure = #f, so the two reads coincide on
this path -- but it is live for the three PURE callers.


A TIME SIGNATURE IN A TEXT FONT: THE `text' STENCIL HEAD IS UNPORTED -- OPEN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

NEW at PARITY 16, root-caused, not fixed.  font-name.ly overrides
Staff.TimeSignature.font-name to "Linux Libertine O", and the row is a defect on
BOTH gates at once: the SVG carries two extra text elements ("3" and "4") and the
diagnostics gate is missing the oracle's two "cannot find SVG font #f" warnings.
One defect, two symptoms, and the count matches exactly -- two digits.

scm/output-svg.scm's `fontify' dispatches on the type of its first argument: a
STRING is a Pango description and goes to pango-description-to-text, which sets
real text; a font SMOB goes to font-smob-to-text, which builds a file name out of
(font-name-style font) -- and font-name-style is (string-downcase (ly:font-name
font)), which for a text font is now "unknown" -- then asks ly:find-file for
"unknown.svg", fails, warns, and draws NOTHING.

The port drives its own C# backend, which has heads for named-glyph, glyph-string,
utf-8-string and url-link but none for `text', so whatever reaches it is emitted
as ordinary text.  The fix is in which stencil expression the engine builds for a
music grob whose font resolves to a text face, and then in the backend honouring
it.  Worth 1 comparator row and 1 diagnostics row.


A SHARED GLYPH-BOX CACHE WAS UNGUARDED -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CffFont.GlyphBox memoized into a plain Dictionary with no lock.  A face is parsed
once and kept for the life of the process -- AllFontMetrics caches it behind a
Gate, and every TextFace holds one -- so the memo is reachable from as many
threads as touch the font.  The SWEEP never does (BatchDriver engraves one file
at a time in one process, which is why this cost nothing for the life of the
port), but the test suite does, because xUnit runs test classes in parallel, and
a torn insert threw "Operations that change non-concurrent collections must have
exclusive access" out of an unrelated font test.

Guarded with the same idiom AllFontMetrics already used.  It cannot change any
measurement: a glyph's box is a deterministic function of its index, so
serializing the memo only serializes it, and computing one twice is harmless.
_boxCache was the only state CffFont mutates after construction.

Fenced by CffGlyphBoxCacheTests, which hammers a COLD cache from every core and
requires every reader to agree with a single-threaded run.  A/B'd: the fence
fails 3 of 3 runs with the lock removed and passes 3 of 3 with it.

================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- A MUSIC STRING IN A TEXT FACE, AND AN UPSTREAM MISSPELLING
================================================================================

PARITY 17 (2026-08-16).


A MUSIC-ENCODED STRING SET IN A TEXT FACE DRAWS NOTHING -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`\override Staff.TimeSignature.font-name = "Linux Libertine O"' puts a fetaText
run into a Pango font, and the oracle then draws NEITHER digit and warns twice:

    warning: cannot find SVG font #f
    warning: cannot find SVG font #f

The port drew both digits as ordinary text.  One defect, two gates -- the
comparator row carried `extra text:Linux Libertine O:2.2000:3' and `:4', and the
diagnostics row carried the matching MISSING pair.

THE CHAIN, and it turns on WHICH copy of the encoding is read.  select_font
(font-select.cc:82) overwrites its own LOCAL `encoding' with `latin1' when
font-name is a string, and answers a Pango font.  Text_interface::interpret_string
does NOT reuse that local: it reads font-encoding from PROPS again and computes

    bool is_music = scm_is_true (scm_memq (encoding, music_encodings));   // :240
    return fm->text_stencil (layout, str, is_music, features_str);        // :241

so the grob's own fetaText survives and the run reaches text_stencil as a MUSIC
string.  Pango_font::text_stencil then applies its final guard

    if (!scm_is_null (dest.expr ())
        && (!music_string || !music_strings_to_paths))                    // :574

-- the SVG backend sets music-strings-to-paths, so for a music string BOTH terms
are false, the utf-8-string encapsulation is SKIPPED, and the expression stays the
raw per-glyph drawing.  output-svg.scm's glyph-string draws that through
music-string-to-path, which asks ly:find-file for `<font-name-style>.svg' and, on
failure, warns and returns unspecified.  LilyPond ships .svg companions for the
EMMENTALER faces ALONE -- the pinned oracle's font tree has nine, all
emmentaler-* -- so the lookup fails for every text face.  The run keeps its shaped
extents; only the drawing is lost.

WHAT THE PORT HAD.  TextInterface.InterpretString dispatched on the C# TYPE of the
selected font (`font is TextFontMetric') instead of on the encoding.  The two
agree whenever the encoding and the font kind agree and disagree exactly when
font-name forces a text font under a music encoding -- trap 12a's shape one level
up, a C# type test standing in for an upstream property read.

⚠ AND THE PREDICATE WAS ALREADY THERE, WITH NO CALLERS.
TextInterface.IsMusicEncoded is a faithful port of line 240, complete with its
doc comment, and NOTHING IN THE ENGINE CALLED IT.  Trap 17a again: the read side
is perfect and no one asks it.  A grep for the mechanism finds it and looks
reassuring.

Three changes.  InterpretString computes musicString = IsMusicEncoded(props) and
passes it down; TextFontMetric.TextStencil takes it and skips the utf-8-string
wrapper exactly where upstream skips it; and SvgBackend gains a `glyph-outline'
head that warns `cannot find SVG font #f' and draws nothing.  A bare
glyph-outline reaches a backend ONLY for this case -- TextFontMetric is the head's
only producer and it leaves a run unwrapped only when the run is a music string --
so the head needs no further test.  The message prints `#f' rather than the file
name because upstream passes the failed RESULT to the warning
(output-svg.scm:291); that is upstream's and rule 2 keeps it.

MEASURED: font-name.svg GLYPHS-DIFFER -> PLACEMENT-DIFFERS, corpus-wide exactly 2
`cannot find SVG font' warnings, 0 regressions on a full sweep, and the
diagnostics gate 2,136 -> 2,137 with MISSING 2 -> 1.  The row does
not reach MATCH: it carries a residual 2.4242 on "Rest in DejaVu Sans Mono", which
was invisible before because an inventory mismatch never reaches placement
comparison.  That residue is the fallback-face class (D23/D31), not this defect.

Fenced by MusicStringOnTextFontTests (3 cases, 2 of them controls -- the latin1
run must STILL be wrapped, and the extents must be identical either way, because
upstream returns dest.extent_box() on both branches) and
SvgMusicStringFallbackTests (2 cases, 1 control).  A/B'd: with the wrapper skip
reverted the claim fails and both controls hold.


ly:system::get-staves ALWAYS ANSWERS THE EMPTY LIST -- UPSTREAM MISSPELLS ITS
OWN OBJECT, AND THE PORT HAD CORRECTED IT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

lily/system.cc reads the system's alignment object at seven places.  SIX spell it
`vertical-alignment'.  ONE -- get_maybe_spaceable_staves, system.cc:1023, which is
the body behind all three ly:system::get-*-staves callbacks -- spells it

    Grob *align = unsmob<Grob> (get_object (me, "vertical_alignment"));

with an UNDERSCORE.  Nothing declares that name and nothing writes it:
scm/define-grob-properties.scm declares `vertical-alignment' and
scm/define-grobs.scm gives System the ly:system::get-vertical-alignment callback
under the hyphen.  So `align' is null, the loop never runs, and get-staves,
get-spaceable-staves and get-nonspaceable-staves answer the EMPTY LIST for every
system LilyPond has ever engraved.

ITS ONE VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE IS annotate-spacing.  scm/paper-system.scm guards both
staff maps with `(< 1 (length ...))', so no staff-level spacing or padding
annotation is ever drawn.  The port had translated the function faithfully but
under the DECLARED name, so the callback fired, the alignment was found, and the
port drew four padding annotations per page that the oracle does not draw.

MEASURED, NOT INFERRED, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHAT SETTLED IT:

  - the pinned oracle draws ZERO `padding' annotations across ALL 2,316 reference
    pages;
  - annotate-spacing is nevertheless fully ACTIVE on those pages -- the oracle
    draws `basic-dist (system-system-spacing)', `basic-dist (top-system-spacing)',
    `basic-dist (last-bottom-spacing)', `bottom-margin' and `extent-estimate',
    which are the SYSTEM-level annotations and do not go through get-staves.

That pair is what rules out "annotate-spacing is simply off" and leaves the
misspelling as the only account.  Note the shape of the earlier dead end: the
padding VALUES the port computed were right -- Page_layout_problem::get_spacing_spec
returns the Lyrics' own nonstaff-relatedstaff-spacing, padding 0.5, on both pairs,
and the port read exactly that.  Chasing the value could never have found this,
because the divergence is one level up, in the LIST.

Reproduced under rule 2, which covers upstream's typos as explicitly as its dead
code.  The port now looks the alignment up under upstream's misspelled name, in
GetMaybeSpaceableStaves ONLY -- the port's five other reads correspond to
upstream's six hyphenated sites and keep the hyphen.  The body is left whole
rather than folded away, so that correcting one symbol restores the function on
the day upstream fixes it.

MEASURED: both rows GLYPHS-DIFFER -> MATCH directly, corpus 2,234 -> 2,236, 0
regressions, one transition kind.  The corpus has SIX files that set
annotate-spacing; the other four were MATCH before and after, which is the
control -- their oracle pages carry no padding annotation either, so the change
is inert wherever the annotation was already absent.

Fenced by VerticalOrganizationEngraverTests.
the_staves_callbacks_answer_empty_under_upstreams_misspelled_object, whose CONTROL
is the load-bearing half: the alignment IS reachable under the hyphenated name on
the same system, so the emptiness is the misspelling and not a system that never
grew an alignment.  A/B'd: the fence fails with the hyphen restored.


D44, THE DEVICE-DOT TIE -- A THIRD RULE REFUTED, AND THE FAMILY RE-SIZED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

STILL OPEN.  PARITY 16 implemented two candidate rules and refuted both by full
sweep; this session refuted a third, and did it by MEASUREMENT rather than by a
sweep, which is much cheaper.

THE INSTRUMENT.  ~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity17/hb_advance.py drives the
SYSTEM libharfbuzz through ctypes -- the same shape as PARITY 14's ft_decompose.py
-- creating an hb_font_t over one of the pinned oracle's own .otf files and asking
hb_font_get_glyph_h_advance directly.  Rule 35a: a library rule is an oracle and
has to be READ.  Its selfcheck (hb at scale == upem must reproduce hmtx exactly)
passes 65 of 65 glyphs on C059 at upem 1000, libharfbuzz 10.2.0.

WHAT IT SAYS, over 285,940 (glyph, scale) samples on five pinned faces:

    em_mult with a TRUNCATED multiplier, +0x8000      414 misses  (0.145%)
    exact rational, round half away from zero        5883 misses
    float32 multiplier + roundf                       984 / 56,347

-- so the answer is NEAR the classic em_mult form and is none of the three.  Every
one of the 414 misses is an exact .5 tie where hb rounds UP and the truncated form
rounds down.

⚠ AND THE MULTIPLIER FITS NO SIMPLE CLOSED FORM.  Solving for it by interval
intersection (advance == floor((v*mult + 32768)/65536) constrains mult from many
glyphs at one scale) gives, for C059 at upem 1000:

    scale   mult must lie in     exact (scale<<16)/upem   truncate  round
     4134   [270920, 270925]     270925.824               IN        OUT
     4347   [284885, 284886]     284884.992               OUT       IN

One case needs a multiplier BELOW the exact value and the other ABOVE it.  That is
PARITY 16's "the two sets sit in the same window and need OPPOSITE answers"
reproduced against the library itself in a single run, which is what makes it a
property of HarfBuzz rather than of the corpus.  (The solver assumes the
(v*mult + 32768) >> 16 form; where its answer sits far from the exact value the
FORM is what is wrong, so those rows are not measurements of a multiplier.)

⚠ WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and why no fix was landed off these numbers: whether
libharfbuzz 10.2.0 is the version the PINNED ORACLE links.  The oracle statically
links harfbuzz, pango and freetype -- ldd lists only libc, libm, libpthread, libdl
and libresolv -- so the version cannot be read off the binary.  PARITY 14 met the
same limit with FreeType (system 2.13.3 against the oracle's 2.14.1) and set the
rule: rest STRUCTURAL claims on the system library, never sub-unit NUMERIC ones.
D44 is a sub-unit numeric claim.

THE FAMILY IS SMALLER THAN THE PLAN RECORDS, and the arithmetic is the evidence.
Expressing every small dx in the residue as a fraction of one device dot
(0.0341433 staff spaces):

    WHOLE dots, 8 rows -- D44 proper
      emmentaler-fractions-4 (1,2), emmentaler-number-kerning-4 (1,2,4),
      markup-finger-figuredbass-fontsize (1), repeat-dc-al-coda (1),
      repeat-ds-al-coda (1), repeat-ds-mark-override (1),
      repeat-ds-return-count (1), repeat-ds-torture (1)

    EXACTLY HALF a dot, 3 rows -- the same defect one step downstream
      kievan-notation, midi-pedal, pedal-bracket
      (a CENTRED run is placed at -width/2, so a one-dot width error moves its
       origin by half a dot)

    NOT dot-quantized at all, 4 rows -- a DIFFERENT mechanism
      accidental-ancient 0.35, markup-with-true-dimensions 0.37/0.74,
      collision-head-solfa-fa 0.71, dead-notes 0.34-0.80

So D44 is worth ELEVEN rows, not eight and not the whole sub-0.02 tail.  ⚠ The
plan's D44 row lists "probably the sub-0.02 tail incl. dead-notes' residue" among
its constituency; dead-notes' dx values are 0.34-0.80 of a dot and are NOT this
defect.  Correcting that is the point of the table.

================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- THE PURE READS THAT WERE NOT PURE
================================================================================

PARITY 18 (2026-08-16).

Three closures, one class.  Every one of them is a read that upstream takes on
the PURE side and the port took on the ORDINARY side, and in each case the port
carried a written note saying the pure machinery did not exist yet.  It does:
`unpure-pure-container.cc' landed with line breaking, and the notes had simply
never been re-checked.  That is trap 18, three times in one file family, and it
is the same shape PARITY 12 closed for `Stem::internal_pure_height' and PARITY 13
closed for `Staff_grouper_interface::get_extremal_staff'.

⚠ THE SIGNATURE THAT NAMES THIS CLASS IS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN in the vertical
stand-in note above: "if a cross-staff or multi-staff score ever again shows a
separation that is an exact multiple of its spacing, look for a NEW early trigger
first".  PARITY 18's whole GO'd residue was separations at whole and half staff
spaces, and two of the three closures below are exactly that.


(1) Staff_symbol_referencer::pure_get_position ANSWERED THE ORDINARY POSITION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`PureGetPosition' was `GetPosition', under a note reading "the port has no
pure-property machinery yet (unpure-pure-container.cc), so this answers the
ORDINARY position -- the same standing divergence every pure variant takes
today".  `Grob.PureRelativeYCoordinate' had twelve callers by then.

`internal_get_position (me, pure)' is now shared by both readings and differs in
ONE term, exactly as upstream: the pure branch reads
`pure_relative_y_coordinate (c, 0, INT_MAX)'.  The staff symbol's own coordinate,
and the no-staff-symbol branch below it, stay ORDINARY on both paths because
upstream writes them that way (rule 2).

WHAT IT COST.  `Dot_column::calc_positioning_done' is upstream's own reason for
the distinction and says so in a comment: a dot's real Y may be linked to a
beam's, and a beam has no position before line breaking.  The port's ordinary
read forced `Y-parent-positioning' -> the alignment's `positioning-done' ->
beam positioning, from inside a callback that runs before line breaking -- and
`minimise_least_squares' then reported "vertical spacing is triggered before line
breaking" on every sweep.  `dot-column-vertical-positioning.ly' exists to catch
precisely that and says so in its texidoc.  Three rows to MATCH
(`rest-dot-position' 36 marks at a flat -1.0, `dot-column-vertical-positioning',
`dot-column-rest-collision') and the programming error gone, 1 -> 0.

FENCED by `StaffSymbolReferencerTests', five cases.  The claim is carried by a
PAIR on the same grob shape -- an unpure-pure container whose halves are 1.5 and
0.5 gives an ordinary position of 3.0 and a pure position of 1.0 -- and the
CONTROL is a plain-number Y-offset, where the two readings agree.  That control
is the whole reason the defect survived: wherever the chain has no pure/unpure
split, reading the wrong branch is invisible.  A/B'd with the fix reverted: the
three pure cases fail, the ordinary case and the control hold.


(2) Staff_grouper_interface::maybe_pure_within_group ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Upstream's loop:

    if (Page_layout_problem::is_spaceable (*i)
        && ((pure && !Hara_kiri_group_spanner::request_suicide (*i, start, end))
            || (!pure && (*i)->is_live ())))

The port had `IsSpaceable (elts[i]) && elts[i].IsLive' for BOTH branches, under a
note saying suicide was unported so the answer was always "no".  ⚠ THIS IS THE
SIBLING OF THE NOTE PARITY 13 FIXED, in the same upstream file: `get_extremal_staff'
carried the same claim, was corrected, and nothing re-checked the function below
it (trap 17c -- when one member of a family is repaired, sweep the family).  The
CLASS-LEVEL doc comment stated it for both methods and is now corrected for both.

WHAT IT COST.  The answer decides whether the gap under a staff is priced as
`staff-staff-spacing' or as `staffgroup-staff-spacing'.  Before line breaking
nothing has suicided, so every removable staff read live and a group's LAST staff
was mistaken for an interior one.

(3) get_spacing_spec AND calc_maybe_pure_staff_staff_spacing READ ORDINARILY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Both read their properties with `get_maybe_pure_property' upstream; the port used
plain `GetProperty' at all twelve sites in the first and all three in the second,
under a note claiming "every spec the vendored defaults state is a plain alist for
which the two answers coincide".  ⚠ IT IS NOT: `VerticalAxisGroup.staff-staff-spacing'
is `(ly:make-unpure-pure-container ly:axis-group-interface::calc-staff-staff-spacing
ly:axis-group-interface::calc-pure-staff-staff-spacing)' -- two DIFFERENT procedures
-- and an ordinary read runs the unpure one, which is what carried (2) into every
pure spacing estimate.  This was PARITY 15's recorded finding (1), unchased until it
had a measured row impact.

MEASURED, (2) and (3) together: SIX rows, and two of them are the GLYPHS-DIFFER
brace rows.  `satb-template-with-changed-instrument-names' and its ssaattbb sibling
were a flat -1.5 over 109 and 111 marks; `satb-template-with-men-women-and-descant'
-1.5/-3.0; `page-spacing-staff-group-hara-kiri' a flat -11.0 over 28 of 29 marks.
`hairpin-span-bar' (brace173 -> brace174) and
`ssaattbb-template-with-men-women-and-descant' (brace163 -> brace165) came with them,
which CONFIRMS PARITY 17's re-attribution: a system 1.5-3.0 staff spaces too tall
gives a brace one or two optical sizes too tall with nothing in the brace search
wrong.  The port's own instrumentation is what named the gap -- a per-mark dump
showed the choir group unmoved and everything from the organ group down shifted by
exactly -1.5, so the divergence was ONE gap and the rest of the page inherited it.

FENCED by `StaffGrouperWithinGroupTests', four cases: the disagreeing PAIR (pure
false, unpure true, same grouper and same range), plus two controls -- a
non-removable lower staff, where both branches must agree, and a grouper's last
staff, where both must answer false.  A/B'd with the fix reverted: only the pure
case fails.

⚠ WHAT WAS RULED OUT ALONG THE WAY, so no session re-derives it.  The first
hypothesis was a NEW early trigger of the vertical stand-in, and it is REFUTED by
measurement: `Align_interface::align_to_ideal_distances' is never called at all on
these files.  Traced from the port (a file-appending probe, because BatchDriver
redirects both console streams): every vertical translation on
`satb-template-with-changed-instrument-names' comes through
`PageLayoutProblem.AppendSystem' -> `GetMinimumTranslationsWithoutMinDist', 385
calls, of which 4 are unpure.  The stand-in did not fire and is not implicated.


(4) get_fixed_spacing READ THE ORDINARY BOUND IN A PURE LOOKUP
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`sys->get_maybe_pure_bound (LEFT, pure, start, end)' had been ported as
`sys.GetBound (LEFT)'.  `SystemGrob.GetMaybePureBound' existed, with the right
signature, and this call site -- its only consumer -- did not use it.  Trap 17a's
shape: the accessor is present and faithful and nothing calls it.  A system's real
bounds are whatever the last line breaking left behind, so a pure lookup was
answering about a different line.  Worth `volta-bracket-nest', whose brackets sat
a flat 2.0 apart from the oracle's.

FENCED by `FixedSpacingPureBoundTests', three cases: the pure lookup must not find
a forced distance the ordinary bound carries; a control asserting the two bound
accessors answer differently on that fixture at all (without which the first case
proves nothing); and a control where nothing is forced and both answer negative
infinity.  A/B'd with the fix reverted: only the first fails.
⚠ Its first draft asserted that the UNPURE lookup finds 7.0 and that expectation
was WRONG, not the port -- a fixture `Item' has no column, so `Grob::get_system'
answers null and the unpure branch never reaches the bound either.  Rule 35a: a
fence that fails is as likely to be reporting a bad expectation as a bad port.


THE TRAP-24 SWEEP THIS OPENED, RUN AND CLEAN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every method in the Engine taking a `bool pure' parameter was scanned for ordinary
`GetProperty' reads inside its body.  Ten survivors, EVERY ONE verified by hand
against upstream (trap 32a) and every one FAITHFUL -- upstream reads all of them
with plain `get_property': `cross-staff' in side-position-interface.cc (216, 268),
`self-alignment-X/Y' in self-alignment-interface.cc (62), `stacking-dir',
`padding' and `minimum-translations-alist' in align-interface.cc (175, 188, 193,
289), `line-break-system-details' in page-layout-problem.cc (1229), and
staff-symbol-referencer.cc's own `staff-position' fallback.  The class is closed
for property reads; (4) above is what the same sweep found on the BOUND accessor.


================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- THE ARGUMENTS THAT WERE ACCEPTED AND DROPPED (PARITY 19,
2026-08-17)
================================================================================

Three defects, and TWO of them are one class: a Scheme binding that declares
upstream's arity, accepts the optional argument, and never looks at it.  Nothing
in this project could see them.  The arity is right, so the entry-point closure
and the ledger are satisfied; the call returns a well-formed value, so nothing
throws; and the C# function each one owed had been ported FAITHFULLY and simply
had no caller -- trap 17a, for the third and fourth time.


(1) ly:stencil-empty? IGNORED ITS AXIS ARGUMENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Bootstrap/RegistryPrimitives.cs' declared arity 1..2 and answered
`stencil.IsEmpty' -- the WHOLE-stencil question -- for every call.  Upstream
(stencil-scheme.cc) answers `s->is_empty (from_scm<Axis> (axis))' when the
argument is supplied.  `Stencil::is_empty (Axis)' was already ported, as
`Stencil.IsEmptyOn', with C# callers; this, its only SCHEME consumer, never
called it.

⚠ WHAT IT COST, and why it is more than one row.  A stencil empty on ONE axis is
the ordinary case for spacing: `\hspace' is X-only, `\vspace' is Y-only.  EIGHT
sites in the vendored layer ask about a single axis, and not one of their rules
had ever run:

    lily/stencil.scm:164-165   stack-lines -- a Y-empty, X-non-empty line is
                               promoted to Y=(0 . 0) so it "registers as a line
                               of its own".  Every \column, \center-column and
                               \left-column containing a spacer was mis-measured.
    lily/titling.scm:87-88     marked-up-headfoot -- the same rule for headers,
                               footers and titles
    lily/stencil.scm:662-663   box-stencil's h-space?/v-space? -- whether a box
                               drawn round a pure spacer keeps the raw extents
    lily/define-markup-commands.scm:1448-1449, 2106

FOUND FROM `markup-conditionals-single-page', where all ten music marks sat
2.0659 too high while the header markup was byte-identical and unmoved.  The
chain, measured with port-side instrumentation rather than reasoned:
scoreTitleMarkup is `\column { \if \should-print-all-headers { \bookTitleMarkup
\hspace #1 } \fill-line { piece opus } }', whose first line is X=[0,1] and
Y-EMPTY.  stack-lines should promote it; the port left it Y-empty; so
`Paper_book::get_score_title' skipped `align_to (Y_AXIS, UP)', the title prob
reached `Page_layout_problem::append_prob' with an empty Y interval,
`minimum_distance = iv[UP] - bottom_skyline_.max_height ()' was -infinity, and the
header height never constrained the first spring.

    BEFORE  spring[0] ideal=4 min=0        -> music at 4+5 = 9.0 below top margin
    AFTER   spring[0] ideal=4 min=6.0659   (= header height 5.0659 + padding 1)
    ORACLE  music at 11.0649 below top margin; 6.0659 + 5 = 11.0659.

Worth FOUR rows: markup-conditionals-single-page, markup-boxed-spacers (the file
that tests boxing spacers, which is site 662-663 exactly), titling-3, titling-4.


(2) ly:event-length IGNORED ITS MOMENT ARGUMENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Same shape.  `Bootstrap/TranslationPrimitives.cs' answered the bare `length'
property.  Upstream's two-argument form is translator.cc's
`get_event_length (e, now)', which moves the whole length into the GRACE part when
`now' is an in-grace moment -- grace notes take no main-part time.
`Translator.GetEventLength (e, now)' was ported faithfully and has eight C#
callers; the binding never used it.

The vendored `Bend_engraver' is the only Scheme caller that passes a moment, and
it is the whole cost: a fall starting inside `\grace' computes
`(ly:moment-add now (ly:event-length (event-cause grob) now))' for its stop
moment, and with a main-part length there the band is never released --
`start-translation-timestep' never reaches `stop-fall', so the FIRST fall of a
grace block is drawn and every later one in that voice is not.  MEASURED with a
probe that reports and then calls the real stencil callback: the oracle makes 12
BendAfter stencils on `bend-after-start-in-grace' and the port made 8.

⚠ THE FIX'S FIRST DRAFT WAS WRONG IN A WAY THE PROBE CAUGHT AND A SWEEP WOULD NOT
HAVE: the optional-argument test was INVERTED, so the two-argument branch was
taken only when no moment was supplied.  The row stayed GLYPHS-DIFFER and the
moment probe still showed `#<Mom 3/4>' where the oracle shows `#<Mom 0G3/4>'.
Run the probe again after the fix, not only the row.

Worth ONE row: bend-after-start-in-grace.


(3) memq COMPARED WITH ReferenceEquals, AND ITS SIBLING ALREADY SAID SO
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`SchemeUtilities.Memq' -- the engine's `scm_memq' -- walked the list comparing
with `ReferenceEquals'.  Guile fixnums, booleans and characters are IMMEDIATES,
so `(memq 3 '(3))' is true there and two separately boxed 3s are never
ReferenceEquals here.  `Figured_bass_engraver' reads `implicitBassFigures', a list
of figure NUMBERS, through it, so NO implicit figure had ever been suppressed.

⚠ THE SIBLING `Assq' CARRIES A NOTE ABOUT EXACTLY THIS DEFECT, written the day
`ottavationMarkups' exposed it, and `Memq' and `AssqRemove' were left standing.
Trap 17c: when one member of a family is repaired, sweep the family.  Both now use
`ReferenceComparer.Instance.Equals', which is LilyScheme's own `eq?'.

Worth ONE row: figured-bass-implicit.

FENCED by `OptionalArgumentBindingTests', six cases, three of them controls, and
each A/B'd with its own fix FULLY reverted (trap 32d): with the reverts in, the
three claim cases fail and all three controls hold.  ⚠ The stencil control's first
draft asserted that a spacer is empty as a WHOLE, and that expectation was wrong,
not the port -- `Box::is_empty ()' is the AND of both axes, so a stencil empty on
one axis only is not empty as a whole.  Rule 35a again.


THE OPTIONAL-ARGUMENT SWEEP THIS OPENED, RUN AND CLEAN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every `DefinePrimitive' in the Engine declaring a maximum arity above its minimum
was scanned for whether the body reads the optional index.  72 primitives, 12
survivors, EVERY ONE verified by hand against upstream (trap 32a) and only the two
above are defects:

  * `ly:parser-output-name', `ly:parser-clear-error', `ly:parser-has-error?' and
    `ly:make-stencil' read their optional argument through a HELPER (`Parser (a,
    0, ...)', `ToInterval (a, 1, ...)') -- the scanner cannot see that, they are
    correct.
  * `ly:grob-pure-height' drops `val', and so does UPSTREAM: grob-scheme.cc:137
    defaults it and then returns the extent unconditionally.  Reproducing dead
    code is rule 2.
  * `ly:arpeggio::pure-height' takes start/end and upstream ignores them too.
  * `ly:side-position-interface::x-aligned-side' / `::y-aligned-side' /
    `::pure-y-aligned-side' DO use theirs, via `OptionalArgument (a, n)'.  This
    matters: `chain_offset_callback' composes them as `(PROC GROB (orig-proc
    GROB))', so the second argument is always supplied in practice.
  * `ly:regex-exec' declares 2..3 where upstream declares 2..2; a superset that
    nothing calls with three.
  * `ly:option-usage' is a recorded port-only stub.

The class is closed.


================================================================================
PARITY GRIND -- THE FONT FAMILY THE PORT GUESSED AT (PARITY 20, 2026-08-17)
================================================================================

Ruling R14 (Jeremy, 2026-08-17), option (a).  `TextFontChain.Normalize' SNIFFED a
requested font-family name for a category word:

    contains "mono"/"typewriter"/"courier"  -> typewriter -> Nimbus Mono PS
    contains "sans"                          -> sans       -> Nimbus Sans
    otherwise                                -> serif      -> C059

UPSTREAM HAS NO SUCH STEP.  It hands the name to fontconfig and never inspects it.
Under the reference corpus's own pinning -- and `fc-match' answers this, MEASURED
over fifteen patterns (trap 8b, rule 35a) -- a family none of the 24 vendored faces
provides resolves to TeX Gyre Schola Regular, at whatever style was asked for:

    "DejaVu Sans" / "DejaVu Serif" / "DejaVu Sans Mono" / "Linux Libertine O"
    "Arial" / "Courier New" / "Comic Sans MS" / "Foo Bar Baz"   -> TeX Gyre Schola
    "DejaVu Sans:weight=bold"                                   -> Schola Bold

because reference-fonts.conf.in aliases the generic names and nothing else, and its
one <dir> is the oracle's own font directory.

Worth SEVEN rows, every one measured to MATCH before the change was written:
markup-time-signatures, markup-accidentals, markup-font-select,
font-family-override, font-name (0.0001), metronome-mark-formatter (0.0013),
dead-notes.  Plus one DIAGNOSTICS row -- see (3).

THE SURPRISING USER-VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE, recorded because the ruling weighed it:
`\override Staff.TimeSignature.font-name = "DejaVu Sans"' now draws in a SERIF.
The old answer was the wrong face in the right category; the new one is what
fontconfig answers over a directory of 25 files.  The request cannot be honoured
either way -- the user asked for a font that is not there -- so the only question
was which wrong answer, and parity decides it.  D23 is untouched: TeX Gyre Schola
is one of the 24 vendored faces, so no option here introduced system-font fallback.


(1) A FAMILY IS A LIST, AND THE WHOLE-STRING VERSION WOULD HAVE REGRESSED A ROW
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LilyPond writes CSS family LISTS and fontconfig walks them, taking the first entry
it can satisfy.  MEASURED under the corpus's pinning:

    "Linux Libertine O"                 -> TeX Gyre Schola
    "Linux Libertine O,serif"           -> C059
    "Linux Libertine Mono O,monospace"  -> Nimbus Mono PS

`kievan-notation.ly' sets all three of those, so "unknown name -> Schola" applied
to the whole string would have sent it to Schola where the oracle reaches C059
through the list's SECOND entry.  `Normalize' therefore walks the comma-separated
list and matches a category name EXACTLY within an entry.  That is also why
kievan-notation is NOT an R14 row: both engines already land it on C059 and its
0.0171 is D44's half device dot.

An EMPTY entry is skipped rather than defaulted, and there is a real one to skip:
`font-name = "Bitstream Vera Sans, Bold"' is a Pango description, so
`FontInterface.ParseDescription' takes " Bold" off as a style word and hands
`Normalize' the family "Bitstream Vera Sans," -- trailing comma included.


(2) THE TWO GROUPS OF CATEGORY NAMES, AND WHY fc-match CANNOT MEASURE ONE OF THEM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/!\ THE RULING'S PREMISE WAS INCOMPLETE, AND THE RATCHET IS WHAT CAUGHT IT.  The
first implementation matched only the four CSS generics (serif, sans, sans-serif,
monospace -- what reference-fonts.conf.in aliases, and what
ly/paper-defaults-init.ly:170-181 asks for under -dbackend=svg).  The full sweep
came back +7 improvements and ONE REGRESSION: `markup-music-glyph.svg', MATCH ->
PLACEMENT-DIFFERS at 0.2731.

That file sets `font-name = "LilyPond Sans Serif"' DIRECTLY, which bypasses
paper-defaults-init.ly's backend switch -- so LilyPond's own virtual family names
DO reach the SVG backend, contrary to the reading that they are the non-SVG branch
only.  And upstream's shipped `fonts/00-lilypond-fonts.conf' aliases exactly three
of them, by category, with prefer lists that are D23's chain face for face:

    LilyPond Serif       -> C059, Century Schoolbook L, TeX Gyre Schola, DejaVu Serif, Noto CJK
    LilyPond Sans Serif  -> Nimbus Sans, Nimbus Sans L, TeX Gyre Heros, DejaVu Sans
    LilyPond Monospace   -> Nimbus Mono PS, Nimbus Mono L, TeX Gyre Cursor, DejaVu Sans Mono

/!\ AND fc-match MISREPORTS THEM.  LilyPond loads that conf into its own FcConfig
at startup (`lily/font-config.cc'), so those three names are aliased INSIDE the
oracle's process even though FONTCONFIG_FILE has replaced the system
configuration.  A shell `fc-match "LilyPond Serif"' answers TeX Gyre Schola; the
oracle answers C059.  This is trap 8b's sibling: the probe's environment matched
the corpus's and was still not the oracle's, because the oracle ADDS
configuration in process.  Read these three off the conf, never off fc-match --
and the measurement that settled it was the corpus's own bytes, not a probe: the
oracle's "c'est"->"un" advance on that page is 5.0386, the sniffing heuristic gave
5.0386, and Schola gives 4.9703.

`Generics' therefore carries SEVEN names in two groups, and the second group is
upstream's own aliasing rather than a port-side category guess.


(3) THE DIAGNOSTICS ROW IT CLOSED, WHICH IS PART OF D40's REMAINING DEBT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Diagnostics 2138 -> 2139 (99.63% -> 99.67%), TEXT-DIFFERS 3 -> 2.  `pdf-copy-paste'
asks for "Noto Serif JP"; the old heuristic sniffed it to C059-Roman, and D40's
missing-glyph warning NAMES THE FACE, so the port reported

    no glyph for character '⾒' (U+2F92 KANGXI RADICAL SEE) in font `C059-Roman.otf'

where the oracle reports texgyreschola-regular.otf.  Both now agree.  R8(A) records
that the D31 tofu files "still owe the oracle's no-glyph-for-character warnings";
this pays one of them, and it is paid by resolving the FAMILY upstream's way rather
than by touching the warning.  The file's SVG row is unchanged -- still
GLYPHS-DIFFER, still on g1-skip-list.tsv, as D31 requires.


(4) D23 AMENDED THE SAME DAY: ROBOTO WAS NEVER IN THE CHAIN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

D23 had read "URW face -> TeX Gyre face -> pinned Roboto -> tofu" since it was
decided, and `Fonts/TextFace.cs' carried a doc comment saying the port "continues
into the Roboto package".  There is no Roboto PackageReference and no Roboto face
in the repository: the chain has always stopped at TeX Gyre.  Removing the step
CHANGES NO BEHAVIOUR and makes the written decision describe the code.  Keeping it
would have been a parity RISK rather than a gain -- the pinned oracle's one <dir>
has no Roboto, so any code point Roboto covered and the 24 faces do not would draw
a real glyph in the port where the oracle draws none.  The tofu that remains is
D31's, which is ruled and skip-listed.


FENCED BY `TextFontChainTests' (Engine), seven cases, every one paired with a
control that must come out differently -- a fence checking only the unmatched case
would pass with the whole family table collapsed onto one face.  A/B'd with the
change FULLY reverted (the sniffing body restored AND `For's fallback put back to
serif -- trap 32d): all seven fail.  Restored: all seven pass.


================================================================================
THE TWO ROWS THAT MEASURE OUR OWN EMMENTALER BUILD (PARITY 20, 2026-08-17)
================================================================================

Ruling R15 (Jeremy, 2026-08-17), option (a): `accidental-ancient' and
`markup-with-true-dimensions' join R9's committed PORT-GENERATED baseline.  This is
D43's class exactly and R9 already ruled it, one day earlier, for the six skyline
rows -- the machinery existed and was built for this.

NOT A DEFECT AND NOT A SKIP.  The port builds Emmentaler from the Metafont sources
by a written decision (tools/font-build), so the fonts are genuinely ours with
provenance verifiable from source to binary.  That build is not outline-identical
to the oracle's, and the difference is MEASURED to the font unit rather than
bounded.  One font unit is 0.004 staff spaces (upem 1000; emmentaler-20's em is the
20pt staff height, four staff spaces):

    accidental-ancient           -0.0120 = 3 units    accidentals.hufnagelM1
    markup-with-true-dimensions  +0.0127 = 1 unit x 2^(10/6)   scripts.trill
                                 +0.0254 = the same, twice     (\fontsize #10)

WHY IT SURFACES ON TWO ROWS OUT OF 2,316.  Every glyph DIMENSION is read from the
LILC table, and our LILC tables are byte-identical to the oracle's.  The OUTLINE is
consulted in one place -- stencil-integral.cc's get_glyph_outline_bbox, feeding
skylines -- plus `\with-true-dimensions', which reads it by definition.

/!\ AND THE ROUTE IS THE SKYLINE, NOT A BBOX FUNCTION, which corrects the shape the
ruling was written in.  `stencil-true-extent' (Scheme/lily/stencil.scm:1096) calls
`ly:skylines-for-stencil' and takes the pair's two max heights; a music glyph's
skyline is built by StencilIntegral.AddNamedGlyphSegments ->
CffFont.AddOutlineToSkyline -> GlyphOutlineSkyline.AddOutline.  And accidental
placement reads each accidental's `horizontal-skylines' (AccidentalPlacement.cs),
which is the same walk.  So BOTH rows ride ONE mechanism, not two, and the ruling's
"check whether GlyphOutlineSkylineTests' coverage genuinely extends" resolves as:
it fences that function's SEGMENT-COUNT rule and says nothing about which SOURCE
the extent comes from.  That was the real gap, and one fence closes it for both rows.

THE CORRECTNESS FENCE, WHICH IS NOT THE BASELINE (rule 33).  The baseline claims NO
DRIFT and nothing else.  `GlyphOutlineSkylineTests' gained two cases:

  * a_glyphs_true_extent_is_read_from_its_outline_and_not_from_its_declared_box --
    on both rows' OWN glyphs (rule 35b).  MEASURED, in stencil units:

        scripts.trill           declared X=[-1.4937,1.4937]  ink X=[-2.2704,1.9471]
        accidentals.hufnagelM1  declared X=[-0.1757,1.0544]  ink X=[-0.1546,1.0333]

    The true extent equals the INK on both axes to 1e-4, and the ORDINARY extent of
    the very same stencil equals the LILC-declared box to 1e-9.  Two readings of one
    glyph out of two different tables; the fixture's premise (that the two sources
    disagree by more than the comparator's 0.0100) is asserted, not assumed.
  * a_glyph_whose_outline_fills_its_declared_box_reads_the_same_either_way -- the
    control, and the glyph had to be MEASURED: `accidentals.sharp' is the one whose
    declared and ink boxes agree to four decimals on BOTH axes, so reading the wrong
    source is invisible there.  That is what shows the discrimination above is a
    property of those glyphs rather than of the mechanism.

A/B'd the way R9's fence was, by BREAKING the mechanism in the plausible direction:
AddNamedGlyphSegments made to contribute the glyph's DECLARED box instead of tracing
its outline.  The claim fails; the control still passes; and all four segment-count
cases also still pass, which is the direct evidence that they never covered this.

WHAT THE ROWS DO NOW.  Graded by `compare-output.py --baseline baseline/svg
candidate/svg' at 8 of 8 MATCH, WITH R9's control run: without the flag all eight
read GLYPHS-DIFFER, because the baseline side's glyph paths then resolve against the
ORACLE's half of glyph-identity.tsv.  They CONTINUE to read PLACEMENT-DIFFERS
against the oracle corpus in the ordinary sweep and floor there -- expected, and not
work.  Frozen from the PARITY 20 sweep of 02:08-02:15, and proven current rather
than assumed: both pages were re-rendered from the then-current binary and compared
byte for byte with that sweep's output.

RULED OUT ALONG THE WAY: shipping the oracle's .otf (it reverses the font-build
README's provenance decision), chasing the Metafont/FontForge version difference
(open-ended, buys two rows, and the README already judged the difference a toolchain
artifact), and a bounded POSITION tolerance (D29 forbids it, and R13 was recommended
against on the same ground the same day).

/!\ tools/font-build/README.txt section 7 recorded this on 2026-08-02, named
accidentals.mensuralM1 as one of the two largest, and named the one code path that
would expose it.  Trap 27a: a finding recorded in a build README is still a finding.
