D23 AMENDED -- THE FALLBACK STEP THAT NEVER EXISTED (PARITY 20, 2026-08-17)
================================================================================

Jeremy, 2026-08-17, alongside ruling R14.  D23 had read

    URW face -> TeX Gyre face -> pinned Roboto 1.0.209.315 -> tofu

since it was decided on 2026-08-05.  VERIFIED: there is no Roboto
PackageReference in the Engine and no Roboto face in the repository, so the chain
has always been

    URW face -> TeX Gyre face -> tofu

Removing the step CHANGES NO BEHAVIOUR, because the step never ran.  What it
changes is that the written decision now describes the code.

AND KEEPING IT WOULD HAVE BEEN A PARITY RISK RATHER THAN A GAIN.  The pinned
oracle's one font directory has no Roboto, so any code point Roboto covers and the
24 vendored faces do not would draw a REAL GLYPH in the port where the oracle draws
none -- a new divergence class, in exchange for coverage D31 has already ruled on.
The tofu that remains is D31's, which is ruled and skip-listed.

WHERE THE CLAIM HAD SPREAD.  It was not one sentence.  Corrected in the same cycle,
after grepping for it rather than fixing only the one that was known:

  Fonts/TextFace.cs                     TextFontChain's class doc claimed the port
                                        "continues into the Roboto package instead"
  Fonts/KerningTable.cs                 the wider GPOS surface was "for the faces
                                        D23's fallback chain may grow (the pinned
                                        Roboto package)"
  Bootstrap/NotApplicableEntryPoints.cs the font-plumbing N/A category described the
                                        font layer as "the 24 vendored faces plus the
                                        pinned Roboto fallback"
  assets/fonts/text/README.txt          the same fallback claim, PLUS a "D23
                                        two-phase protocol" for re-baselining
                                        DejaVu-dependent references as PORT-GENERATED
                                        Roboto references -- which also never
                                        happened: D30's corpus pinning solved that
                                        problem instead, so there is nothing to
                                        re-baseline
  THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt section 10.3  "the port's fallback face is Roboto,
                                        consumed as the OFL-licensed ... package"

/!\ THE NOTICES ENTRY WAS A CORRECTION, NOT A DELETION, and the distinction
mattered.  Roboto IS in this repository -- the Lily.Shell APP takes
CodeBrix.Platform.Fonts.Roboto.OflLicenseForever (1.0.209.315) for its UI chrome and
CodeBrix.Platform.Fonts.RobotoMono.OflLicenseForever (1.0.216.1150) for its terminal
surface -- and section 10.3 was the ONLY place in the notices file that mentioned it.
Deleting the sentence would have removed a real dependency's attribution while
fixing a false statement about where it is used.  Section 10.3 now records both
packages against the shell app, notes that neither takes part in engraving or in the
D23 chain, and states that nothing of theirs is vendored or ships inside the
CodeBrix.LilyPort packages.  RobotoMono had not been mentioned at all.

ALSO CORRECTED, and it is the wording R15 flagged: G1's baseline clause said "D23
fallback-dependent files match committed PORT-GENERATED baselines".  None of the
eight rows on that baseline is fallback-dependent -- D43's six and R15's two are
font-BUILD-dependent.  Re-worded to "files whose divergence is in an ASSET the port
builds or ships rather than in the engine", in the plan, in
tools/regression-harness/README.txt, and in the D43 entry above.  Left alone, the
sentence describing G1 when it goes green would have been false about eight of its
rows.


================================================================================
PARITY 21 -- THE HARFBUZZ MULTIPLIER, AND SIX MECHANISMS BEHIND IT
================================================================================

THE HARFBUZZ MULTIPLIER IS TAKEN IN FLOAT -- D44 CLOSED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

D44's eleven rows all read "one device dot short".  PARITY 16 refuted two rules by
sweep and PARITY 17 refuted a third by measurement, and the sharp result it left
behind was that hb's effective multiplier sits ABOVE the exact value at one scale
and BELOW it at another -- which no closed form over the exact value can do.

IT IS NOT A CLOSED FORM OVER THE EXACT VALUE.  hb_font_t::mults_changed takes the
quotient in FLOAT and only then scales it to 16.16:

    float upem = face->get_upem ();
    x_multf = x_scale / upem;                            // float32 division
    x_mult  = (int64_t) scalbnf (fabsf (x_multf), +16);  // exact x65536, truncated

    hb_position_t em_mult (int16_t v, int64_t mult)
    { return (hb_position_t) ((v * mult + 32768) >> 16); }

float32 rounds the quotient to nearest, so the multiplier lands above the exact
value as often as below it, and THAT is what decides an exact half-unit tie.  The
refuted reading -- (x_scale << 16) / upem in exact integer arithmetic -- differs
from it only where the quotient's float rounding crosses a unit, which is rare
enough to look right and exactly where D44's rows sit.

MEASURED, not recalled (rule 35a -- a library rule is an oracle).  Driven through
the system libharfbuzz 10.2.0 by ~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity21/hb_pipeline.py
over the pinned oracle's own faces:

    em_mult, multiplier from the FLOAT quotient      0 misses of 1,738,110
    em_mult, multiplier from exact integer division  847 misses of 109,480 (C059 alone)
    roundf of a float32 product                     1919 misses of 109,480
    exact rational, round half away from zero       1944 misses of 109,480

/!\ THE VERSION QUESTION DID NOT NEED A BISECT.  The pinned oracle statically links
harfbuzz, so its version cannot be read off the binary, and R13 was written as a
bisect in shape.  It was not one: the rule the SYSTEM library yields reproduces the
oracle's own corpus on all eleven rows, so there was nothing to hunt.  hb_advance.py
now takes HB_LIBRARY so a later version hunt is possible without installing
anything; it was not needed.

THE PIPELINE HAS FOUR INTEGER STAGES, NOT TWO.  Two of them are upstream of anything
PARITY 16 tried:

  1. pango_size = lround (requested_size * PANGO_SCALE)     lily/font-select.cc:215
  2. x_scale    = pango_units_from_double (pixel_size)      pangofc-font.c
                = floor (pango_size * 1200/72 + 0.5)
     -- A WHOLE NUMBER OF PANGO UNITS, up to half a unit from the exact value.
  3. adv_pu     = em_mult (units) [+ em_mult (kern)]        harfbuzz
  4. width_pu   = PANGO_UNITS_ROUND (adv_pu)                pango-shape.c, per glyph

/!\ THE KERN IS SCALED SEPARATELY.  GPOS's apply_value calls em_scale_x on the pair
value and ADDS it to an x_advance em_scale_x already produced, so a kerned step owes
TWO em_mults.  em_mult of a sum is not the sum of two em_mults.

BOTH FONT CLASSES OWED IT.  TextFontMetric.TextStencil is the text path;
TextInterface.MusicFontTextStencil is the fetaText path, and it reaches Pango the
same way (font-select.cc's else branch covers latin1 AND fetaText).  The music path
recovers its hb scale from the scale it already measures with --
x_scale = FontScaling * PANGO_SCALE / dot -- which keeps that path's effective scale
exactly what it was and changes only the arithmetic that lands on the grid.

Fenced by ShapedAdvanceTests, whose cases are the ones where the readings DISAGREE:
a fence written on an ordinary advance passes under all four rules.


FONT-FEATURES REACHED GSUB ONLY -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The port routed `font-features' to the substitution table and nowhere else, so a run
asking for -kern was kerned anyway.  HarfBuzz turns `kern' on for a horizontal run
without being asked, so the tag only ever appears in a run that wants it OFF --
emmentaler-fractions and emmentaler-number-kerning each devote their FOURTH PAGE to
exactly that request, and those two pages were D44's last two rows for a reason that
had nothing to do with D44.  KerningTable.Enabled reads the same -tag/+tag spelling
SubstitutionTable reads, last entry winning.


assq-remove! REMOVED EVERY MATCH -- FIXED, TWICE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

libguile/alist.c:344 -- "Delete the FIRST entry in alist associated with key" --
implemented as scm_sloppy_assq to find one handle and scm_delq1_x to unlink that
pair.  Both of the port's copies filtered the whole list instead: LilyScheme's
assq-remove!/assv-remove!/assoc-remove! trio and LilyPort's own
SchemeUtilities.AssqRemove (trap 20 -- a fix applied to one copy does not reach the
other).

THE DIFFERENCE IS ONLY VISIBLE ON A DUPLICATED KEY, and an alist used as a SCOPED
CHAIN is exactly that: removing the inner entry uncovers the outer one, where
removing every match erases both.  select-markup-replacement in
define-markup-commands.scm relies on it, so a second \replace-with-tag over the
first's result did not replace -- markup-tag-multi-replace-with-tag.  Fenced by
AlistRemoveTests, every case paired with a unique-key control that BOTH readings
pass.


invalidate-alterations WAS DEFINED AND NEVER CALLED -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Trap 17a's own signature: a faithful helper with zero callers leaves no stub, no
throw and no ledger row.  Clef_engraver::inspect_clef_properties calls
apply_on_children (context (), Lily::invalidate_alterations) before it sets the
glyph (clef-engraver.cc:151); the port had the vendored Scheme procedure and neither
the call nor the walk.  apply_on_children is a free function in that file, not a
Context method -- it applies the procedure to the context and recurses over
children_contexts, which is how a Staff-level clef change reaches the Voice contexts
that hold localAlterations.

Closed accidental-clef-change AND accidental-styles-1/-2, which the board had filed
under cautionary accidental styles.  Two rows attributed to the wrong mechanism for
several sessions is the cost of not asking what a defined-but-uncalled procedure was
for.


THE UNIT LENGTH WAS COMPUTED ONCE PER FILE, NOT ONCE PER BOOK -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

framework-svg.scm's output-framework calls
(set-unit-length (ly:output-def-lookup layout 'output-scale)) for the book it is
about to write.  BatchRunner kept ONE value for the whole input file and rendered
every book with it, so a file holding books at different global staff sizes wrote
the earlier books with the LAST book's scale.  The backend divides font sizes by
that number and nothing else, so the geometry stayed right and every text font-size
came out doubled -- which reads as a GLYPH-INVENTORY difference, not as a layout
error (book-change-global-staffsize-abs-fonts-standard-size).


A PARSER SCOPE IMPORTED (lily) ITSELF, NOT ITS PUBLIC INTERFACE -- FIXED IN PART
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ly_make_module reaches both of its imports through ly_use_module, and that is

    module-use! (mod, module-public-interface (used))          lily/ly-module.cc:37

LilyModules.Make imported the MODULES.  (lily) holds 964 public and 832 private
bindings, and importing it whole put all 1,796 in front of the parser's identifier
lookup -- so a name the Scheme layer happens to use internally SHADOWS the user's own
assignment from an enclosing scope.  part-combiner.scm declares a GOOPS accessor
called `tuning' (a plain define-class slot accessor, not exported), so
markup-score.ly's

    tuning = \markup { \score { ... } }
    \header { subsubtitle = \markup { "Originalstimmung:" \tuning } }

found the accessor, answered "not a markup", and dropped the embedded score off the
page.  The (lily) import now takes .Interface(), which is LIVE rather than a
snapshot because (lily) is a define-module and boot-9's beautify-user-module!
installed a real interface module that every later define-public adds to.

/!\ AND IT UNCOVERED A SECOND DIVERGENCE UNDERNEATH IT.  Upstream reaches every
Lily:: name by binding it against the LILY MODULE ITSELF (lily/lily-imports.cc);
LilyPondScheme.LookupProcedure resolved them through interpreter.CurrentModule,
which during parsing is a PARSER SCOPE.  That worked only because the scope
imported (lily) whole -- and several Lily:: names are plain defines, not exports:
Lily::deprecated_setter_object_property (lily-imports.cc:96) is
(define deprecated-setter-object-property ...) at scm/lily.scm:672.  So the scope
fix made four unit tests fail and NO corpus row move, which is the shape of a
defect the comparator is structurally blind to (trap 1).  LookupProcedure now asks
(lily) first and the current module second.

/!\ THE FULL SUITE FOUND THIS, NOT THE SWEEP.  Three full sweeps passed clean over
the broken build.  A session that trusts the ratchet as its only gate would have
shipped it.

/!\ DELIBERATE REMAINING DIVERGENCE: the ROOT module is still imported whole.
Upstream takes its public interface too -- in Guile that is the-scm-module, set by
boot-9.scm:3152.  Taking .Interface() on the port's root module leaves the Scheme
layer unable to see `define-module' at all, so the layer does not boot.  MEASURED,
not assumed: the whole corpus errored on the first render.  The narrow half is the
half the defect was in; the root half is recorded here rather than forced.


ly:run-translator HAD NO CALLERS -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Upstream has exactly one route into translation.  Score::book_rendering
(score.cc:146) and ly:score-embedded-format (score-scheme.cc:146) BOTH call
ly_run_translator, and the zero-duration report lives inside it:

    if (!g->iterate (music, false))
      { music->warning (_ ("skipping zero-duration score")); ... }

The port's two drivers assembled the global context themselves and DISCARDED
Iterate's bool, which is the whole of that report -- trap 17f, a fix wired into a
driver nothing reaches, with the port's own ly:run-translator primitive left
callerless as the tell.  no-music expects eight of those warnings and got none.
GlobalContext.Run is now the one route and all three sites take it.


A MANUAL BREAK WAS DROPPED IN A SKIPPED TIMESTEP -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Paper_column_engraver::pre_process_music guards only the COLUMN MAKING with
skipTypesetting; handle_manual_breaks (false) is OUTSIDE the if and runs every
timestep (paper-column-engraver.cc:126-150).  The port had turned the guard into an
early RETURN, which took the manual breaks with it.

That is trap 1c one step over -- the defect is in WHICH STATEMENTS the condition
covers, not in the condition.  A \break in a timestep that STARTED skipped attaches
to the column made at the beginning of the skipped section, which is exactly what
that retained column is for; dropping it merged skiptypesetting-break's two systems
into one, and the row read as a glyph-inventory difference because a system's staff
lines, clef and bar number are furniture the note glyphs do not miss.


THE HARNESS THREW AWAY THE PARSE-ERROR MESSAGE -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BatchDriver printed a file's parse-error COUNT and never its Diagnostics, so a file
that still produced SVG could report an error with no message anywhere -- trap 1b at
the harness level.  markup-score was such a file: one parse error, no text, and an
embedded score missing from the page.  --diagnostics prints them, OPT IN and
deliberately not the default, because compare-diagnostics.py attributes every line
since the previous result line to the file named next and extra lines on a full
sweep would read as EXTRA diagnostics.


================================================================================
PARITY 22 -- FIVE MECHANISMS, AND ONE HYPOTHESIS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT
================================================================================


A STAND-IN ONLY AN EMBEDDED \score COULD SEE -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Align_interface::align_elements_to_ideal_distances builds a Page_layout_problem over
its own system and solves it RAGGED (align-interface.cc:299-306); the port took the
MINIMUM distances instead, and said so.  A PAGED score cannot tell the difference,
because page layout builds the real Page_layout_problem afterwards and re-solves --
which is why the stand-in survived twenty parity sessions and why PORT-COVERAGE's own
measurement found the callback "never called at all" on the files it was chased on.
An EMBEDDED `\score' markup gets no page layout at all, so the packed staves were its
final answer.

MEASURED: markup-tag-recognized-in-lyrics' lyric line sat 0.5873 staff-spaces too
close to its staff.  The oracle's distance is 5.5000 exactly, which is the Lyrics
VerticalAxisGroup's nonstaff-relatedstaff-spacing BASIC-DISTANCE (engraver-init.ly:
649-652) -- and a spring at zero force IS its ideal length, so the number identifies
the mechanism on its own.  Closed tag-group and markup-tag-recognized-in-lyrics, and
stencil-expressions-1 came with them (its page compresses by 8.7 staff-spaces where
the oracle compresses by 9.2 -- the same fact its diagnostics row was reporting, so
that row closed too).

/!\ THE NO-SYSTEM HALF IS STILL THE RECORDED STAND-IN.  Only the with-system branch
is upstream's now.


A CALL WHOSE STATED PURPOSE IS NOT ITS ONLY EFFECT -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

System::post_processing ends with a loop that computes EVERY grob's stencil, and
upstream's comment says why: "Generate all stencils to trigger font loads.  This
might seem inefficient, but Stencils are cached per grob anyway" (system.cc:582-591).
The port had ported the translate and not the loop.

Loading fonts is not the only thing that loop does.  A stencil callback may have SIDE
EFFECTS ON OTHER GROBS, and the only reason those land is that every callback has run
before the drawing loop consumes anything.  `stem-span-stencil'
(scm/music-functions.scm:517-536) is the case that proves it: it hides the stems it
spans by SETTING their `stencil' to #f.  Without the pass, the drawing loop had
already committed twelve of them -- cross-staff-stems.ly drew 30 rects where the
oracle draws 18, and it was the last real GLYPHS-DIFFER row.

MEASURED, not reasoned: a probe re-implementing Span_stem_engraver out of public
bindings only (~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity22/span-stem-interleave.ly) runs
UNCHANGED on both engines and reproduces the oracle's own 18 rects.  Both engines
create 8 spans, 7 of them with 2 connectable stems and 1 with only 1; both hide 14
stems and both read #f back.  The port's own draw-loop trace showed every SPANSTENCIL
evaluation happening INSIDE the drawing loop, which is what the pre-pass fixes.

DIVERGENCE, deliberate: upstream's `uniquify' sorts by POINTER before removing
duplicates, so its visiting order is arbitrary.  The port dedupes by reference in
first-seen order.  That is an ORDER difference only, and it cannot be observed here:
a callback that overwrites another grob's stencil leaves the same value behind
whichever of the two ran first.


AN EXPLICIT #f IS AN ANSWER, NOT AN ABSENCE -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every upstream reader of a skyline property spells the test
`if (!is_scm<Skyline_pair> (get_property (elt, "vertical-skylines"))) continue;'
(axis-group-interface.cc:770-772, 920-923): the grob is left OUT of the skyline
altogether.  The port's ReadSkylinePair stood in for an unset property by building
flat skylines from the grob's extents -- a documented divergence, and the right one
for a property nothing has answered.  It was the WRONG one for a property answered
`#f' on purpose, because standing in there invents the obstacle the user asked to
remove.  That is skyline-removed.ly's whole subject (\tweak vertical-skylines ##f).
The fallback is now taken only when the value is neither a pair nor a boolean.


THE PURE READS IN side-position-interface's TAIL -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Side_position_interface::aligned_side reads `staff-padding' through
get_maybe_pure_property and every coordinate through maybe_pure_coordinate /
maybe_pure_extent -- SIX reads in the staff-padding tail (side-position-interface.cc:
406-453).  The port made all six ORDINARY.  The first read of the same property, 200
lines earlier, was already maybe-pure, which is trap 12a's shape at file scale: one
correct site is not evidence about its neighbours.

WHY IT COSTS A ROW.  On a spanner that is not broken yet, an ordinary read answers
about the WHOLE spanner and then CACHES on the original -- and Grob's copy constructor
hands that cache to every broken piece (grob.cc:111, ly_alist_copy).  So a property
whose value is an unpure-pure-container gets its unpure half called ONCE, on the
original, and each piece inherits the one answer.  `offsets.ly' carries
\once \offset staff-padding #'(1.0 2.0) TextSpanner across a \break; `offsetter'
distributes one value per broken sibling only when (>= total-found 2), and the port
was reaching that code with total-found = 0.

MEASURED on both engines with one probe file
(~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity22/offset-siblings.ly): the oracle prints
`same-as-orig=#f siblings=2' TWICE, once per broken piece; the port printed
`same-as-orig=#t siblings=0' ONCE, and now prints the oracle's two lines.


TWO DIAGNOSTICS THAT WERE NOT THE PORTED TEXT -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Volta_engraver's conflict message takes `ly_scm_write_string (volta-numbers)'
(volta-engraver.cc:175-177) -- volta-numbers is a LIST.  The port read it with a
SYMBOL reader and printed that reader's fallback, so the message said
"discarding conflicting volta numbers: ?".  `ly:expect-warning' could not match it,
which is why the file reported BOTH an unexpected warning and an unencountered
expected one.  Printer.Write is scm_write to a string port, which is what
ly_scm_write_string is.  Swept the family (trap 17c): Drum_notes_engraver's
"unrecognised percussion sign" takes ly_scm_write_string too, and had the same
symbol reader; the two agree on a symbol and diverge on everything else, and that
message exists precisely for the case where the value is not what was expected.

Tuplet_engraver's "No tuplet to end" is `ev->debug_output' upstream
(tuplet-engraver.cc:90), not `ev->warning'.  The port printed it as a warning on
every run.  Rule 15: severity is part of the translation.  EventDebugOutput is the
missing sibling of EventWarning, and it is the only ev->debug_output in a translator.


/!\ REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: THE TEXT INK EXTENT IS NOT THE RESIDUE'S CAUSE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Nine residue rows are a TEXT LABEL above the staff sitting a little lower in the port
than in the oracle, over material that is byte-identical on both sides -- and this
file's own KNOWN REMAINING note (see the text-advance section) says that when a
residue cluster points at text HEIGHT, the unrounded charstring CONTROL box is the
place to look.  It was looked at, and it is NOT the cause.

~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity22/text-ink-descender.ly measures the ink box of a
text stencil on either engine, through upstream's own ly:text-interface::print.  Over
strings with and without descenders the two agree to 2.4e-05 worst case -- the same
bound the note already recorded -- against deltas of 0.0139 to 0.9450.  The port's
CharstringRun already includes both off-curve control points in its bounds
(CharstringRun.cs:710-712), so it is a CONTROL box on both sides.  Do not re-derive
this.

WHAT IS MEASURED ABOUT THAT CLUSTER, for the session that takes it: the port's
outside-staff clearance for those labels comes out at -0.040, -0.007 and -0.005 --
BARELY negative, i.e. right at the boundary where avoid_outside_staff_collisions
decides not to move the grob at all.  The label extents are identical on both engines
to the last digit (probe 7, mensural-probe.ly), the polite-deferral machinery is
faithful, and AvoidOutsideStaffCollisions is faithful.  So the input that differs is
sub-0.04 and the OUTCOME is quantized, which is why the deltas jump to 0.46 (the
default outside-staff-padding) and 0.945.


/!\ font-name-add-files WAS BLOCKED BY A RULING -- CLOSED AT PARITY 23
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The file loads two font FILES it writes itself and asks for them by family name
(ly:font-config-add-font, ly:font-config-add-directory).  Both were accepted N/A entry
points under category `font-plumbing' (D13/D23), so the faces were never loaded and the
row could not MATCH while that stood.

RULED 2026-08-17 (R16): a document MAY supply its own fonts, because upstream implements
both entry points as fontconfig APPLICATION fonts and D23 forbids only the SYSTEM half.
Built at PARITY 23; the row MATCHes.  See "DOCUMENT-SUPPLIED FONTS" in the PARITY 23
section for what closing it actually took -- which was three Scheme-layer gaps as well as
the font work.


================================================================================
PARITY 23 -- THREE MECHANISMS, AND THE ROW THAT WAS BLOCKED BY A RULING
================================================================================


A HOLE IN EVERY TEXT SKYLINE WHEREVER THE TEXT HAD A SPACE -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

add_glyph_string_segments (stencil-integral.cc:565-650) divides a glyph's two metric
sources -- `xlen = real_bbox[X].length () / bbox[X].length ()' -- and adds the KERNED
BOX instead of tracing the outline when the quotient is not finite.  Its own comment
says which case that is: "The value will be nan for whitespace, in which case we just
want filler".  Whitespace divides 0 by 0 on either engine, because a space glyph's ink
box has zero length in both sources.

The port had dropped the branch, with a recorded reason: "the port has one source for
both -- the charstring interpreter -- so the ratio is the scale factor itself and the
fallback has no case to serve".  That is true of the ROUNDING case the note was
thinking of and false of the case upstream's comment actually names (trap 17i: a call's
stated purpose is not its only effect).  A glyph with no outline contributed NOTHING, so
a text run's skyline stopped at its last inked glyph and had a hole over every interior
space.

MEASURED on mensural-ligatures-invalid.ly: the label "BB " has a grob extent 3.824 wide
and its skyline reached 3.079.  Padded by outside-staff-horizontal-padding that is
21.639 in system coordinates, and the NoteColumn it has to clear starts at 21.900 -- so
the outside-staff pass could not see the obstacle and the label was never raised.  With
the filler the port computes a 0.945 push, which is the oracle's number exactly.

The port's text runs are per-glyph `glyph-outline' expressions rather than upstream's
one `glyph-string', so the glyph's own advance now rides IN the expression: the skyline
walker cannot derive it from the face, because a synthesised space's advance is
rewritten by the shaper and a kern belongs to the pair rather than the glyph.

SEVEN corpus rows: caesura-style-comma, key-clefs, mensural-ligature-citations-1 and -2,
mensural-ligatures-invalid, repeat-sign-1, metronome-marking-fractional.
Fenced by StencilIntegralTests (two cases, each with a control).


PANGO'S SYNTHETIC SMALL CAPITALS -- PORTED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

\fontCaps sets font-variant to small-caps (define-markup-commands.scm:4105) and the SVG
backend writes it straight into the <text> element, so the DRAWING was already identical
on both engines and only the MEASUREMENT differed.  The port carried the flag and never
used it.

It is a SYNTHESIS, not a font feature.  C059 has no `smcp' lookup in its GSUB at all --
which is why font-features.ly's explicit smcp line measures exactly like its plain
neighbour on both engines -- and the pinned oracle's \fontCaps still measures
differently from plain text.  Pango splits the item at each run of lowercase, uppercases
it, and sets that piece at the face's own x-height over its own cap height.  C059-Roman's
OS/2 says sxHeight 466 and sCapHeight 722 in a 1000-unit em: 0.6454293628808865.

MEASURED against the pinned oracle with ~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity23/, which runs
unchanged on both engines.  Every X now agrees to the last digit:

    \fontCaps normal                6.6579448818897635
    \bold \fontCaps normal          7.20423779527559
    \large \bold \fontCaps normal   8.057820472440945   (markup-commands' own 1.2292)
    \fontCaps Hello                 5.872648818897638
    \fontCaps Normal                7.340811023622047
    \fontCaps NORMAL                unchanged from plain
    \fontCaps 0123456789            unchanged from plain

Characters that are not lowercase are left entirely alone, and a size change ends the
run for BOTH kerning and ligatures, because Pango has split the item and never kerns or
ligates across two of them.

TWO corpus rows: markup-commands, font-features.
Fenced by SmallCapsSynthesisTests.


DOCUMENT-SUPPLIED FONTS -- RULING R16, BUILT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ly:font-config-add-font and ly:font-config-add-directory came OFF the accepted-N/A list
under D25's reversible-by-demand clause.  D23 is untouched and the distinction is the
ruling: upstream implements both as fontconfig APPLICATION fonts
(all-font-metrics.cc:306,319), the same set LilyPond's own bundled faces go into, and
font-config.cc builds that source separately from the system directories.  A document
that carries its font files beside it depends on the host for nothing.

The registry is keyed by the family name each FILE declares (SfntReader.ReadFamilyName,
typographic family ID 16 preferred over ID 1, Windows records before Macintosh) and is
consulted by TextFontChain.For BEFORE Normalize, so an exact family match wins as
fontconfig's best-match does.  A document font resolves to ONE face and not to the head
of a chain: the document named it, and putting a vendored fallback behind it would draw
a different typeface for any code point it happens not to cover.

THE THIRTEENTH PER-FILE LEAK, and it is registered in LilyPondInit.RestoreDefaults:
upstream builds one fontconfig configuration per process, and font-name-add-files.ly
DELETES its font files on the way out.

/!\ CLOSING THE ROW TOOK FOUR GAPS, NOT ONE, and three of them were in the Scheme layer
rather than the font layer.  The ruling sized the font work correctly and the file could
not reach it:

  * opendir / readdir / closedir / rmdir / delete-file were absent, so the file's
    clean-up block never ran -- and because ly:make-tmpfile-name is deterministic, the
    directory it failed to remove made the NEXT run fail at mkdir instead.
  * read-char and peek-char were absent, so the file's base64 decoder could not walk its
    own string port.
  * set-port-encoding! was a no-op.  Scheme writes bytes by setting an 8-bit codec and
    writing one character per octet; under a UTF-8 writer every octet above 0x7F came out
    as TWO bytes, so the font files the test wrote were the fonts' mojibake.  Nothing
    failed and nothing warned (trap 1b at the Scheme layer).

All three are in CodeBrix.LilyScheme and fenced there by BinaryPortTests; the font half
is fenced by DocumentFontTests, including the leak.

ONE corpus row: font-name-add-files.

/!\ STILL N/A, BUT NO LONGER "DELIBERATELY" -- RULED R18 THE SAME DAY, UNBUILT.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ly:font-config-display-fonts and ly:font-config-get-font-file were left N/A at PARITY
23 on the reasoning that both are asked about the HOST's fontconfig set, which is the
half D23 forbids.  Put to Jeremy at the PARITY 23 GO; he ruled (R18, 2026-08-17) that
they come OFF N/A and answer for the PORT'S OWN FONT WORLD instead:

  get-font-file   a VENDORED face  -> the assembly it ships in and its embedded-
                                      resource path within that assembly
                  a DOCUMENT font  -> its real on-disk path (R16's registry)
                  anything else    -> #f
  display-fonts   the 24 vendored faces plus whatever this document registered

/!\ THIS IS A RULED EXCEPTION TO RULE 2 AND THE ONLY ONE IN THE PROJECT.  Jeremy's
words: "this *is* a case where we do not need to match what upstream is doing exactly;
we are just doing something *similar*."  Upstream reports the host's fontconfig view
including its configuration directories; the port has no host font world to report, and
answering about its own is the point.  DO NOT later "correct" these two toward
upstream's output.

Both still THROW until R18 is built, and both owe TESTS -- Jeremy asked for them by
name.  Their fences cannot read the oracle (the port answers something upstream does
not); the authorities are the port's own asset manifest and R16's registry.  Full text
in CONDENSED_PLAN_3 sec 3b.


================================================================================
PARITY 24 -- THE FONT BUILD IS THE RESIDUE, AND THE DIAGNOSTICS FOUR CLOSED
================================================================================


THE PORT'S OWN EMMENTALER BUILD COSTS ELEVEN CORPUS ROWS -- MEASURED, NOT ARGUED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/!\ THIS IS THE SESSION'S MAIN FINDING AND IT IS NOT AN ENGINE DEFECT.  It is trap
27a -- an asset the port BUILDS is an input to the comparison, and a divergence in it
looks exactly like a defect -- at a size nobody had measured.

tools/font-build/README.txt sec 7 recorded the font build's divergence from the
official lilypond-2.27.2 release as "bounding boxes, on roughly 105-124 of 668 glyphs
per font ... median difference 1 font unit, maximum 3", and concluded: "A 1-3 unit
difference there is far below anything that could change a layout decision."

THAT CONCLUSION IS REFUTED.  Two things were wrong with it.  First, it compared
BOUNDING BOXES only; the outlines themselves differ STRUCTURALLY -- emmentaler-26's
`clefs.G' is 40 curve segments in the port's build and 47 in the release's, and where
the release writes

    C (335,-260) (147,-260) (2,-89) (2,112)
    C (253,552) (235,615) (218,678) (211,743)
    C (211,743) (205,794) (204,845) (204,897)

the port's build writes

    C (344,-260) (108,-260) (2,-46) (2,112)
    C (253,552) (226,645) (204,722) (204,897)

-- the same bbox, a different curve.  Second, a skyline is not a bbox: the difference
is invisible wherever the EXTREME point decides an answer and visible wherever the
profile's SHAPE does.

MEASURED BY A/B (trap 8a), by swapping the release's nine emmentaler .otf files in,
rebuilding, sweeping and grading, then restoring and verifying by hash:

    MATCH 2293 -> 2304.  ELEVEN rows go green:

      repeat-sign-global-size5, repeat-sign-global-size10   (cluster C)
      caesura-style-comma-over-bar-line, merge-rests-engraver
      ssaattbb-template-with-all-staves
      accidental-ancient, markup-with-true-dimensions       (R15's baseline pair)
      show-skylines, skyline-debug, skyline-embedded-ps,
      skyline-slur-segments                                 (four of D43's six)

    and skyline-boxes-ellipses improves GLYPHS-DIFFER -> PLACEMENT-DIFFERS.

    WHAT REMAINS is FOUR rows of real engine work -- collision-head-solfa-fa,
    markup-compound-meter-denominator-style, skyline-boxes-ellipses,
    skyline-grob-rotation -- plus the eight ruled-out skip-list rows.  With the
    release's fonts the port would stand at 2304 + 8 = 2312 of 2316.

/!\ ONLY THE .otf MATTERS, AND THAT SEPARATION IS MEASURED TOO.  Swapping the .otf
alone reproduced the release EXACTLY on every probe while the DRAWN outline stayed the
port's own (911 characters of path data against the release's 1030).  The skyline comes
from the CFF charstrings through GlyphOutlineSkyline, which is upstream's
FT_Load_Glyph/FT_Outline_Decompose path; the .svg font is the SVG backend's drawing
source and D29's named-glyph identity already tolerates it.

CLUSTER C, WHOSE MECHANISM THIS IS, IN FULL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

repeat-sign-global-size5/size10 each hold two \score blocks and only the second -- the
one carrying \layout { #(layout-set-staff-size 30) } -- moves, by a constant per
staff-to-staff GAP that accumulates down the score.

The gap is a ROD, not a spring: at that staff size the springs' basic-distance of 9 is
long past, and align-interface.cc:169's `dy = down_skyline.distance (skyline[-stacking_dir])
+ padding' decides it.  A probe set (~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity24) narrowed what
builds that rod by A/B on the INPUT, each variant running unchanged on both engines:

    two default staves at layout-set-staff-size 30      44.2061 both engines
    ... \omit TimeSignature                             44.2061 both engines
    ... \omit Clef                                      25.4686 both engines
    ... clef on the UPPER staff only                    34.4263 both engines
    ... clef on the LOWER staff only                    35.9143 both engines
    ... both clefs                          oracle 44.2061, port 44.1605

So EACH CLEF ALONE IS EXACT and only the two together lose 0.0457 units.  A single
clef against a flat staff skyline tests the glyph's EXTREME point, which the two builds
agree on; two clefs facing each other test the SHAPE of the profile between them, which
they do not.  25.4686 is the staff-only answer to four decimal places by hand --
12.2343 + 12.2343 + 1.0 padding -- which is what licensed reading the rest.

/!\ AND THE READING THE BOARD CARRIED INTO THIS SESSION HAD NO DISCRIMINATING POWER.
"A constant ABSOLUTE length, not a constant number of staff-spaces -- look for a
quantity converted with the WRONG staff-space" was derived from the error being equal in
the two files.  But the second \score is the SAME ABSOLUTE SIZE in both files; only the
paper scale differs.  So "constant in absolute terms across the two files" says only
that the error lives inside that score and does not depend on the paper -- which is true
of every possible cause, including this one.  A measurement that cannot come out the
other way is not evidence (trap 8a's shape, applied to an inference).

DISPOSITION -- RULED THE SAME DAY, R19 (Jeremy, 2026-08-17).  THE PORT BUILDS ITS
OWN EMMENTALER FONTS, FINAL, and the engine must be correct against the fonts the
port SHIPS.  His words: "if LilyPond is building their own .OTF files from Metafont
sources -- then that is what we are doing.  Final.  End of discussion.  So, we need
our stuff to work with *our own fonts* that we are shipping.  Final."

/!\ SHIPPING THE RELEASE'S BINARIES IS REFUSED.  Do not re-propose it, and do not
read the A/B above as an option -- it is a MEASUREMENT of what the two builds cost
against each other, and its only use is attribution.

WHAT FOLLOWS is G1's second clause, in the gate's own words: "files whose divergence
is in an ASSET the port builds or ships rather than in the engine match committed
PORT-GENERATED baselines."  The port is NOT WRONG on these rows -- it lays them out
correctly for the outlines it actually has.  R9's machinery is how the project says
so, and the owed work is a baseline extension from 8 rows to 13
(repeat-sign-global-size5, repeat-sign-global-size10,
caesura-style-comma-over-bar-line, merge-rests-engraver,
ssaattbb-template-with-all-staves), FROZEN FROM A FRESH SWEEP, each with a reasoned
block in the manifest header and -- R9's standing instruction -- EACH WITH ITS OWN
FENCE.  The correctness claim for these five is the skyline rod, and it is
hand-computable: the staff-only probe answers 12.2343 + 12.2343 + 1.0 padding =
25.4686 on BOTH engines to four decimals.

R20 -- RULED AND BUILT THE SAME DAY (Jeremy, 2026-08-17): RUN THE CORPUS AGAINST
BOTH FONT BUILDS.  LILYPOND'S ANSWER MUST BE EXACT; OURS IS MEASURED AND RECORDED.

Jeremy's design, and it is stronger than the per-mechanism fences it replaced.  A
hand-computed fence covers the consumer it was written for; ONE oracle-backed run
covers every consumer of a skyline, including the ones nobody has identified yet.

  tests/fixtures/lilypond-fonts/   the nine release emmentaler-*.otf, byte-for-byte,
                                   with SHA256SUMS.txt and a README.  /!\ NEVER
                                   SHIPPED -- PackagedFontTests opens the built
                                   .nupkg and asserts they are absent, and they live
                                   under tests/ so no asset glob can reach them.
  BatchDriver --fonts DIR          substitutes them with NO REBUILD, through
                                   FontAssets.SearchPaths (BatchRunner.UseFontsFrom).
  THE GATE                         with LilyPond's fonts the port grades 2304 MATCH
                                   + 8 ruled out = 2312 of 2316.  Any divergence
                                   there is the ENGINE and is a defect.
  THE LEDGER                       tools/regression-harness/font-delta.py and the
                                   committed font-delta-ledger.tsv: 145 rows, every
                                   number exact, ceiling 0.05 mm.  Worst row
                                   markup-with-true-dimensions at 0.044635 mm,
                                   median 0.004393 mm; 2172 of 2317 pages place
                                   every mark identically.

/!\ THE MEASUREMENT IS IN MILLIMETRES, CONVERTED PER PAGE.  Displacements come out
of the SVG in the paper's own staff-spaces, and a staff-space is 1.7573 mm at the
default 20 pt staff but 0.4393 mm at staff size 5.  The ranking really does change:
repeat-sign-global-size5 is the largest in staff-spaces (0.0914) and only the SECOND
largest in mm (0.040154, against markup-with-true-dimensions' 0.044635).

/!\ ANY CHANGE TO A RECORDED NUMBER IS A SIGNAL, INCLUDING A SMALLER ONE.  The font
build is byte-reproducible and the engine deterministic, so there is no noise to
absorb; --check fails on a changed value, a new row, a vanished row, and the ceiling,
and all four are mutation-proven rather than merely green.

/!\ THE FIXTURE DIRECTORY IS .otf ONLY, AND THAT IS MEASURED, NOT STYLISTIC.
Substituting the .svg fonts as well grades 121 of 2316 MATCH against 2304 with the
.otf alone: a music glyph is identified through a committed index that
generate-glyph-identity.py builds from THE PORT'S OWN fonts (D29), so LilyPond's
drawn outlines resolve against nothing and every page reads GLYPHS-DIFFER.  It would
gain nothing either -- D29 exists so that a difference in DRAWN bytes does not count.
CONSEQUENCE: the gate is exact at NAMED-GLYPH identity and cannot be strengthened to
byte-identity while the port draws from its own .svg fonts, which R19 says it keeps.

/!\ HEADROOM IS 11% -- 0.044635 mm against 0.050 mm.  The ceiling is a backstop; the
per-row record is the control.  A font rebuild on a different FontForge could breach
it and would then need a re-ruling, which is the same warning tools/font-build's
README now carries.

R9 AND R15 ARE DISCHARGED, AND baseline/svg IS DELETED (Jeremy, 2026-08-17).

The baseline held eight committed pages and claimed one thing: NO DRIFT since the
freeze.  What replaced it is strictly more:

  * SIX of the eight are now certified by the GATE, and certified BETTER -- the gate
    checks them against the ORACLE, where the baseline only ever checked them against
    the port's own earlier answer.  A baseline can only tell you the port still says
    what it used to say, including when what it used to say was wrong.
  * THE TWO THE GATE CANNOT CERTIFY -- skyline-boxes-ellipses and
    skyline-grob-rotation, which stay non-MATCH even under LilyPond's own fonts --
    carry a content hash in font-delta-ledger.tsv instead.  /!\ BOTH CARRIED OVER
    WITH THE IDENTICAL sha256, which is the evidence that the retirement lost
    nothing rather than the assertion that it did not.
  * TEN MORE PAGES GAINED COVER THEY NEVER HAD: the rule in the ledger is general --
    any page the gate does not certify records the hash of our own rendering -- so it
    also covers the two remaining engine rows and the eight ruled-out skip-list rows,
    none of which anything watched before.  It is self-maintaining: a page drops out
    the moment engine work makes the gate vouch for it.

/!\ THE CORRECTNESS CLAIM WAS NEVER THE BASELINE'S AND IS UNAFFECTED.  R9's own
standing note said so -- "the BASELINE claims no drift and nothing else... landing a
baseline without its fence is the failure mode" -- and the fence is
GlyphOutlineSkylineTests, hand-computed from upstream's own flattening expression
with measured controls.  It stays.

compare-output.py --baseline still exists and is still the right thing for any
port-against-port comparison, which is exactly what the font-parity run is; only the
committed DATA is gone.

/!\ skyline-boxes-ellipses and skyline-grob-rotation are NOT covered by that and
must not be swept into it: both stay non-MATCH even with the release's fonts, where
their four D43 siblings go green, so both carry a residue the font does not explain.
They are engine work.


THE ly:message SINK IS OPEN: THE LOG LEVEL DEFAULTED ONE STEP TOO LOW -- FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Warn.Level defaulted to LogLevel.LevelWarn.  Upstream's is LOGLEVEL_INFO
(flower/warn.cc:44, under the comment "Define the loglevel (default is INFO)"), and the
levels are cumulative bit masks, so the port was filtering out Info, Progress AND Basic
-- three whole severities, the entire ly:message / progress_indication / basic_progress
family.  Warn.Message was never unwired; it computed its text and threw it away
(trap 1b).  One line.

What the sweep log gained, and it is upstream's own text at upstream's own severities:
"Interpreting music...", "Preprocessing graphical objects...", "Finding the ideal number
of pages...", "Fitting music on 1 page...", "Drawing systems...", and the 65 files'
"MIDI output to `...'" that Performance.cs:138 had been computing and discarding.

The runner owes three more, because BatchRunner replaces upstream's own driver
(trap 17f) and a driver's messages are ported text, not the port's prose (rule 15):

  Processing `<file>'          lily-parser-scheme.cc:112, BASIC
  Parsing...                   lily-parser-scheme.cc:116, INFO
  Changing working directory   main.cc:735-756, INFO -- BatchDriver takes a per-file
    to: `<dir>'                scratch directory for the isolation the oracle harness
                               gets from a fresh temp directory per process, so it
                               reports it the same way

NOT owed: "Success: compilation successfully completed" is ly:exit's (scm/lily.scm:1014)
and is per PROCESS.  The oracle harness runs one process per file so its reference logs
carry it per file; a driver that engraves 2,146 files in one process would be inventing
content by repeating it.  Accepted under R17 and recorded here.


THE OUTPUT NAME MUST NOT CARRY A DIRECTORY -- FIXED (main.cc:729-761)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Upstream's main () splits --output into a directory and a file part, prepends the old
working directory to global_path, prints "Changing working directory to:", chdir's, and
reduces the output name to the BARE file part.  lily-parser-scheme.cc:40-42 states the
consequence as a contract: "Output name is treated simply as a file name because any
directory part should have been handled in main ()."

BatchRunner composed Path.Combine (outputDirectory, name) and handed the ENGINE the
result, so Performance::write_output -- which is faithful -- printed an ABSOLUTE path in
both of its messages.  One graded diagnostics row (skiptypesetting-all-true-midi, whose
"cannot create a zero-track MIDI file; skipping `%s'" is the oracle's bare name) and 65
files' worth of "MIDI output to".

/!\ NOT A HARNESS CONVENTION: generate-reference.sh passes -o "${work}/${name}",
absolute, exactly as BatchDriver does.  Upstream consumes the directory part itself.

The port now takes the output directory as the working directory for the span in which
output is WRITTEN and no longer, and writes bare names into it.  The engraving that came
before still runs in the driver's per-file scratch directory, which is what keeps a
file's SIDE files out of the output directory -- the oracle gets that isolation from a
fresh temp directory per process, and this is the port's equivalent.  Recorded as a
deliberate divergence in placement, not in the names the engine sees.

/!\ Lily.Shell owes the same split for its own -o (rule 14) -- EngraveCommand accepts a
path and does no split, so `lily.shell engrave x.ly -o out/dir/name' still does not
behave like lilypond's.  Sized: no corpus row, no gate; it is a shell-surface item.


A DIAGNOSTIC MUST START ITS OWN LINE -- FIXED (R17)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Scheme/lily/graphviz.scm writes a digraph whose last byte is `}' with no newline after
it.  Upstream can afford that: the process exits next, and the only thing that ever
follows is ly:exit's Success line, which nothing grades.  In a log carrying 2,146 files
the next thing was a WARNING, and `}warning: ...' is a line compare-diagnostics.py
cannot parse -- one graded row lost to a missing newline, and nothing to do with the
digraph's own bytes, which rule 2 puts out of scope.

Flower now has LineTrackingWriter: it remembers whether the last character it wrote was
a newline, Warn.Emit ends an open line before writing, and LilyPondScheme.CreateInterpreter
gives the interpreter's ErrorWriter the SAME instance so the two can see each other's
output.  R17 puts formatting at that boundary in scope and upstream's own bytes out of
it, which is exactly this shape.


A PURE PROPERTY READ THAT SKIPPED ITS CACHE -- FIXED (trap 1c)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Grob::pure_y_extent reads Y-extent through get_pure_property (grob.cc:548), and
internal_get_pure_property (grob-property.cc:226-241) has a branch the port did not:

    if (Unpure_pure_container *upc = unsmob<Unpure_pure_container> (val))
      {
        // Do cache, if the function ignores 'start' and 'end'
        if (upc->is_unchanging ())
          return internal_get_property (sym);      // <-- caches on the mutable alist
        else
          return call_pure_function (val, ...);
      }

The port's PureYExtent called CallPureFunction on the raw property data, so an
UNCHANGING container -- one declared with no pure half, whose answer therefore cannot
depend on start/end -- was re-evaluated on every pure enquiry instead of once.

The port's own Grob.GetPureProperty already had the branch; PureYExtent simply did not
go through it.  Trap 17c in miniature: one member of the family was right and its
sibling was not.

OBSERVABLE ONLY THROUGH A POISONED PROPERTY, which reports on every read: the port
raised SIX cyclic-dependency reports over debug-property-callbacks.ly where the oracle
raises FIVE.  The port's own backtrace named the site once the log level was upstream's
-- the extra report's innermost frame was VerticalAxisGroup.adjacent-pure-heights with
no NoteHead.Y-extent frame above it, where the oracle's every report has one.  Zero
corpus rows either way; the cache is part of the specification, not an optimisation.


THE NO-SYSTEM HALF OF align_elements_to_ideal_distances IS NO LONGER A STAND-IN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Upstream's whole else-branch is one line (align-interface.cc:302-308):

    else
      programming_error ("vertical alignment called before line breaking");

The port stacked the elements at their PURE minimum translations instead, recorded here
since PARITY 22, on the reasoning that the port's horizontal spacing reads early and a
refusal would burn every staff's one-shot offset at zero.

MEASURED: refusing regresses NOTHING.  Zero comparator regressions across a full graded
sweep, and the message fires exactly ONCE in 2,146 files -- arpeggio-no-staff-symbol,
which is the file whose diagnostics row this was.  The stand-in had cost that row twice
over: it suppressed upstream's own report (MISSING) and raised two cyclic-dependency
reports of its own (EXTRA), which is the BOTH verdict.

The reasoning was not absurd, and that is the lesson: a stand-in adopted because a
refusal "would" cost something is a hypothesis about the port, and it stayed unmeasured
for two sessions while it held a graded row open.


R18 BUILT -- THE PORT'S OWN FONT WORLD, AND THE ONLY RULED EXCEPTION TO RULE 2
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ly:font-config-get-font-file and ly:font-config-display-fonts came OFF the accepted-N/A
list and are implemented in FontPrimitives.InstallFontWorldQueries.

  get-font-file   a family the DOCUMENT registered (R16) -> its real on-disk path,
                                                            consulted FIRST, as
                                                            TextFontChain.For consults
                                                            the registry first
                  one of the 24 VENDORED faces           -> the assembly it ships in
                                                            and its embedded-resource
                                                            path within that assembly,
                                                            "<Assembly>.dll/<resource>"
                  anything else                          -> #f
  display-fonts   the 24 vendored faces plus whatever this document registered, to the
                  optional port, defaulting to (current-error-port) as upstream does

TWO DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES FROM UPSTREAM'S LETTER, both under the ruling:

  * a name that matches nothing answers #f where All_font_metrics::get_font_file
    answers the EMPTY STRING.  Jeremy's ruling says #f, and #f is the answer a Scheme
    caller can test: "" is TRUE in Scheme and would send a caller looking for a file
    called "".
  * the listing is the port's own font world rather than a fontconfig dump with
    directory information, which is the whole point of the ruling.

A vendored face answers a RESOURCE path because it has no other kind: the faces are
EmbeddedResource entries and FontAssets.Read reaches the disk only through a
normally-empty dev override, so there is no on-disk path to name and inventing one would
be a lie.  A family answers its REGULAR face, as fontconfig's best match does, and the
style order comes from TextFontChain's own family table rather than from reading a file
name -- the three collections spell "regular" three different ways (C059 "Roman", URW
"Regular", TeX Gyre lower-case).

Fenced by FontWorldQueryTests.  /!\ ITS EXPECTED VALUES CANNOT COME OFF THE ORACLE, by
construction; the authorities are the asset manifest and R16's registry, and every case
carries a control that comes out differently -- above all the #f arm, without which an
implementation answering the same thing for every name would pass everything else.

MEASURED BEFORE THE RULING and worth keeping: get-font-file has ZERO callers anywhere,
and display-fonts has ONE (lily.scm:1044, gated on show-available-fonts, which prints
and exits).  That is why a divergence is affordable here and nowhere else.


PARITY 26 -- THE TEXT SKYLINE'S METRIC SOURCE, AND WHY TWO CORPUS ROWS TURN ON IT
==============================================================================
This is the account of `skyline-boxes-ellipses' and `skyline-grob-rotation', the
last two rows the GATE does not certify.  It is recorded here rather than closed,
because the divergence is one the project has already RULED IN FAVOUR OF and the
two rows are its only visible consequence in 2,316 pages.

WHAT DIFFERS.  Read numerically off both engines with ly:skyline->points and
ly:skyline-max-height (the -ddebug-skylines DRAWING rounds to four decimals, and
four decimals is exactly the precision the question is about):

    quantity                 oracle                 port                delta
    max height (up)   -0.03300964259350392  -0.033001306825172244    8.3e-06
    max height (down)  1.6214403128075787    1.6214642086767959     -2.4e-05
    leftmost x         0.017605142716535432  0.017600696973425194    4.4e-06

THE ORACLE'S NUMBERS ARE PANGO'S.  -0.03300964259350393 is exactly the height
pair its own glyph-string expression carries: Pango's INK RECTANGLE, quantized to
whole Pango units and then scaled.  `add_glyph_string_segments' composes each
glyph's transform out of THREE differently-rounded boxes -- Pango's scaled ink
rect (get_scaled_indexed_char_dimensions), FreeType's unscaled metric box
(get_unscaled_indexed_char_dimensions), and their length ratio as `scale_factor'
-- and upstream's own comment on that chain reads "FIXME: this looks extremely
fishy."  The port has ONE source for both, its charstring interpreter, which
AddGlyphOutlineSegments already records as a deliberate divergence; its chain
cancels EXACTLY, verified in doubles for the measured numbers.

SO THE RESIDUE IS ~1e-5 STAFF SPACES = ~4.2e-05 mm -- 1,200 times below the font
ledger's accepted ceiling of 0.05 mm, and below what four decimals can print.
It becomes visible in exactly two places: where a coordinate lands on a rounding
boundary (12.3500 against 12.3501), and where a skyline merge tie-break lands on
exact equality and keeps or drops a DEGENERATE, zero-width building.  Only a
-ddebug-skylines page can see either, because only such a page draws the skyline
partition itself.  That is why two pages of 2,316 differ and no other does.

WHAT WAS REFUTED, so nobody re-runs it (probes and evidence in
~/ClaudeHome/lilyport-probe-parity26/):

  * THE MERGE.  A LITERAL Python transcription of lily/skyline.cc's whole build
    path, fed the port's own raw inputs, agrees with the port's C# TO THE LAST BIT
    of every left, right, slope and y-intercept, on all six skyline builds of a
    two-glyph markup.
  * THE SORT.  The same transcription gives the same answer under a stable sort
    and under a reproduction of libstdc++'s std::sort introsort.  (PARITY 25 had
    only shown that reversing tie order moves nothing, which is weaker.)
  * THE TRANSFORM CHAIN.  Upstream's four-step
    translate/translate/scale/translate cancels exactly in doubles for the
    measured numbers, so the extra steps are not where the difference enters.
  * THE ADVANCE.  1.2291590551181102 on both engines, to the last bit.
  * THE SINGLE-GLYPH OUTLINE.  Ten one-character markups render skyline drawings
    bit-identical to the oracle's, line for line.  The difference needs two.

/!\ CLOSING THE TWO ROWS MEANS MODELLING PANGO'S DEVICE-QUANTIZED GLYPH EXTENTS.
    There is no other route: the quantity that leaks is Pango's rounding residue
    and the port cannot compute it from its own sources.  D45 refused Pango's
    metrics as an answer in as many words.  So this is a DECISION, not a defect,
    and it is written up in the plan's section 3e.


D46 -- RULED (Jeremy, 2026-08-18).  THE DIVERGENCE STANDS; BOTH ROWS ARE RULED
OUT OF G1 RATHER THAN CLOSED.  G1 IS GREEN.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RULING.  The port keeps the real CFF outline as its single metric source for
a text glyph's skyline.  `skyline-boxes-ellipses' and `skyline-grob-rotation' go
onto g1-skip-list.tsv, which takes it from nine rows to ELEVEN, and G1 closes at
2,305 MATCH + 11 ruled out = 2,316 of 2,316.

WHY THIS WAY ROUND, IN THE TERMS THE PROJECT HAS ALREADY USED TWICE.  D31 draws
the line at what a USER CAN SEE: tofu exists because a reader who is missing a
Hebrew glyph should SEE that something is missing.  D45 drew the same line for
the music font and refused Pango's metrics as the answer, in as many words --
"we are moving away from Pango in LilyPort".  D46 is that line applied a third
time, and the quantity on the far side of it is 4.2e-05 mm:

    against the font ledger's own accepted ceiling (0.05 mm)   1,200x smaller
    against the worst row the port already SHIPS (0.044635 mm) ~1,060x smaller
    against one dot of a 1200 dpi printer                        ~500x smaller
    against one pixel of a 96 dpi screen                       ~6,300x smaller
    against the finest detail human vision resolves            ~2,400x smaller

  It is also below the SVG backend's own four-decimal print precision, so it
  cannot be represented in the output format, let alone seen in it.

/!\ WHAT THE SKIP-LISTING DOES NOT DO.  It changes NO code and NO rendering.
    Every score LilyPort engraves is byte-for-byte what it was before the ruling;
    what changed is what G1 CLAIMS about itself.  The rows still grade and still
    floor exactly as any other row does -- being ruled out of G1 is a statement
    about the GATE, never about the measurement (the skip list's own header says
    so, and it is still true of these two).

/!\ AND WHAT IT DOES ACCEPT, STATED HONESTLY.  The divergence is SYSTEMATIC in an
    INTERNAL quantity -- every text skyline in the port is bounded by the real
    outline where upstream uses Pango's quantized ink rect -- not something local
    to these two files.  They are simply the only two inputs in 2,316 that can
    EXPOSE it, because only a -ddebug-skylines page draws the skyline partition.
    Skylines feed vertical spacing, so in principle a comparison landing on exact
    equality could tip somewhere else.  The bound on that is EMPIRICAL, not
    proven: across the whole corpus it tips nothing, and no mark moves on either
    of the two rows themselves.  That is a weaker guarantee than a proof and it is
    recorded as such -- but it is the same class of guarantee, three orders of
    magnitude tighter, as the one R19/R20 already accept for the port's own
    Emmentaler build, which DOES move marks on 155 pages.

/!\ DO NOT "CORRECT" THIS LATER.  Modelling pango_font_get_glyph_extents's
    rounding would make it load-bearing in every text skyline for the life of the
    port, to move two debug pages that no reader can tell apart, and it would run
    against D45 and D46 both.  If it is ever re-opened, the trigger is a MEASURED
    consequence somewhere a user can see -- not the two skip-listed rows, which
    are the known and accepted cost.

WHERE THE RULING IS RECORDED IN CODE:
  * StencilIntegral.AddGlyphOutlineSegments -- the consumer, and it carries the
    full text of the ruling in its <remarks> plus a note on the tracing line.
  * TextFontMetric's GlyphOutlineSymbol -- the producer, with a pointer to it.
  * tools/regression-harness/g1-skip-list.tsv -- the two rows, with the reasoning
    and the revisit condition.

/!\ AND A TRAP FROM THE MEASUREMENT ITSELF.  The first dump recorded
    Height(left)/Height(right) instead of the raw (x1, y1, y2, x2).  A building
    rebuilt from its own recomputed heights is not the same building --
    y_intercept = y1 - slope*left, so Height(left) is y1 only up to rounding --
    and the comparison reported six false differences before that was found.
    Trap 32a in a brand-new instrument.


PARITY 26 -- main.cc's OUTPUT-NAME SPLIT IS NOW A NAMED FUNCTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
`BatchRunner.SplitOutputName' is upstream's main.cc:729-761: an existing DIRECTORY
is taken as one, and anything else is split, the FILE part becoming the output
base name (lily-parser-scheme.cc:37-60).  It lives in the engine rather than in
Lily.Shell so there is one implementation and the main suite fences it
(OutputNameSplitTests, 6 cases, each with a control; plus an end-to-end case in
BatchRunnerTests for the RunFile overload that consumes it).

  /!\ THE FILE PART KEEPS ITS EXTENSION.  File_name::file_part rejoins base and
      ext, and the --output arm of output_file_name_for_input_file_name is the one
      that does NOT clear ext_.  So `-o name.pdf' really does engrave to
      `name.pdf.svg'.  Stripping it would be an improvement, which is a parity bug.
  /!\ A DIRECTORY PART OF "." IS DROPPED, which is upstream's `dir != "."' guard
      and is what keeps `-o ./name' from being a different instruction from
      `-o name'.

  This discharges the Lily.Shell half of the output-path item PARITY 24 opened
  (rule 14): `lily.shell engrave x.ly -o out/dir/name' now behaves as
  `lilypond -o out/dir/name x.ly' does, where before it created a directory called
  `name'.


PARITY 26 -- TRAP 14'S LOCAL/RETURN-VALUE HALF, AUDITED
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SIZED: 340 boolean-read sites in the engine, 218 of them on property reads (swept
at PARITY 1 and PARITY 6), leaving 122 on locals and return values.

MECHANICALLY COMPLETE FOR ONE SUBCLASS: upstream reads exactly FIVE named
properties with Scheme truthiness -- centerBarNumbers, circled-tip, stencil,
suspendMelodyDecisions, tuplet-slur -- and the port spells all five
IsSchemeTrue/IsFalse.

BY HAND for the rest, roughly 50 sites across 20 files: LineSpanner's five
bound-details reads, ConstrainedBreaking's four (including the !scm_is_bool
special case), PageBreaking, OutputDef, Item::break_visible, AccidentalEngraver,
TupletBracket, MusicFunction, Clef_engraver's forceClef pair, VowelTransition,
TextInterface, PitchedTrillEngraver, DeprecatedProperty, Hairpin, MelodyEngraver,
BarEngravers.  EVERY ONE IS FAITHFUL.

  /!\ ONE LOOKED LIKE A HIT AND WAS NOT.  tuplet-bracket.cc:106 reads
      bracket-visibility with scm_is_true where the port uses exactly-#t -- but
      upstream only USES that value under `if (scm_is_bool (bracket_vis_prop))',
      where the two predicates agree, and both sites that matter (lines 134 and
      334) really are from_scm<bool>.  The port is right.  Read what a value is
      USED FOR before reading the predicate it was computed with.

The class stays open as a residual, now with a measured size and a named
remainder rather than an open worry.
